- [2.7.68] - 2026-07-05 — fix(command): /sc-status MCP-profile + hook-profile were guessed, not read. The output template had "MCP profile" and "Hook profile" lines but the files-to-read list never named their sources, so two concurrent sessions disagreed — one showed "light"/"standard", the other "—"/"—". Added `.mcp-profile` (→ MCP profile) and `.profile` (→ hook profile; absent → standard) to the list. Validated by running the (now-fixed) command in the OTHER concurrent session: session cost correctly showed radius-bundles' own $1991.65 (not this session's), lessons=30 (its own), last-compact its per-session flag — the v2.7.64/.65/.66 cross-session fixes all hold. Also confirmed NOT a bug: that session's "Memory 68 days ago" is honest — session-memory-write has a differential-write gate (only rewrites when git open-files/commits/decisions change), so a stable-git project keeps an old memory file by design.

- [2.7.67] - 2026-07-05 — fix(command): drop the /sc-status "Cost (lifetime)" line. The global .session-cost accumulator it read is provably over-counting — two /sc-status runs 20 min apart showed lifetime jumping $64444 → $73160 (+$8715) and turns 226277 → 256226 (+29949), i.e. ~30k turns in 20 minutes, physically impossible. Rather than patch the accumulator (the 8th /sc-status-adjacent defect), the misleading line is removed; session cost stays (transcript ground-truth, accurate). Dropped the now-unused .session-cost / .main-tokens reads from the command too. All remaining fields verified correct this round (session cost, subagents deduped to 50, recent blocks, tools/confidence, last-compact). The accumulator itself remains for the budget cap (per-session path is sound) but is no longer surfaced as a lifetime total; a proper ledger is the real fix if lifetime reporting is wanted.

- [2.7.66] - 2026-07-05 — fix(command): two more /sc-status inaccuracies (4th round from the user reading real output). (1) Subagents "135 runs" over-reported — SubagentStop writes several entries per agent, so the raw entry count (135) is ~2.7x the real run count (49 unique agent_ids). Now dedups by agent_id (keeps max cost per agent, like the statusline) before counting/aggregating; the cost total was coincidentally right (dups carried equal cost) but the run count was inflated. (2) "Recent blocks" was ALWAYS "—" — the command read a nonexistent "learn-from-blocks log" when the data lives in scope/.blocked-commands (505 lines, format [ts] category — reason — command). Now reads that file's last 3 lines. NOTE: this is the ~7th distinct /sc-status bug across 4 rounds — the command is a hand-rolled aggregation over ~12 undocumented scope-file formats and keeps being subtly wrong; the durable fix is to simplify it toward authoritative sources (CC's own per-session total_cost_usd, fewer fields), not to keep patching fields one at a time.

- [2.7.65] - 2026-07-05 — fix(command): /sc-status session cost was 100x under-reported for upgrade-spanning sessions. It read `.main-tokens-$SID.cost_usd`, but that per-session accumulator only started counting when v2.7.63 deployed — so a session running across the upgrade showed only the cost since the upgrade ($22.83) instead of the full session ($2343.54, ground-truthed by re-summing the transcript: opus, cache_read 3397.8M × $0.50 dominates). Fixed: /sc-status now COMPUTES session cost directly from the session transcript (~/.claude/projects/<enc-cwd>/<sid>.jsonl, per-message pricing) — ground truth, immune to when the accumulator started. A one-shot status command can afford the ~0.5s parse. The budget cap still uses the accumulator (accurate for sessions that start after v2.7.63; the cap is a soft warn/block). Third /sc-status fix from the user reading real output — none of these were reachable by the test suite (cross-session, upgrade-transition, real-transcript-scale).

- [2.7.64] - 2026-07-05 — fix(command): /sc-status showed the WRONG session under concurrency. It identified "this session" by the globally-newest per-session file, which — with two sessions running — grabbed another project's session: session cost read $1.16 from a shopify-radius-bundles session while the actual claude-supercharger conversation had spent $17.31 (~10x under), and "Last compact" read the DEPRECATED global .memory-restored (dead since v2.7.47's per-session split) showing "~3 days ago" when the session had never compacted. Fixed: STEP 0 now DERIVES the current session id from the cwd's own CC transcript dir (encode cwd → newest <sid>.jsonl in ~/.claude/projects/<enc>/), and every per-session field (.main-tokens/.tool-history/.memory-restored/.repetition-flag) keys off that $SID. Scopes correctly to the current project's session; the bare .memory-restored global is explicitly retired. Surfaced by the user running /sc-status with two concurrent sessions.

- [2.7.63] - 2026-07-05 — fix(budget): the budget cap was non-functional — it compared a per-project `budget` against the machine-GLOBAL lifetime cost. `.session-cost` is one global file that accumulates total_usd across ALL sessions/projects forever and never resets (a user's /sc-status showed $64320.42 over 225906 turns since Jun 23). budget-cap check read that global total, so `"budget": 5.00` compared $5 against $64320 → 1286408% → every non-read tool blocked instantly, and the "start a new session" advice didn't reset anything. Now cost is tracked PER-SESSION (keyed by session_id in .main-tokens-<sid>.cost_usd, exactly like tokens already were) and the cap compares against THIS session's spend. A fresh session starts at $0 and "start a new session" actually resets it — matching the README/message wording. Verified: fresh session passes despite a $64k global; per-session spend blocks/warns/passes at the right thresholds. The global .session-cost stays as an accurately-labeled lifetime total (compaction-backup relabeled in v2.7.61; /sc-status now shows session + lifetime separately). Root cause: cost was the one telemetry stream never keyed by session — tokens (.main-tokens-<sid>) and subagent costs (.subagent-costs-<sid>) always were. 5 budget-cap check tests updated + a fresh-session regression test.

- [2.7.62] - 2026-07-02 — fix(security): config-scan false-positived on legitimate project agent/command files. A user's real session flagged `.claude/agents/ai-engineer.md` as "Potential prompt injection" at SessionStart — a scary warning on a file they authored. Root cause: three over-broad patterns matched normal AI prose — bare `system prompt` ("consider the system prompt design"), bare `you are now` ("you are now the reviewer"), and `act as (a) new` ("act as a new pair of eyes"). These are ADVISORY warnings, not blocks, so precision beats recall (a missed advisory is not a bypass — prompt-injection-scanner on tool output + memory-write-guard are the real defenses). Each now requires an adversarial context: system-prompt attacks need an exfil/override verb or a redefinition (`reveal/override/ignore ... system prompt`, `system prompt:`); persona hijack needs a malicious persona (`you are now DAN/uncensored/...`); role hijack needs an AI/persona object (`act as an uncensored AI`). Verified: legit agent prose is clean, all real injection (ignore-instructions, DAN, reveal-system-prompt, im_start token, uncensored-AI) still flags — incl. inside an agent file. 2 regression tests.

- [2.7.61] - 2026-07-02 — fix(hooks): post-compact-inject was silently broken for ~30 releases — CONFIRMED against the live CC binary (user hit it on a real /compact). It emitted `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PostCompact","additionalContext":...}}` (v2.7.30), but CC's schema allows hookSpecificOutput ONLY for PreToolUse/UserPromptSubmit/PostToolUse/PostToolBatch/Stop/SubagentStop — PostCompact is not in the list, so the output failed validation ("(root): Invalid input") and the restored post-compaction context NEVER reached Claude. This is exactly the item the v2.7.55 audit flagged as doc-ambiguous and deliberately left unchanged (the doc was inconsistent; refused to blind-change) — now the binary settled it. Fixed: emit RAW plain text (the channel the sibling precompact-priorities.sh uses successfully on the compact lifecycle), which the binary accepts. Meta-guard added: no PreCompact/PostCompact hook may emit hookSpecificOutput (would have caught this) + a raw-text shape assertion. Also fixed compaction-backup's misleading "Session cost so far: \$53609.92328979" — that is the machine-GLOBAL lifetime accumulator, not one session; now labelled "Total cost so far (all sessions)" and rounded to cents. Noted but NOT changed: config-scan flags project .claude/agents/*.md as possible prompt injection — likely a false positive (agent definition files legitimately contain imperative instructions); needs a heuristic exception, deferred.

- [2.7.60] - 2026-07-02 — feat(test): hook LIVENESS audit + harness — guard the "silently dead decision hook" class. Swept every BLOCK/DECIDE hook with a TRIGGERING payload and asserted it actually fires (exit 2 or a valid deny/decline/allow/block decision). Result: all 17 decision hooks are ALIVE (the pattern that made smart-approve a weeks-long no-op, v2.7.29, would now be caught automatically). The sweep also surfaced one real coverage gap: output-secrets-scanner caught the AWS access-key ID (AKIA…) but NOT the AWS SECRET access-key VALUE (AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=wJalr…) — the more dangerous, prefix-less half, which an env dump exposing only the secret would leak unscanned. Added a high-signal pattern (label + exact 40-char value; verified no FP on AWS_REGION). New permanent test-hook-liveness.sh (17 assertions) turns luck-based audit finds into an automated gate; +2 secret-scanner tests. This is the direct fix for the session's recurring theme — features that break silently across a big surface.

- [2.7.59] - 2026-07-02 — fix(hooks): deep payload-drift audit vs captured real CC payloads — 2 cwd-drift regressions + a class guard. Audited every hook's payload field reads against the actual captured CC payloads (subagent/elicitation/etc.): the subagent + elicitation field reads are correct (v2.7.11/.12 hold; elicitation-guard's server_name/schema/message match), and no hook reads the nonexistent tool_response.exit_code. BUT two hooks — bash-output-compactor + repetition-detector — read `.cwd // ""` WITHOUT the `.workspace.current_dir` fallback: the perf-sweep @tsv consolidation (v2.7.42/.44) had dropped the dual-field pattern that v2.6.57 added to all 43 hooks, so on CC 2.1.176+ (which sometimes sends the CWD only in workspace.current_dir) they fell back to the unreliable hook-process $PWD → wrong project-scoped debug/profile/suppress detection. Both restored to `.cwd // .workspace.current_dir // ""`. NEW meta-test (test-hook-payload-contract.sh) guards the whole class going forward: asserts every .cwd field read carries the fallback AND no hook reads tool_response.exit_code — proven non-vacuous (a planted bad read fails it). Turns a recurring manual-audit finding into an automated gate.

- [2.7.58] - 2026-07-02 — perf(hooks): tool-history-tracker — one fork on every PostToolUse instead of two. It ran a jq (extract session_id) AND a python3 (build the entry) on EVERY PostToolUse — the highest-frequency hook event, and the one frequent hook that CAN'T be pre-gated (it must record every call for the confidence-gate). But python already parsed the payload and returned session_id, so the jq was pure redundancy. Folded: python now prints the sanitized session id (line 1) + entry JSON (line 2), read fork-free in bash via parameter expansion. ~15ms saved per tool call — adds up over a tool-dense session. Verified: the same failure post-scan (v2.7.42/.44) already pre-gates failure-tracker/dep-vuln-scanner, and no other frequent hook has an ungated redundant fork. All 6 tool-history tests pass unchanged (behavior identical: per-session isolation, failure inference, 20-entry trim).

- [2.7.57] - 2026-07-02 — perf(hooks): kill the turn-end transcript-slurp cost — data-driven from real profiling data collected this session. lesson-record (Stop) and notify-stop (Stop) both extracted the last user + last assistant message with `jq -rs`, which SLURPS the entire transcript. On a long session the transcript grows large (measured: 47MB / 29k lines), so this cost ~550ms (lesson-record, 1 slurp) and ~1.1s (notify-stop, TWO slurps) EVERY turn-end — lesson-record was the #1 hook in the profiling data at 737ms avg. The last pair is always in the tail, so both now `tail -n 400 | jq -rs` (notify-stop's two slurps also consolidated to one pass). Benchmarked 550ms → ~10ms (55×), byte-identical output. Regression test: extracts the correct last pair from a 1200-line transcript full of prior noise. ~1.6s of per-turn-end CPU removed on long sessions, ~550ms of it off the user-facing turn-completion path.

- [2.7.56] - 2026-07-02 — feat(agents): tier subagent models by task after an optimization audit. The 9 agents pinned 8 to Sonnet-4.6 with Reviewer alone on Opus-4.8 — an inconsistent split (why is review worth Opus but architecture / root-cause debugging not?) that predated Opus 4.8 becoming the session default, so delegating to reasoning agents ran them BELOW the main loop. Now tiered by the deliverable: read-only REASONING agents where reasoning quality IS the output — architect, debugger, planner, researcher — join reviewer on Opus-4.8; EXECUTION/throughput agents — code-helper, writer, data-analyst, general — stay on Sonnet-4.6 (fast + cheaper, keeps the subagent bill down). Tool scoping audited alongside and confirmed correct (reasoning agents are read-only; only code-helper/writer/data-analyst/general can mutate). test-agents.sh model assertions rewritten to the tier principle (5 opus + 4 sonnet). User-chosen strategy.

- [2.7.55] - 2026-07-02 — fix(hooks): deep contract re-audit vs the current CC hook matrix — one real drop-bug fixed. git-safety.sh's --force-strip REWRITE path (reachable: `git push --force` on a non-protected branch → strip --force) emitted `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"updatedInput":...}}` WITHOUT hookEventName. A hookSpecificOutput missing hookEventName is dropped by CC (verified class, v2.7.30) — so the rewrite was silently discarded and the original --force push ran unmodified. Now emits hookEventName:"PreToolUse", matching the hook's own deny/fallback paths. Regression test asserts the rewrite carries hookEventName + updatedInput and no longer contains --force. Full enumeration otherwise CLEAN: all 18 PreToolUse gates use permissionDecision; PermissionRequest→decision.behavior; Elicitation→action; Stop→decision:block; every PostToolUse rewrite uses updatedToolOutput; git-safety was the sole hookSpecificOutput emit missing hookEventName (reentry-detector's match was a detection regex, not an emit). Doc-ambiguous items (PostCompact additionalContext, SubagentStart blockability, PermissionDenied→retry) were reasoned through in v2.7.30/.31 and left as-is — they depend on binary behavior the docs describe inconsistently and can't be resolved from docs alone.

- [2.7.54] - 2026-07-02 — fix(security): close a skill-poisoning-scanner bypass for NAMESPACED plugin skills. The scanner globbed for the raw invoked skill name to find its file on disk, but plugin skills are invoked as "plugin:skill" while the file is named by the bare skill — so the glob matched nothing, the file was never read, and the skill loaded UNSCANNED (a full bypass for any plugin skill: reproduced — bare 'mskill' → exit 2 blocked, 'evilplugin:mskill' → exit 0 allowed). Now searches the bare name (after the last ':' or '/') in addition to the raw value, and dedups matches. Regression test added; clean namespaced skills still pass (no false positive). Audited alongside mcp-provenance.sh, which was found solid (NFKC-normalized, specific structural patterns) — no change.

- [2.7.53] - 2026-07-02 — feat(security): /trust-mcp command + tool to allowlist an Elicitation server without hand-editing config. The elicitation-guard's trust list lived only in .supercharger.json, which is itself protected by the self-modification path-guard — so there was no in-session way to whitelist a server. New tools/trust-mcp.sh manages a global scope allowlist (scope/.trusted-elicitation-servers, one lowercased name/line) that the guard reads IN ADDITION to .supercharger.json; /trust-mcp <server> | --list | --remove <server> drives it. Server names normalized (lowercased, sanitized). 4 tests: add/list/remove + guard honors scope-file trust. README + command help carry a security note (trusting a server disables the guard for it).

- [2.7.52] - 2026-07-02 — feat(security): elicitation-guard now also catches credential-request PHRASING in the prompt text, not just field names. A server can use an innocuous field name ("value") but ask for a token in prose ("paste your GitHub token here") — the v2.7.49 field-name check missed that. Added a conservative message heuristic (action verb + possessive + credential noun within a short span) so real phishing prose is declined while benign prompts ("enter the project name", "enter the API endpoint URL", "type your name") pass. Same trusted-server bypass and decline path. 3 new tests incl. two false-positive traps; audit records now carry msg_trigger.

- [2.7.51] - 2026-07-02 — fix(budget-cap): bound the .session-cost flock so a stuck holder can't hang the session. budget-cap runs on every PostToolUse and took `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX)` with NO timeout — the kernel releases flock when a holder DIES, but not when it's merely wedged, so an alive-but-stuck holder would hang every subsequent tool call forever. Now acquires non-blocking with a ~2s budget, then proceeds best-effort without the lock (a rare lost cost delta under real contention beats a permanent hang; uncontended acquire still succeeds on the first try). Regression test: a 6s-held lock no longer hangs the hook (returns in ~2s and still writes state). Also fixes the local test-suite hang where an interrupted run left orphaned budget-cap writers deadlocked on the lock.

- [2.7.50] - 2026-07-02 — feat(security): make the elicitation-guard block VISIBLE. Elicitation events carry no systemMessage/additionalContext, so a v2.7.49 decline was silent in-session — the credential form just vanished. The guard now fires a desktop notification ("Blocked credential request from <server>") on decline, in the BACKGROUND (never delays the block) with stdout/stderr isolated so it can't corrupt the decline JSON, respecting the .no-desktop-notify off-switch and 10s cooldown. Tests run under a suppressed test HOME so no real notifications fire; added a suppression test. Verified: decline JSON stays pure, hook returns in ~50ms.

- [2.7.49] - 2026-07-02 — feat(security): Elicitation credential guard — ships the blocking guard the elicitation-audit hook had only teased. MCP servers can solicit input via an `Elicitation` form; a malicious/compromised one uses it to phish an "API token" or "database password" in a routine-looking dialog. New synchronous `elicitation-guard.sh` DECLINES an elicitation (`{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"Elicitation","action":"decline"}}`) when its schema contains credential-style field names (password/passwd/secret/token/api_key/apikey/bearer/private_key/access_key/client_secret, plus delimited key/pat/pin/otp/mfa/auth/creds/pwd, camelCase-normalized) AND the server is not in `.supercharger.json` `trustedElicitationServers`. Field-NAME based (values never inspected); benign forms (username/email) and false-positive traps (monkey, patch_notes) pass through. Kill switch `SUPERCHARGER_ELICITATION_GUARD=0`; declines audited to `audit/elicitation-guard.jsonl`. The async elicitation-discovery.sh keeps logging every request's shape. 7 new tests. Contract verified against current CC docs: Elicitation supports action accept/decline/cancel, no additionalContext/systemMessage.

- [2.7.48] - 2026-07-02 — perf(agent-router): trim per-prompt forks on the UserPromptSubmit path. Consolidated three separate `jq` reads of the input payload into one `@tsv` extraction for the two single-line fields (session_id + project_dir), split with fork-free bash parameter expansion; the multi-line prompt is still read in its own `jq` so a newline can't corrupt the split. Replaced three `cat` scope-file reads with a `<`-redirect and the `read` builtin (zero forks). Net ~3 fewer process spawns per prompt. All 9 agent-router routing tests unchanged; verified a multi-line prompt with an embedded 'error at line 42' still routes to the Detective and extracts project_dir correctly.

- [2.7.47] - 2026-07-02 — fix(statusline+budget): two cross-session leaks. (1) "Mem: Restored" badge used a GLOBAL `.memory-restored` flag, so one session compacting lit the badge in EVERY concurrent session for 5 min (and mis-fired economy-reinforce there too) — now keyed by session_id across writer (post-compact-inject), statusline reader, and economy-reinforce (+ its ack flag). (2) budget-cap's transcript resume-offset was a single GLOBAL int while each session reads its OWN transcript_path — concurrent sessions ping-ponged it (small transcript rewinds, large one re-reads an already-counted region), over-counting both tokens and cost. Offset is now a PER-TRANSCRIPT map with legacy migration + an offset==0 re-derive guard. Regression tests: cross-transcript independence, legacy migration, cross-session badge non-leak. No single-session behavior change (verified: this session's 7.16M content-token count matches a ground-truth transcript re-count exactly).

- [2.7.46] - 2026-07-02 — fix(test): repair CI on Linux after v2.7.45. The v2.7.45 always-on timing broke ONE existing test on ubuntu-latest (bash 5): "HOOK_START_MS is 0 when no sentinel" — true on bash 3.2 (auto-timing off) but not bash 5 (auto-timing on → populated). CI runs on ubuntu AND macos; I verified only macos locally and missed it (the always-on threshold tests themselves passed on bash 5 — only this stale assertion failed). Made the assertion bash-version-aware. No hook/behavior change; v2.7.45's deployed hooks were always correct — only the test was stale. Lesson: for anything gated on bash version / EPOCHREALTIME, check BOTH CI OSes before shipping.

- [2.7.45] - 2026-07-01 — feat(perf): always-on slow-hook timing so `/perf` works out of the box (leverages the perf audit). Hook timing used to record only while a `.profiling` sentinel was set, so `/perf` was empty by default. Now, on **bash 5+** (where `EPOCHREALTIME` gives a zero-fork millisecond clock), lib-suppress/lib-timing record any hook fire slower than a threshold (default 40ms, `SUPERCHARGER_PERF_THRESHOLD_MS`) automatically — overhead is two clock reads + a compare with no I/O on the fast common case, so it surfaces exactly the hooks worth optimizing (and catches regressions) with no setup. On **bash 3.2** (macOS default — no cheap ms clock) auto-timing stays OFF to avoid adding a python fork per hook; those users enable full `.profiling` explicitly (unchanged, and `/perf` now guides this per bash version). Full profiling (`.profiling`) still logs every fire on any bash. 3 tests. No behavior change to hook logic; zero overhead added on bash 3.2.

- [2.7.44] - 2026-07-01 — perf(hooks): repetition-detector (hottest hook — every Bash AND Read) split its jq @tsv fields with one bash `read` instead of 4 separate `awk` forks. Loop/re-read detection verified unchanged. No behavior change.

- [2.7.43] - 2026-07-01 — perf(hooks): more pre-gates on the PostToolUse chain (round 2). **failure-tracker** (fires every Bash, forked 3 jq): most commands succeed, so a single cheap grep over the raw payload for its own failure markers / `interrupted:true` now short-circuits before any jq — 37→~10ms on the common success path; the precise per-field check still runs for actual failures, so nothing is mis-recorded (verified: `command not found` and interrupted still record). **slow-tool-detector** (every tool): the hook needs `duration_ms`; a presence pre-gate skips the jq+python when the payload doesn't carry it. **session-memory-write** (Stop, every turn): collapsed two `git rev-parse` forks (branch + toplevel) into one. Behavior unchanged; existing suites green. Still deferred: budget-cap's jq (telemetry-critical, low ROI), repetition-detector internals, the UserPromptSubmit chain.

- [2.7.42] - 2026-07-01 — perf(hooks): cheap bash pre-gates + statusline git cache (from the performance audit). The audit measured ~420ms of blocking-hook latency on EVERY Bash tool call, dominated by hooks that fork python3/jq to parse the payload before discovering they don't apply. Added raw-string pre-gates that exit BEFORE any interpreter fork when a hook obviously can't act: **commit-check** (68→~20ms — only acts on `git commit`), **dep-vuln-scanner** (65→~20ms — only after installs), **human-approval-gate** (149→~10ms — a single grep for its high-risk trigger keywords replaces a python cwd-parse + 5-level parent-dir walk + config reads that ran on every Bash). Net ≈230ms shaved off every Bash call, with behavior unchanged (each gate still fires on its real triggers — verified: git commit, terraform destroy, drop database still act). **statusline** (hottest path — renders on CC's refresh timer): the branch + uncommitted-diff (2 git forks) are now cached per-repo, keyed on `.git/HEAD`/`index` mtime + a ~3s time bucket, so rapid re-renders reuse instead of re-forking git (biggest benefit on large-WIP repos where `git diff` is 50-150ms); staleness bounded ~3s, branch always fresh. `.statusline-git-*` TTL-cleaned. Deferred (bigger/lower-ROI): the shared parse-cache architecture, session-memory-write's git forks, budget-cap's jq. No behavior change; no new tests needed (existing suites green).

- [2.7.41] - 2026-07-01 — fix(security): close 7 verified gate bypasses found by a red-team audit. A parallel security/perf/portability audit red-teamed the blocking gates; every finding below was reproduced (piped payload → observed exit 0 = bypass) and re-verified after the fix. **(1) safety.sh — `rm -rf` on absolute system/home paths** (`rm -rf /etc`, `/usr/local`, `/Users/x/.ssh`) slipped through — only cwd-ancestors and a small literal target set were caught. Added an absolute-target class: system roots (/etc,/usr,/bin,/sbin,/lib,/boot,/sys,/dev,/root,/System,/Library,/private/etc), home + another user's home + sensitive home dotdirs (.ssh,.aws,.gnupg,...). Deliberately NOT /var (macOS temp lives there). **(2) safety.sh — `find . -delete` / `find -exec rm`** were entirely unguarded → added. **(3) git-safety.sh — `git push origin +main`** (leading-`+` refspec = native force-push, no `--force` flag) bypassed the force check → now detected, incl. `+HEAD:master`. **(4) path-guard.sh — a RELATIVE path through an in-repo symlink** (`escape -> /etc`, write `escape/x`) escaped the project; only absolute paths were realpath-checked → relative paths now blocked when they resolve outside the root. **(5) mcp-sql-guard.sh — newline / `/**/` comment between verb and object** (`DROP\nTABLE`) bypassed the per-line grep → query is normalized (strip SQL comments, collapse whitespace) before matching. **(6) mcp-sql-guard.sh — only 4 SQL MCP servers matched** → broadened to 21 (neon, mssql, bigquery, snowflake, clickhouse, cockroach, ...); kept an allowlist so non-SQL tools with a `.query` param don't false-positive. **(7) mcp-github-write-gate.sh — write with OMITTED branch** defaults to the repo's default branch (usually main) and skipped the protected-branch check → now blocked. All fixes verified to block the exploit AND still allow legit usage (rm -rf /tmp/build, ./node_modules, normal push to main, in-project relative writes). Portability audit: clean (no BSD/GNU breaks). Performance audit findings deferred to a follow-up. 15 red-team regression tests added.

- [2.7.40] - 2026-07-01 — fix(hooks): finish the audit's channel cluster — route Claude-actionable feedback to `additionalContext` instead of `systemMessage`. `quality-gate` (lint issues to fix) and `repetition-detector` (loop/re-read advice) emitted `{"systemMessage"}`, which only reaches the USER — so Claude never saw the feedback it was supposed to act on. Both now use `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` (PostToolUse). `stop-failure` emitted `stopReason` (only applies with `continue:false`, so its API-error advice was dropped) → now `additionalContext` (StopFailure is context-only). `config-scan` (prompt-injection / rogue-hook alert at SessionStart) now emits **both** `systemMessage` (alert the user) AND `additionalContext` (warn Claude to stay skeptical of a hostile project config) — a security alert should reach both. `rate-limit-advisor` was already correctly dual-channel (left as-is). 1 test updated. This closes the hook-vs-doc contract audit's remaining channel items. Note: the tail-cap log-trim race (flagged earlier) was investigated and intentionally NOT fixed — the only correct fix adds a lock (python fork) to every log write to avoid a rare single-line loss, a net-negative trade.

- [2.7.39] - 2026-07-01 — change(statusline): token counts now show CONTENT tokens (input + output), excluding ALL cache. Previously the token basis was input + cache_write + output (excluding only cache_read). That made the parent figure look cache-inflated — a real session's breakdown was input 0.29M · cache_write 51.2M · cache_read 2546M · output 6.1M, so the old "new tokens" basis reported 57.6M (89% cache_write). Switched both main (budget-cap) and sub (statusline) to input + output only — the intuitive "conversation content" number (that session: main 57.6M → 6.5M, sub 3.1M → 770K). cache_read was already excluded; now cache_write is too. Cost figures are unchanged (still CC's real numbers). Legacy subagent rows without the input/output breakdown fall back to total_tokens. Tests updated to the content basis. 1171 passing.

- [2.7.38] - 2026-07-01 — style(statusline): reorganize line 2/3 grouping. Line 2 is now `Context | Time | cache`; the Session/Weekly rate-limit gauges move to line 3, after the token/cost block (`Tokens: … total 60.8M/$119.07 equiv | Session: 39% · Weekly: 40%`). Groups "conversation size + elapsed" on line 2 and "usage accounting + limits" on line 3. Display-only. 1171 passing.

- [2.7.37] - 2026-07-01 — feat(statusline): unified tokens+cost line + rebalanced layout. Merged the split/duplicated token & cost info (sub tokens had been on both line 2 and line 3) into one coherent line 3 showing Claude + subagents together, each with BOTH tokens and cost, plus the combined total: `Tokens: main 480K/$91.91 · sub 3.1M/$27.16 · total 3.6M/$119.07 equiv`. Rebalanced the layout: line 2 now carries the "where am I" info (context, cache, **Time**, Session/Weekly rate limits — Time moved down from line 3), leaving line 3 dedicated to token/cost accounting. `equiv` (plan-equivalent API $, since subscription users aren't charged per-token) now shows **once**, on the total, instead of trailing the main figure. When no subagents ran, line 3 collapses to `Tokens: 480K/$91.91 equiv`. Tests updated to the new format. 1171 passing.

- [2.7.36] - 2026-07-01 — feat(statusline): total session tokens (Claude + subagents). The bar now shows the combined new-token total for the session: `<main>+<sub> = <total> tok` on line 2 (e.g. `480K+3.1M = 3.6M tok`). Parent (main Claude) tokens weren't previously available — CC's payload `total_output_tokens` is per-turn-ish (reads ~hundreds for multi-hour sessions), not cumulative. Added per-session parent-token accumulation to budget-cap, which already parses the parent transcript incrementally for cost: it now also sums NEW tokens (input + cache_write + output, excluding cache_read — same basis as the sub display) into `.main-tokens-<session_id>`, keyed per-session (separate from the machine-global cost file), with the same reset-on-rotation guard so it never double-counts. The statusline reads `.main-tokens-<session>` + the sub tokens and renders the breakdown. `.main-tokens-*` added to scope-cleanup's TTL (1 week) so it doesn't leak. Verified incremental (55K → 67.2K on a second turn, not re-summed). 2 tests. 1171 passing.

- [2.7.35] - 2026-07-01 — feat(statusline): real git diff, drop redundant in/out, show combined total. A status-line review surfaced three issues. (1) The `+985/-234` segment was NOT a git diff — it was CC's `cost.total_lines_added/removed` (cumulative lines Claude edited this session, never resets), yet it was formatted like an uncommitted-diff stat next to the branch (a clean tree still showed `+985/-234`). Replaced with a real `git diff HEAD --numstat` (staged + unstaged vs HEAD) → shows current WIP size, nothing when clean, genuinely useful as a commit gauge. (2) Dropped the `563.4K in / 500 out` segment on line 2 — `in` just duplicated the context figure and `out` was a tiny low-signal number; the context bar + cache% already cover it. (3) The subagent segment now shows the combined total so the sub cost isn't misread as part of the main number: `(sub: 3.1M / $27.16 · total $119.07)`. Note: `sub:` tokens are subagents-only (spawned agents), not parent+sub. 3 test assertions. 1169 passing.

- [2.7.34] - 2026-07-01 — feat(notify): duration gate + subtitle layout for the "Done" notification. (1) The per-turn `notify-stop` "Claude — Done" banner now only fires for turns longer than a threshold (default **30s**, override with `scope/.notify-min-seconds`) — quick back-and-forth stays silent, long tasks (where you've likely switched away) still ping. The hook already computed turn duration; it just wasn't gating on it. (2) Restructured the notification into the three tiers macOS actually supports: **title** (`Claude — Done (2m 14s)`), **subtitle** (`<branch> · $<cost> session` — previously the branch was crammed into the title and cost into the body), **body** (`"query" -> response`). Added optional subtitle support to `notify-helper._send_notification` (transliterated + sanitized like title/body; Linux `notify-send`, which has no subtitle tier, folds it into the body with a newline). Native macOS notifications don't support line breaks or markdown in the banner — the title/subtitle/body tiers are the only structure available. 2 new tests (gate + threshold override). 1167 passing.

- [2.7.33] - 2026-07-01 — fix(notify): macOS notifications were mojibake for any non-ASCII char. A screenshot of a "Claude — Done" notification showed `Claude ,Äî Done ,Üí next` — AppleScript's `system attribute` (used to pass the message/title as env vars, a deliberate v2.6.72 RCE-safe channel) reads env vars as MacRoman, so UTF-8 punctuation/symbols (em-dash `—`, arrow `→`, `⇒`, smart quotes, and the economy.md logic-shorthand symbols) rendered as garbage. Fixed by transliterating the message + title to ASCII with `iconv //TRANSLIT` before the osascript call (`—`→`-`, `→`→`->`, `⇒`→`=>`), then stripping any leftover non-ASCII; the raw string is kept as a fallback if iconv is unavailable, and Linux `notify-send` (which handles UTF-8 natively) is left untouched. Caught and fixed a self-inflicted regression in the same change: `iconv //TRANSLIT` exits non-zero even on success, which under notify-permission's `set -euo pipefail` aborted the hook before it notified — added `|| true` (the new notify-events test caught it before ship). The v2.6.72 backtick/`$` RCE sanitization is preserved. 1 new test. Note: the notification shown while testing was `notify-stop` ("turn complete"), a separate hook from the `notify-permission` one fixed in v2.7.32 — that fix is confirmed working (auto-approved tools produce no permission banner).

- [2.7.32] - 2026-07-01 — fix(notify): no "Permission Needed" desktop notification for tools smart-approve auto-approves. Fallout from v2.7.29 (which fixed smart-approve so auto-approval actually works): notify-permission.sh fired a mac notification on EVERY PermissionRequest — its header claimed "Only fires for tools not auto-approved by smart-approve" but the code never checked. So every auto-approved read-only tool / safe command still pinged the user for a decision they never had to make. Extracted smart-approve's allow-list into a shared `lib-smart-approve.sh` (`smart_approve_verdict`), and both hooks now consult the SAME verdict — smart-approve to emit the allow decision, notify-permission to skip the notification — so they can't drift. smart-approve.sh shrank from ~150 lines of inline case/grep logic to a thin wrapper over the shared verdict (behavior preserved exactly: read-only tools, in-project Write/Edit, safe/read-only bash, GET-only curl, test/build runners; never inline-eval, npx/bunx, or subagent bash). 1 new test (mocked notifier: auto-approved → no notification, genuine prompt → notifies).

- [2.7.31] - 2026-07-01 — fix(hooks): the deferred channel-cluster — session memory + standards now actually reach Claude. `session-memory-inject.sh` (2 emit sites: main memory + checkpoint recovery) and `standards-inject.sh` both injected at SessionStart via `{"systemMessage":…}` — which only reaches the USER, so the project memory (`.claude/supercharger-memory.md`) and detected-stack standards were **never entering Claude's context**. The hooks appeared to work (you saw the banner) but Claude wasn't getting the content. Switched all three to `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` (the documented Claude channel for SessionStart). Of the 4 deferred hooks, **2 were left unchanged on purpose after verifying against the doc**: `permission-denied-advisor.sh` fires on `PermissionDenied`, which supports ONLY `retry` (not additionalContext) — CC already stops retrying on denial, so its systemMessage-to-user is the only thing it can do; and `precompact-priorities.sh` emits raw text on PreCompact, which the contract's exit-0 rule adds as context for that event — converting it to JSON could change compaction behavior that can't be verified without triggering a real compact, so it stays. This completes the hook-vs-doc contract audit: 10 hooks fixed across v2.7.29–.31, 3 false positives ruled out, 2 verified-leave-alone. No test changes (existing SessionStart tests assert content, not the field name).

- [2.7.30] - 2026-07-01 — fix(hooks): full contract-audit sweep of the remaining 75 hooks vs the current official output contract — 6 verified fixes. Every finding was re-verified by hand against the doc before fixing (the audit agents produced ~3 false positives, discarded). **stop-verify.sh — BROKEN (high):** emitted `{"stopReason":…}` on a Stop hook, but `stopReason` only applies when `continue:false` — so the project `.claude/verify.sh` gate NEVER blocked completion; failures were silently ignored. Now emits `{"decision":"block","reason":…}`. **tool-history-tracker.sh — payload drift (med):** read `tool_response.exit_code`, which doesn't exist on PostToolUse Bash → EVERY command logged as success, so confidence-gate never saw failures. Now infers failure from `interrupted`/strong stderr markers (same as failure-tracker). **code-security-scanner.sh — malformed (med):** `hookSpecificOutput` missing the required `hookEventName` (and used `permissionDecision:"ask"` despite a "warn, don't block" intent) → dropped by CC. Now warns Claude via `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext`. **Channel fixes (med) — systemMessage(→user) where the header says inject-to-Claude:** typecheck.sh, tool-failure-advisor.sh, post-compact-inject.sh emitted `{"systemMessage":…}`, so Claude never received the tsc errors / failure hints / restored post-compaction context they're meant to act on. Switched to `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` (supported on PostToolUse / PostToolUseFailure / PostCompact per the doc matrix). **Ruled out (false positives):** cache-health.sh + cost-forecast.sh (additionalContext IS supported on PostToolUse/PreToolUse), setup-check.sh (`Setup` IS a valid event). **Deferred (real but need per-hook care):** session-memory-inject / standards-inject / precompact-priorities (same systemMessage→additionalContext channel class); permission-denied-advisor (PermissionDenied only supports `retry`, not additionalContext). **Confirmed clean:** all blocking gates (safety/path/env/mcp-*/git-safety) use exit 2 correctly; scope-guard + session-* Stop hooks don't wipe cumulative state (v2.7.22/.23 holds); the belt-and-suspenders exit-2 + deny-JSON gates work (stderr delivers the reason). 3 tests updated/added.

- [2.7.29] - 2026-07-01 — fix(hooks): pilot of the hook-vs-doc contract audit — found smart-approve silently dead + agent-handoff-gate re-fire spam. Ran a 17-hook pilot (highest-risk: blocking gates + payload-heavy readers) against the current official hook output contract fetched fresh. **CRITICAL: smart-approve.sh was completely non-functional.** Registered on `PermissionRequest`, it emitted the *PreToolUse* shape (`{"permissionDecision":"allow",...}`), but PermissionRequest approves via `hookSpecificOutput.decision.behavior` — so CC discarded every auto-approval as malformed JSON and fell through to a normal prompt. The entire prompt-reduction feature had been a no-op (you were being asked to confirm every read-only tool and safe command it was meant to auto-approve). Fixed to the correct `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PermissionRequest","decision":{"behavior":"allow"}}}` shape. NOTE: this RE-ENABLES auto-approval — the conservative allow-list (read-only tools, in-project Write/Edit, safe/read-only bash, GET-only curl, test/build runners; never inline-eval, npx/bunx, or subagent bash). Disable with `SUPERCHARGER_*` or by removing the hook if you prefer manual prompts. **DRIFT: agent-handoff-gate.sh** had no `stop_hook_active` re-entry guard, so its `[AGENT-GATE]` quality warning was re-injected up to ~10x per SubagentStop — added the shared once-per-(session,message) latch (same as subagent-stop-check). The other 13 pilot hooks passed (blocking gates all use exit 2 correctly; mcp-output-truncator uses the correct `updatedToolOutput`; the v2.7.11–24 telemetry hardening holds). Minor items logged for the full sweep (exit-2 gates that keep their detailed reason only on stdout → terse stderr; mcp-sql-guard dead mysql/sqlite matcher branch; failure-tracker systemMessage vs additionalContext). 2 tests tightened/added. Pilot yield justifies auditing the remaining ~87 hooks.

- [2.7.28] - 2026-07-01 — test(hooks): regression guard against registering a passive hook on a Worktree* provider event. The v2.7.26→.27 regression (a passive hook on `WorktreeCreate` broke `isolation: worktree`) shipped with CI fully green because no unit test spawns a live worktree — the failure only appears when CC actually delegates creation to the hook. Added a static guard in test-install.sh asserting `lib/hooks.sh` registers nothing on `WorktreeCreate`/`WorktreeRemove`; verified the regex genuinely catches the anti-pattern. Closes the specific gap that let the regression through. Left the worktree provider-hook feature unbuilt by design (a real guard would require owning worktree creation for every isolated agent — disproportionate risk for no current problem). 1161 passing.

- [2.7.27] - 2026-07-01 — fix(hooks): revert v2.7.26 — WorktreeCreate is a PROVIDER hook, and registering a passive hook on it breaks `isolation: worktree`. Building the worktree guard the right way (capture the real payload before assuming its shape) immediately paid off: triggering a real worktree-isolated agent spawn returned `WorktreeCreate hook failed: hook succeeded but returned no worktree path (echo the path to stdout / return hookSpecificOutput.worktreePath)`. So despite appearing in CC's valid-events list, `WorktreeCreate` is NOT observational — CC *delegates worktree creation* to the registered hook and requires it to return the new worktree path. Our passive `worktree-discovery.sh` returned nothing, so CC failed the creation — meaning v2.7.26's "fix" (registering it on the events) broke worktree isolation for every agent. The original PreToolUse mis-wiring never fired but was harmless; the event registration was actively harmful. Reverted: removed both `WorktreeCreate|*`/`WorktreeRemove|*` registrations and deleted `worktree-discovery.sh` (a passive observer is fundamentally incompatible — you can't watch these events without taking over worktree creation, which is out of scope for a guard). Documented the provider-vs-observer distinction in HOOK_AUTHORING.md. Full-mode hook count 106 → 104. Lesson: a "valid event" is not necessarily an *observable* event — verify behavior by triggering it, don't assume from the events list.

- [2.7.26] - 2026-07-01 — fix(hooks): worktree-discovery was registered on the wrong surface and never fired. A doc-vs-code audit (verifying all 25 registered events against the current CC hooks reference — all valid post-2.7.25) surfaced that `worktree-discovery.sh` was wired as `PreToolUse|WorktreeCreate,WorktreeRemove` — but `WorktreeCreate`/`WorktreeRemove` are hook EVENTS, not tool names, so the PreToolUse matcher never matched (the adjacent `CronCreate,CronDelete,CronList` line IS correct — those genuinely are tools). Re-registered on the two events directly (`WorktreeCreate|*`, `WorktreeRemove|*`), so the discovery hook now actually captures git-worktree lifecycle payloads to `audit/worktree-payloads.jsonl` — the intended observability for designing future worktree safety guards (block creation outside the project tree, prevent removal of dirty worktrees). Also generalized the discovery record to capture ALL top-level payload keys (event payloads differ from the tool_input shape it previously assumed). These are the only two binary-valid events Supercharger wasn't effectively subscribed to; every other valid event is covered. Full-mode hook count 105 → 106 (one mis-wired registration became two correct ones). Updated the discovery test to the event payload shape.

- [2.7.25] - 2026-06-30 — fix(hooks): remove two hooks registered on Claude-Code events that no longer exist, silencing a settings.json startup warning. Current CC rejects `MessageDisplay` and `UserPromptExpansion` as unknown hook events (they existed in mid-2026 builds but were dropped), and printed a "Settings Warning … Unknown hook event was ignored" panel on every launch for anyone with the full install. Cross-checked all 27 registered events against CC's current valid-events list — exactly these two were invalid; all others (including the newer WorktreeCreate/CwdChanged/FileChanged/etc.) are fine. Removed both registrations from `lib/hooks.sh` plus the now-dead hook files `messagedisplay-discovery.sh` (payload-discovery only) and `slash-command-guard.sh` (slash-expansion scanner — its event is gone, so it never fired). Existing installs self-heal on next `/sc-update`: the settings merge strips tagged entries and deletes the emptied event keys, and deploy prunes the orphaned .sh files. Full-mode hook count 107 → 105 (safe mode unchanged at 24). Docs (HOOKS.md, HOOK_AUTHORING.md) updated; if CC reintroduces an expansion/display event the hooks are restorable from git history. Removed test-slash-command-guard.sh + the MessageDisplay discovery-test case.

- [2.7.24] - 2026-06-30 — change(statusline): subagent `(sub:)` segment now shows NEW tokens only (input + cache_write + output), excluding cache_read. Investigating a "sub: 12.3M / $5.62" reading showed it was legit but misleading: `total_tokens` sums all four token types, and **~96% of it was cache_read** — the agent's context re-read on every assistant message — so 12.3M represented throughput, not work. The cost was always accurate (computed with correct per-type pricing; the $0.46/M blended rate was the giveaway that cache reads dominated). Now the segment reports the truer "work done" number: e.g. that same session drops from 16.2M → 795K with the cost unchanged. Reads the per-agent breakdown fields already stored by subagent-cost.sh; falls back to `total_tokens` for legacy rows that lack them. `/sc-status` was unaffected (its subagent rollup is cost-only). statusline.sh only. 1 new test. 1181 passing.

- [2.7.23] - 2026-06-30 — fix(scope/leaks): kill the rest of the "Stop wipes cumulative state" bug class + plug scope-file leaks (deep-dive follow-up to v2.7.22). Two read-only auditors swept the file lifecycle. **(B1) scope-guard `clear` (runs every Stop) was wiping three more cumulative/baseline files:** `.snapshot-$SID` (scope-guard's OWN SessionStart baseline that `check` diffs against — wiping it per turn silently disabled ALL scope-alerts after turn 1, the hook destroying the data it needs), `.tool-history-$SID` (confidence-gate's session failure history), and `.subagent-safety-injected-$SID` (the once-per-session preamble-dedup flag → re-injected ~150 tok every turn). Removed all three from the per-turn `rm`; each is now TTL-pruned instead, and the orphan-prune `find` was extended to cover `.snapshot-*`/`.contract-*`. **(B2) `.blocked-commands` was an unbounded append log — already 3.4 MB.** Added a 500-line cap at both writers (git-safety.sh, safety.sh) and trimmed the live file. **(B3) scope-cleanup.sh had 10 dead/misnamed TTL patterns** (`.loop-detector*`→ real `.loop-history`, `.tier-snapshot-*`→ `.snapshot-*`, `.eco-reinforce-counter`→ `.eco-reinforce-acked`, `.last-idle-notify`, `.eco-stop-*`, `.pending-*`, bare `.gate-pending`, three orphaned `.prompt-*`) that pruned nothing, plus ~9 real scope files matched by NO pruner (leaked forever). Rewrote the pattern array: removed the dead, added coverage for `.failed-commands-*`, `.subagent-spawns-*`, `.scan-alert-*`, `.contract-*`, `.router-roster-*`, `.tool-calls-*`, `.standards-inject-*`, `.eco-last-*`, `.snapshot-*`, `.tool-history-*`, `.loop-history`, `.read-history`. **(B4) checkpoint cleanup was a bare `.checkpoint-*` glob** in two Stop hooks (session-complete.sh, session-memory-write.sh) → a concurrent session's Stop deleted OTHER live sessions' checkpoints. Scoped both to `.checkpoint-<session_id>`. Root insight (same as v2.7.22): `Stop` fires every turn, not at session end — session-scoped cleanup belongs at SessionEnd / TTL, not on Stop. 2 new tests + 1 updated. 1180 passing.

- [2.7.22] - 2026-06-30 — fix(scope-guard): stop wiping subagent-cost telemetry on every turn — the actual root cause behind the statusline `(sub:)` segment "disappearing". A filesystem watcher on the scope dir caught it red-handed: `.subagent-costs-<session>.jsonl` was CREATED when an agent ran, then DELETED ~90s later — and `scope-guard.sh`'s `clear` mode (registered `Stop|*|scope-guard.sh clear`, so it runs at **every turn end**) had `.subagent-costs-$SID.jsonl` in its `rm -f` list. So the cumulative session cost log got nuked one turn after any agent finished, which is why the rollup and statusline segment showed briefly then vanished — and why prior live "repairs" never stuck (the next Stop wiped them). `clear` is meant to reset per-prompt scratch state (agent classification, router hash, repetition flags, …); subagent cost is cumulative session telemetry that `/sc-status` and the statusline read, and is already TTL-pruned (1 week) by scope-cleanup. Removed it from the clear list. The earlier v2.7.19–21 work (show the segment, stop re-fire clobber, stop ghost rows) was all real and necessary, but THIS was why it kept vanishing. Note for follow-up: `.tool-history-$SID` is in the same clear list and is also cumulative (confidence-gate reads it as "session tool-history") — likely the same bug class, left untouched pending a decision since it changes confidence/repetition scope. 1 new test (clear preserves subagent-costs, still clears per-turn scratch). No hook-count change. 1178 tests passing.

- [2.7.21] - 2026-06-30 — fix(telemetry): suppress phantom $0 subagent rows from ghost SubagentStop firings. Live-testing the statusline segment showed the per-session subagent log accumulating spurious `agent / $0 / 0 tok` rows alongside the real recording — CC fires extra "ghost" SubagentStop events with a *fresh* agent_id, no `agent_type` (so the name falls back to `"agent"`) and no transcript, yielding $0/0-token entries. They didn't corrupt the cost sum (they add 0) but cluttered `.subagent-costs-<session>.jsonl`, inflated `/sc-status` run counts, and — when one landed before any real agent recorded — left the session file looking empty so the statusline showed no `(sub:)` segment. Fix: in the stop handler, skip writing when it's a brand-new agent_id with the `"agent"` fallback name AND zero cost/tokens — a genuine run always carries an `agent_type`, so this never drops a real (even zero-usage) agent. Verified: ghost firing → no row; real agent → row; ghost after real → real preserved, no phantom. Also cleaned the live session file. 1 new test. No hook-count change. 1177 tests passing.

- [2.7.20] - 2026-06-30 — fix(telemetry): a data-less SubagentStop re-fire no longer clobbers a good subagent recording. Found by live-testing the new statusline segment: spawning an agent recorded `general-purpose / 385K tok / $0.49`, but seconds later the row had reverted to `agent / $0` — a *later* re-fire of the SAME agent arrived after its transcript had been cleaned up, so it computed `$0` with the `"agent"` fallback name, and the v2.7.13 upsert (which always wrote the latest firing) overwrote the richer earlier row. The aggregate was already protected (`cost_delta = max(0, turn−prev)` = 0 for a weaker firing), but the per-agent JSONL row — what `/sc-status` and the v2.7.19 statusline segment read — got downgraded to zero. Fix: track the prior best (highest-cost) row per `agent_id` and, when the current firing is weaker (`turn_cost <= prev_cost`), keep that row (refreshing only `stopped_at`/`duration_s`) instead of replacing it; only overwrite when the new firing has more cost. So the recorded row is now monotonic per agent and survives transcript-cleanup re-fires. 1 new test (data-less re-fire preserves the good row). No hook-count change. 1176 tests passing.

- [2.7.19] - 2026-06-30 — feat(statusline): show subagent tokens + cost on the cost line. CC's statusline payload reports only the PARENT session's `cost.total_cost_usd` — it does not fold in subagent usage (subagents run as separate contexts; CC never exposes their tokens to the parent). So the bar silently undercounted whenever agents were spawned. The cost line now appends a `(sub: <tokens> / $<cost>)` segment computed from this session's `.subagent-costs-<session_id>.jsonl` — e.g. `Tokens: $440.49 equiv (sub: 220.0K / $0.55)`. Deduped by `agent_id` (SubagentStop re-fires can leave more than one row per agent; keep the highest, matching `subagent-cost.sh`'s last-cumulative-wins upsert), and omitted entirely when the session spawned no agents. Defensive (wrapped in try/except, never crashes the bar). 2 new tests (deduped segment present; absent with no agents). No hook-count change. 1175 tests passing.

- [2.7.18] - 2026-06-30 — fix(quality): lesson-record was polluting `lessons.jsonl` with junk fragments, and the `/sc-status` subagent line read as session-scoped when it's global. **lesson-record.sh** matched a diagnostic marker (`root cause`, `fixed by`, …) anywhere in the assistant's last message and recorded "marker → end of first sentence" — so ordinary prose produced garbage: a dashboard reply containing "…the offset drifted (root cause)." stored the fragment `"root cause)."`, and the hook even recorded snippets of its *own* explanations. `lesson-recall` then re-injected that junk into context every prompt. New quality gate: (1) reject parenthetical asides (`(root cause)`); (2) extract the FULL sentence containing the marker, not just the tail; (3) require substance (≥30 chars, ≥6 words); (4) reject the assistant *narrating* about debugging ("I can't pin the root cause down…", "Let me check…") vs. *stating* a finding ("Root cause: the cwd is in workspace.current_dir", "Fixed by adding a guard") — the standalone-`I` check is case-sensitive so it doesn't false-reject `it`/`is`/`in`. Also cleaned the live `lessons.jsonl` (6 junk/dup → 4 real findings). **`configs/commands/sc-status.md`** — relabeled the rollup `Subagents (all sessions)` and documented that it's a cross-session `/usage`-style aggregate (every `.subagent-costs-*.jsonl` on the machine), not the current session — it was correct-per-spec but read as session-scoped. 4 new tests (parenthetical/narration reject, declarative accept, no false-reject on it/is/in). No hook-count change. 1173 tests passing.

- [2.7.17] - 2026-06-30 — fix(telemetry): budget-cap's incremental transcript offset was broken — re-summed the whole transcript and inflated cost ~3.8x. Surfaced (again) by real `/sc-status` data: the stored `main_total` read **$4,916 / 19,718 turns** while the actual transcript held only **5,138 usage-messages** worth ~**$1,288**. Root cause: v2.7.15 advanced the byte offset with `new_offset += len(line.encode())` and used text-mode `f.seek(offset)` — but Python text-mode `seek()` only accepts opaque `f.tell()` cookies, not hand-counted byte counts, so the offset drifted, overshot the real file size (51.7 MB stored vs a 33 MB file), tripped the `offset > size` reset, and re-summed the entire transcript on the next call → runaway. Fix: read the transcript in **binary mode** with `readline()` + real `f.tell()` byte positions that `seek()` honors (also correct for non-ASCII, which text-mode byte-counting mangled); and when the offset is past EOF (rotation or a legacy-corrupt offset) reset `main_total` and the main `turn_count` too, so it re-derives cleanly instead of stacking onto an inflated value — meaning a deploy of this version self-heals the corrupted `.session-cost`. Verified against the live 33 MB transcript: fresh-derive = **$1,292.72 / 5,148 turns** (matches an independent per-message sum), and a repeat call on the unchanged file adds exactly $0. 1 new test (offset-exactness across repeated calls, with a non-ASCII line). No hook-count change. 1169 tests passing.

- [2.7.16] - 2026-06-29 — fix(concurrency): Batch 3 — serialize the `.session-cost` read-modify-write and dedup two Stop re-fires. **(1) `.session-cost` RMW race** — budget-cap (async, every PostToolUse) and subagent-cost (SubagentStop) both read→modify→rename the same file; the atomic rename prevented corruption but not lost updates (last writer wins, the other's delta dropped). Now both serialize the whole RMW under `fcntl.flock` (portable across macOS+Linux — the `flock` shell util isn't on macOS; both writers are already in Python). budget-cap's write was split python-compute / bash-`mv`; moved the tmp-write+rename into Python so the lock spans the full read→write (held across the incremental transcript read since the resume offset lives in the file). Verified: 10 concurrent accumulators racing the same 10-turn transcript from offset 0 converge to exactly `$0.030` / 10 turns — each message counted once, no loss, no double-count (without the lock they'd over-count or drop updates). **(2) session-complete.sh** — added a `stop_hook_active` guard so the session-complete webhook fires once per session, not once per Stop re-entry (duplicate notifications). **(3) event-logger.sh** — skip `subagent_stop` re-fires (was logging ~10 duplicate lines per agent; bounded by the 500-line rotation but noise). **Deliberately skipped** (low value): tool-call-limiter counter lock (only matters with a configured `maxToolCalls` under parallel tools — minor under-count) and repetition-detector global-state re-keying. 2 new tests (concurrent-write correctness; event-logger re-fire dedup). No hook-count change. 1168 tests passing. This closes the bug cluster that started from a single `$0.00` in `/sc-status`: v2.7.11–16 fixed 11 silent failures across 12 hooks (subagent cost/name/duration/report-pin/handoff-gate, budget cap, failure learning, cache monitor, lesson dedup, two security scanners, this race).

- [2.7.15] - 2026-06-29 — fix(telemetry): Batch 2 — revive 3 hooks that were silently DEAD because CC's PostToolUse payload carries no token usage and no exit code. Proven by a captured real payload: `tool_response` = `{interrupted, isImage, noOutputExpected, stderr, stdout}` — no `usage`, no `exit_code`; the payload top-level has no `usage` either. **(1) budget-cap.sh** — the session budget cap read `.usage`/`.tool_response.usage` → always 0 → never accumulated → **never enforced** (a guardrail that did nothing). Rewrote the accumulator to source usage from the `transcript_path` CC *does* provide, summing each assistant message's `usage` (one billed API call) INCREMENTALLY past a stored byte offset (`main_offset`) — O(new lines) per call, no re-sum, no over-count. Tracks `main_total` separately from `subagent_total` so budget-cap and subagent-cost each add only their own delta (invariant `total_usd == main_total + subagent_total`); subagent-cost now preserves the two new fields. Verified: cap blocks writes / passes reads once spend exceeds it. **(2) failure-tracker.sh** — read `.tool_response.exit_code` (absent) → exited on every command → repeated-failure learning never fired. Now infers failure from `interrupted=true` OR a strong stderr/stdout error marker (command not found, Traceback, fatal:, ModuleNotFoundError, Segmentation fault, …) — unambiguous failures only, so benign tools that write progress/warnings to stderr (git, npm) don't trip a false nudge. Also keyed the log by project hash (was global → a failure in repo A nudged in repo B) and capped it at 200 lines (was unbounded). **(3) cache-health.sh** — read cache tokens from `tool_response.usage` (absent) → always skipped → inert. Now sources the latest assistant turn's cache stats from the transcript; degraded-hit-rate warnings work again. Confirmed via a real PostToolUse capture that failed commands fire PostToolUse (not PostToolUseFailure), so failure-tracker is on the right event. 4 existing tests rewritten to the real payload shape + 2 new (failure-marker discrimination, cache transcript-source). No hook-count change. 1166 tests passing.

- [2.7.14] - 2026-06-29 — fix(security): Batch 1 of a deep payload/dead-code audit — 4 silently-broken hooks found by a 3-agent sweep that cross-referenced every hook's field reads + regex against **captured real CC 2.1.181 payloads** (the same method that found the subagent telemetry bugs). All verified empirically, not assumed. **(1) memory-write-guard.sh** — the base64 markers `aWdub3JlI`/`c3lzdGVtI` were matched against `.lower()`-ed content, so the uppercase letters could NEVER match (the exact dead-pattern bug fixed in prompt-injection-scanner at v2.7.7 — this hook was missed). Moved to a `cased_patterns` group matched against the non-lowercased NFKC text; verified a base64-`ignore` memory write now blocks. **(2) skill-poisoning-scanner.sh** — all 10 patterns were case-sensitive against raw skill text (unlike its 3 sibling scanners), so UPPERCASE injection (`IGNORE ALL PREVIOUS INSTRUCTIONS`, `CURL … | SH`) evaded every CRITICAL/HIGH check. Added `re.IGNORECASE` to all; verified uppercase curl-pipe-to-shell now blocks, clean skills still pass. **(3) safety.sh** — the `_NEED_PY` bash fast-path gate was narrower than `safety-detect.py`'s `_SENSITIVE_NAME_RE`, so `cat ~/.my.cnf` (MySQL creds), `.authinfo`, `.crt`, `.cer` never reached the sensitive-file detector. Widened the gate to a superset; verified those reads now block, `cat README.md` still passes. **(4) lesson-record.sh** — no `stop_hook_active` guard, so CC's Stop re-fires (the same repeated-firing behavior behind the v2.7.13 cost over-count) appended an IDENTICAL lesson to `lessons.jsonl` on every re-entry (N duplicates per lesson, which lesson-recall then surfaced N times). Added the early-exit guard (same idiom as notify-stop.sh). 5 new tests. **Still queued (Batch 2/3 — bigger):** budget-cap.sh / failure-tracker.sh / cache-health.sh are all confirmed DEAD (a captured PostToolUse payload proved `tool_response` = `{interrupted,isImage,noOutputExpected,stderr,stdout}` — NO `usage`, NO `exit_code`), so the session budget cap never enforces, repeated-failure learning never fires, and cache-health is inert; fixes need transcript-based usage + stderr-based failure inference. Plus `.session-cost`/`tool-call-limiter` flock for the RMW race. No hook-count change. 1165 tests passing.

- [2.7.13] - 2026-06-29 — fix(telemetry): SubagentStop re-fires no longer over-count subagent cost ~7-10x. Live-validating the v2.7.11/12 fixes (spawning a real throwaway agent) exposed a latent bug the single-stop synthetic tests couldn't: CC fires `SubagentStop` **repeatedly** for one agent (the `stop_hook_active` re-entry — observed ~10x), and each firing carries the *cumulative* transcript total. `subagent-cost.sh` **appended** a JSONL row and added the full cumulative to `.session-cost` every time — so one ~$0.40 agent logged 10 rows totalling ~$2.97 and pushed `subagent_total` to $4+. This was invisible before v2.7.11 (every row was $0, so 10×0 still summed to 0); making the rows nonzero surfaced it. Fix: **upsert one row per `agent_id`** (rewrite the session JSONL dropping any prior row for this agent, last-cumulative-wins) and add only the **delta** (`current_cumulative − previously_logged`) to the `.session-cost` aggregate — so any number of re-fires converges to the agent's true final cost. Also carries forward the real `duration_s` (the first stop captures it from the active file; re-fires find the file already deleted and compute 0). Verified: 3 re-fires with a growing transcript → 1 row at the final cost, `subagent_total` == that single cost (not 3×); two distinct agents still both counted. 1 new test (re-fire upsert + no-inflation). **Not done — live-data repair:** attempted to rewrite the already-inflated `.session-cost`/JSONL for the current session, but the active session's hooks are concurrently writing those files (the aggregate climbed $4.03 → $6.70 during the work), so a mid-session rewrite races and corrupts — restored the backup and left it. The inflation is cosmetic and self-corrects for future sessions once this version is deployed; a one-off cleanup can be run later when no session is active. No hook-count change. 1160 tests passing.

- [2.7.12] - 2026-06-29 — fix(subagent): two more hooks hit by the same CC 2.1.181 payload-key drift the v2.7.11 cost fix exposed — audited every subagent hook against the captured real payloads (SubagentStart = `subagent_id`/`subagent_type`; SubagentStop = `agent_id`/`agent_type`/`last_assistant_message`). **(1) `subagent-safety.sh` (SubagentStart)** read `.agent_id`/`.agent_type`, but Start sends `subagent_id`/`subagent_type` — so `AGENT_ID` was empty and the report-pin path fell back to `<session>-<timestamp>.md`, while the SubagentStop fallback writes `<agent_id>.md`. The two never matched, so the v2.6.82 report-pin (which tells each subagent where to Write its own report) was silently pointing every subagent at the wrong path — the v2.7.1 SubagentStop fallback was quietly compensating the whole time. Now reads `.agent_id // .subagent_id` and `.agent_type // .subagent_type`, so the pinned path matches what recovery reads. **(2) `agent-handoff-gate.sh` (SubagentStop)** extracted the agent's output from `.result`/`.output`, but Stop puts it in `last_assistant_message` — so the gate saw empty output and `exit 0`'d every time, never flagging incomplete/unverified subagent work. Now reads `last_assistant_message` first (result/output/content kept as fallbacks). Also added defensive `.agent_id // .subagent_id` to `subagent-report-fallback.sh` and `subagent-report-notify.sh` for cross-version resilience. **Audited clean (no fix needed):** `subagent-report-fallback`/`subagent-report-notify` (Stop, already used `agent_id` correctly), `subagent-stop-check` (already read `agent_type`), `subagent-circuit-breaker` (keys on `session_id`, present in both), `subagent-discovery` (shape-agnostic). All existing tests still pass (old shape supported via the `// ` fallbacks). 3 new tests (subagent-safety pins from subagent_id; handoff-gate fires/stays-silent on last_assistant_message). No hook-count change. 1159 tests passing.

- [2.7.11] - 2026-06-29 — fix(telemetry): subagent cost tracking was silently logging zeros — three bugs, all from payload-key drift in Claude Code 2.1.181. Surfaced via `/sc-status`: 119 logged subagent runs, every one `cost_usd: 0`, `agent_name: "agent"`, `duration_s: 0`. Captured the real CC payloads (`audit/subagent-payloads.jsonl` `_top_keys`) and found: **(1) cost always 0** — CC's SubagentStop payload carries **no `usage`/token field at all**, but `subagent-cost.sh` read `d.get('usage')`. Fix: when the payload has no usage, sum per-message `usage` from `agent_transcript_path` (which CC *does* provide) — each assistant message is one billed API call, so summing input/cache-write/cache-read/output across the transcript is the correct cost basis; model for pricing is read from the transcript too (payload has no `model` either). **(2) name always "agent"** — SubagentStop sends `agent_type`, the hook read `agent_name`/`name`. **(3) duration always 0** — SubagentStart sends `subagent_id`/`subagent_type` while SubagentStop sends `agent_id`/`agent_type`, so the start handler wrote the `.subagent-active-<id>` file under a different key than the stop handler read it back — the start-time lookup always missed. Fix for (2)+(3): read both spellings (`agent_id or subagent_id`, `agent_name or agent_type or subagent_type or name`) in both handlers so START and STOP agree on the id and the type becomes the name. Verified end-to-end against a real 311K-token agent transcript: name `Marie Curie (Scientist)`, duration 2.0s, cost **$0.35** (was $0.00). Backward compatible — a payload that *does* carry inline `usage` (old shape / future CC) still takes priority over the transcript. 3 new tests (agent_type→name, transcript→cost, subagent_id↔agent_id→duration); existing 5 still pass. No hook-count change. 1156 tests passing.

- [2.7.10] - 2026-06-29 — feat(install): Quick/Custom install fork — cut first-time interactive install from 6 prompts to 2. The wizard asked Mode, Roles, Economy, MCP, Notifications, and Conventional Commits as blocking prompts, but 4 of those are tunable preferences with sensible defaults (and post-install switches) — above-standard friction for a config-layer tool. New top-level fork: **press Enter for Quick** (Full · Developer · minimal economy · light MCP · conventional commits on) which then asks only **Notifications** (a personal/environmental preference that can't be guessed) plus the existing safety prompts for any pre-existing `CLAUDE.md`/`settings.json`; **type `c` for Custom** which runs the unchanged full wizard. Net first-time install: 6 prompts → 2 (1–2 more only if you have existing config to merge). The Quick fork only appears interactively (skipped when any `--flag` is passed, and after the update/reinstall branch). Also: suppressed the secondary "Run MCP setup?" wizard prompt in `deploy_extras` for Quick (the MCP profile is already chosen — it was a third hidden prompt that ate stdin), and dropped the inconsistent "Step X of 7/8" header numbering (it was buggy — commits is dev-only so the total drifted — and meaningless once Quick shows only a subset). All defaults remain post-install switchable (`eco lean`, `tools/mcp-setup.sh`, re-run `./install.sh` → Custom). `--flag` non-interactive installs are byte-for-byte unchanged. 2 new tests (Quick fork applies defaults; Custom fork routes to wizard and honors choices). Verified end-to-end in an isolated HOME: Quick exits 0 with full/developer/minimal/light/commits-on and 2 prompts; Custom exits 0 honoring all wizard choices. 1153 tests passing.

- [2.7.9] - 2026-06-28 — perf(tokens): Batch B token-economy trims from the context-injection audit (the low-risk subset; behavior-changing items B1b/B3/B5 deliberately skipped). **B1a — `thinking-budget.sh` dedup.** The `[THINK]` line was re-injected on every prompt with no dedup — in a debugging/refactor session nearly every prompt classifies "high", stacking the identical ~13-tok line dozens of times into resident context. Now deduped on the classified level via the existing `hook_already_emitted` (600s TTL): consecutive same-level prompts inject once; a level CHANGE (high→low) re-emits immediately. Classifier logic unchanged — zero drift risk. **B2 — `agent-router.sh` roster once per session.** The project-agent roster (`project=Name: desc; …`, 200-600+ chars in repos with `.claude/agents/`) was rescanned and re-injected on every category-change prompt, though it's static for the session. Now gated by a per-session flag: the roster is built+injected on the first emission, then a flag is set and later prompts skip the scan entirely and carry just `task=`/`agent=`/`tier=`. **B4 — `standards-inject.sh` standard-tier cap.** The standard-tier stack-rule body was injected uncapped; now capped at 1500 chars/stack with a "see rules/stacks/<name>.md" pointer. Honest note: the *bundled* stack files are all <1KB, so for default installs this realizes ~no savings — it's a defensive ceiling against an oversized *custom* `~/.claude/rules/stacks/<name>.md` (verified: a 4.4KB custom file injects 1559 chars instead of the full body). The real per-prompt wins here are B1a + B2. **Skipped (need a separate decision):** B1b thinking-budget bash fast-path (classification-drift risk), B3 merge `[CTX]`+`[THINK]` (changes both hooks' contract), B5 `config-scan`→async (security warning would surface a beat later). 3 new tests (thinking-budget dedup + level-change re-emit; agent-router roster-once). No hook-count change. 1151 tests passing.

- [2.7.8] - 2026-06-28 — perf: remove redundant python3 forks from the SessionStart + per-prompt paths (Batch A of a measured hot-path audit; identical output, zero behavior change). A profiling sweep measured a cold `python3 -c` fork at ~21ms on this box vs `jq` at ~2.3ms, so each avoidable python fork on a blocking path is the prize. Four mechanical swaps, each producing semantically identical output: (1) `session-memory-inject.sh:27` — the checkpoint-recovery loop forked `python3` per `.checkpoint-*` file to compute mtime age; replaced with GNU-first/BSD-fallback `stat` + bash arithmetic (the same `<86400s` guard), saving ~21ms × N checkpoint files on every session where the project memory file is absent. (2) `session-memory-inject.sh:68` — the `last_cost` enrichment forked `python3` to read `total_usd` with an mtime guard; now `stat` + `jq -r '.total_usd // empty'`. (3) `standards-inject.sh:153` and (4) `lesson-recall.sh:108` — both forked a 2nd `python3` purely to JSON-escape one string; replaced with `jq -Rs '.'` (python kept as the jq-less fallback), matching the pattern `agent-router.sh`/`context-advisor.sh` already use. Net: ~40-60ms off first-prompt (SessionStart) latency plus a per-prompt fork removed from the lesson-recall match path. Output is byte-identical for ASCII and decodes identically for Unicode (jq emits raw UTF-8, python emitted `\uXXXX` — both valid JSON, and jq -Rs is already the codebase norm). **Deferred to Batch B (behavior-changing, pending decision):** `thinking-budget.sh` fast-path/dedup, `agent-router.sh` per-prompt line trim + once-per-session project-agent roster, merging the `[CTX]`/`[THINK]` lines, capping `standards-inject` full-file body, `config-scan.sh`→async. The `detect_stack` double-fork and economy-tier-resolution "wins" from the audit were dropped — the former still needs its python fork for rule parsing (not mechanical), the latter only pays its cost when the tier scope file is absent (rare in practice). No hook-count change, no new tests (covered by existing suites). 1148 tests passing.

- [2.7.7] - 2026-06-28 — test+fix(security): dedicated prompt-injection-scanner suite, which surfaced a dead-pattern bug. The scanner (PostToolUse on `mcp__*`/`WebFetch`/`WebSearch`/`Read` — it sees ALL external/MCP/file output) had only 4 tests in `test-hooks.sh` against ~16 pattern classes. New `tests/test-prompt-injection-scanner.sh` (24 tests) exercises every class — instruction-override variants, persona hijack, new-instructions, system-prompt-leak (action-verb gated), discard/forget, role override, virtualization, authority-shift, jailbreak, all four token-injection markers, base64 decode/stub, Read `.content` field, plus the false-positive guards (clean RAG blog, bare "system prompt" mention, non-external tool skipped, empty/malformed input). **Bug found & fixed:** the hook lowercased output (`unicodedata.normalize('NFKC', output).lower()`) before matching ALL patterns — but the base64 markers `aWdub3JlI` ("ignore") and `c3lzdGVtI` ("system") are **case-sensitive**, so against lowercased text they could **never fire** (dead since v2.6.84). Split them into a `cased_patterns` tuple matched against the non-lowercased NFKC text; the other patterns still run on the lowercased copy. Verified both stubs now block (`aWdub3JlIHRoZQ==`, `c3lzdGVtIHByb21wdA==`) with no new false positives. No hook-count change (edits to an existing hook + a new test file; `run.sh` auto-discovers `test-*.sh`). This completes the v2.7.x security-hardening line — the entire research backlog from the 3-agent sweep is now shipped and tested. 1148 tests passing.

- [2.7.6] - 2026-06-28 — feat(security): TrustFall / SymJack project-MCP scan at SessionStart (the last high-value item from the May–Jun 2026 disclosure sweep). **TrustFall** (Adversa AI, May 2026): a cloned repo ships `.claude/settings.json` with `enableAllProjectMcpServers: true` plus a `.mcp.json` pointing at an attacker binary; the moment the folder-trust dialog is accepted (or in headless CI, with no dialog at all) every project MCP server auto-spawns as an **unsandboxed** OS process with full user privileges. Anthropic reviewed and **declined to patch** — the trust dialog is their intended boundary — which makes a hook the only viable defense layer. A PreToolUse hook can't help (it fires after the server is already running), so this is a SessionStart advisory in `hooks/config-scan.sh`. Two checks: (1) `scan_settings` now flags `enableAllProjectMcpServers: true` in any settings file — the auto-spawn-without-prompt enabler. (2) New project `.mcp.json` scan flags stdio servers whose `command`/`args` indicate a repo-shipped executable — a relative command path, a command resolving inside the project dir, or a project-local script passed to an interpreter (`node ./scripts/mcp.js`) — the SymJack/TrustFall attacker-binary shape. Well-known launchers with no project-local path (`npx -y @scope/pkg`, `uvx`, `docker run`, `node <package>`) stay **silent** to keep false positives near zero (verified); http/sse servers (no `command`) are ignored — not a spawn vector. Both surface a `systemMessage` advising the user to review `.mcp.json` before trusting the folder. 5 new tests in `tests/test-hooks.sh` (enableAll + relative-command + project-local-script warn; npx + docker silent). With this the research backlog from the v2.7.x sweep is drained. Hook count unchanged (107 full / 24 safe — `config-scan.sh` already registered in safe mode). 1124 tests passing.

- [2.7.5] - 2026-06-28 — fix(security): two incident-backed exfil/config-tamper gaps from the same research sweep as v2.7.3 (CC/MCP disclosures May–Jun 2026). (1) **`ps` env-dump exfil — "Comment and Control" (CVSS 9.4, Apr 2026).** PR-title prompt-injection ran `ps auxeww | base64` to read *every* process's environment (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN`) and exfil it base64-encoded via a PR comment — bypassing GitHub secret scanning, and distinct from the `/proc/self/environ` read we already block (different mechanism: `ps` process listing, not a file read).
