(1) `hooks/output-secrets-scanner.sh` only covered GCP via the service-account JSON shape (`"private_key":"-----BEGIN`); the most common GCP credential — `AIza`-prefix API keys used by Maps, Firebase, Translate, YouTube — was not in the pattern table. Added `'AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}'`. Verified detection in isolation + via the existing `test-hooks.sh` panel (new "detects GCP API key (AIza) in output" test). (2) `README.md` Modes table said `standard | All 83 hooks active`; current truth is 96 (full+developer, commit-check opt-in). Updated to `All 96 hooks active`. Other 28 claims verified working: 6/6 destructive Bash patterns blocked, 12/12 path-guard targets (path traversal incl. URL-encoded, .git/hooks, selfmod×3, ~/.ssh, ~/.aws, /etc, node_modules/.bin, .next, .venv), 6/6 code-security patterns (eval, pickle, SQL, md5, hardcoded secrets, GH Actions inj), 8/8 credential patterns now (AWS, OpenAI, Slack, Stripe, GitHub, JWT, Azure storage, GCP service-account JSON + the new AIza), 3/3 prompt-injection patterns, 4/4 smart-approve cases (Read/Glob/git-status approved, rm correctly not), budget cap blocks over 100%, cost forecast fires before Task, bash-output compactor fires >50 lines, OWASP alignment (least-agency opt-out, audit JSONL, CVE patterns in config-scan), memory infra (MEMORY.md, per-project memory, decisions/learn), PreCompact preservation (backup, inject, priorities), 8 roles, 24 commands, 9 agents, statusline, /sc-status, think flags, stack detection (React/Next + Python/Flask), agent-router. Audit harness gotcha for future test-writers: zsh `echo` interprets `\n` to actual newline, breaking JSON payloads with content strings; use `printf '%s'` to preserve the backslash-n escape. 283 tests passing in test-hooks (was 282).
- [2.6.47] - 2026-06-18 — feat(advisory): shell-escape-advisor for `!`-prefix prompts. Diagnosed a real-session incident where a user `! rm -rf /Users/.../project-dir` ran from inside that project's CWD wiped the directory contents without firing safety.sh — confirmed via events.log (zero block events, zero bash_command events between session_start at 05:39:16Z and tool_failure at 05:43:17Z, only MessageDisplay/subagent_stop in between). Root cause: Claude Code's `!` prompt prefix runs the command directly in the user's shell, bypassing the PreToolUse:Bash hook chain entirely — supercharger's enforcement layer never sees it. safety.sh itself is correct (manual repro with `.cwd` = target blocks via the existing project-root rule). New `hooks/shell-escape-advisor.sh` (UserPromptSubmit) scans prompts whose body starts with `!` for `rm -rf`-with-dangerous-target, `curl|bash`, `wget|sh`, `git push --force`, and `git reset --hard`. Emits a `[Supercharger] …` advisory to stderr — informational only, the shell still executes the command (hooks cannot block shell-escape execution at this layer). Three target tiers preserved: home/`/`/`..` (most severe wording), CWD (`.`, `./`, `*`), absolute path. Silent on safe `! ls`, on prompts that mention rm without the `!` prefix, on empty/malformed JSON. Registered in `lib/hooks.sh:90`. 12 new tests passing. README "Scope of protection" subsection added under Standards alignment documenting the two flows Supercharger cannot reach (user `!` escapes and terminal commands outside the session) and recommending a shell-level guard (zsh function, `command_not_found_handler`) for users who want enforcement regardless of source — explicitly framing supercharger as a Claude-Code-layer, not a shell-level guard. 927 tests passing.
- [2.6.46] - 2026-06-17 — docs: README OWASP Agentic 2026 alignment section + path-guard description bump. Two README changes derived from today's guardrails research scout. (1) Updated the "Path guard" line in the Runtime enforcement section from "5 attack categories" to "6", calling out the new `selfmod` category shipped in v2.6.45 with the Ona Security sandbox-bypass citation. (2) Added a new top-level "Standards alignment" section that maps Supercharger to the [OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications 2026](https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-top-10-for-agentic-applications-for-2026/) framework: Least-Agency (per-category opt-out is least-privilege in practice), Strong Observability (blocked-commands log + audit JSONL satisfies the "tool-use patterns + decision pathways reconstructable" requirement), Self-modification defense (the v2.6.45 path-guard category covers the documented Ona Security bypass shape), and Sandbox-bypass attempt detection (config-scan.sh's three-CVE coverage: CVE-2025-59536, CVE-2026-21852, CVE-2026-33068). Positioning rationale: OWASP Agentic 2026 was released Dec 2025; mapping to it is a defensible "we're in this category" claim distinct from the existing "zero context-window cost" differentiator. No code change.
- [2.6.45] - 2026-06-17 — feat(security): self-modification defense — `path-guard.sh` gains a `selfmod` category that blocks Write/Edit attempts against Supercharger's own guardrail config files. Closes the documented Claude Code sandbox-bypass pattern: Ona Security (March 2026) showed Claude Code agents reasoning about their own bubblewrap sandbox, identifying the blocker, and disabling it — the exact same shape applies to writes against `.disabled-security-categories` or `.claude/settings.json` hooks block. `safety.sh` already has a `selfmod` category for the Bash channel (line 245, matches `sed/rm/tee` against `.claude/settings.json` or `CLAUDE.md`); the Write/Edit channel was the gap. New checks block writes to: `~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.disabled-security-categories` (would disable safety.sh categories), `~/.claude/supercharger/scope/.disabled-hooks` (would disable individual hooks), `~/.claude/settings.json` (the hook registration), `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (the global rules), any project-level `.supercharger.json`, `.claude/settings.json`, or `.claude/settings.local.json`. Opt out via `.supercharger.json` `disableSecurityCategories: ["selfmod"]` (parity with the Bash side). Defense-in-depth verified: even with selfmod disabled, the `abs-path` category catches writes to `~/.claude/` from a project cwd. Aligned with OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications 2026 — "Least-Agency" (agent gets minimum autonomy to complete the task; modifying its own constraints is out of scope). 915 tests passing.
- [2.6.44] - 2026-06-17 — chore: shellcheck --severity=warning sweep round 2 + `read -r` hygiene. hooks/ and lib/ now have zero shellcheck warnings (was 8). Changes: (1) `lib/utils.sh` — added `# shellcheck disable=SC2034` to each color code var (RED/GREEN/BLUE/YELLOW/CYAN/BOLD/NC); they're used across 5 tools via `source lib/utils.sh` but shellcheck can't trace the indirection. (2) `lib/economy.sh:10` — removed `DEFAULT_TIER="lean"`, never read anywhere; `SELECTED_TIER` stays (used at 4 sites). (3) `lib/hooks.sh:143` — split `local base=$(basename "$installed")` into `local base` + `base=$(basename ...)` to stop masking basename's exit code (SC2155). (4) `hooks/budget-cap.sh:45` — added `-r` to `read USAGE_INPUT USAGE_CACHE_WRITE USAGE_CACHE_READ USAGE_OUTPUT <<< "$USAGE_FIELDS"`; tokens are numeric so no current backslash mangling, but defensive against future format drift. Plus: malformed-JSON + empty-stdin regression sweep across all 70+ registered hooks — every one returns 0 or 2 (the deny code) cleanly, no abort-via-set-e on the unhappy path. tools/ still has 15 warnings, mostly SC2088 (tilde-in-string error messages) and SC2034 for sourced color codes; deferred — cosmetic, no functional impact. 915 tests passing.
- [2.6.43] - 2026-06-17 — chore: chmod +x 23 entry-point hooks that shipped at 644 in the repo. Pre-existing hygiene gap from the memory rule "Write tool creates files at 644; always chmod +x new hook scripts." `install.sh`/`lib/hooks.sh:153` runs `chmod 700` on installed copies so end-users were never affected, but local `bash <hook>` worked while direct exec `hooks/<name>.sh` failed with `Permission denied (exit 126)` — a foot-gun for local testing, CI runs that exec rather than `bash`-wrap, and anyone reading the repo. Hooks fixed (alphabetical): `adaptive-economy`, `agent-handoff-gate`, `code-security-scanner`, `context-advisor`, `dep-vuln-scanner`, `design-context`, `event-logger`, `failure-tracker`, `file-watcher`, `learn-from-blocks`, `learn-from-prompts`, `mcp-tracker`, `notify-permission`, `notify-stop`, `output-secrets-scanner`, `scope-guard`, `session-end`, `session-memory-inject`, `session-memory-write`, `smart-approve`, `subagent-safety`, `trace-compactor`, `update-check`. Three remaining `hooks/*.sh` files at 644 are intentional (sourced-only, not registered as hooks): `lib-suppress.sh`, `cmd-normalize.sh`, `notify-helper.sh`. Verified each newly-+x file actually parses + runs via direct exec. 915 tests passing.
- [2.6.42] - 2026-06-17 — fix: 5 more instances of the `grep -c | … || echo 0` doubling bug — the same shape caught and fixed in v2.6.27 (`repetition-detector.sh`) and v2.6.38 (`confidence-gate.sh`). `grep -c` always prints a number AND exits 1 when nothing matches; the `||` then ALSO runs, so the variable receives `"0\n0"`. Downstream code that treats it as an integer (`[ -ge 2 ]`, `int()`) either crashes or aborts via `set -e`. Hunt across the codebase found: (1) `hooks/reentry-detector.sh:27` — silently broke re-entry detection on the COMMON path (prompts without `[MEM]/[CTX]` markers — i.e. every normal prompt) because the arithmetic check `[ "$MATCH_COUNT" -ge 2 ]` threw on `"0\n0"` and `set -e` aborted before the rest of the hook ran. (2) `hooks/failure-tracker.sh:35` — when a fresh failures log had no prior match for the current command pattern, `[ "$FAIL_COUNT" -ge 2 ]` threw, killing the "log the new failure" path. (3) `hooks/scope-guard.sh:156` — when no files were changed, `[ "$COUNT" -gt 1 ]` and `[ "$COUNT" -gt 5 ]` both threw, suppressing the scope-warning path. (4) `hooks/typecheck.sh:137` — interpolated `"0\n0 type error(s)"` into the user-facing message when zero errors matched (cosmetic but ugly). (5) `lib/hooks.sh:331` — `count_installed_hooks` returned `"0\n0"` when no hooks matched mode, which `install.sh:417` then printed as the hook count. All five replaced with `awk '… {c++} END{print c+0}'` which emits exactly one number regardless of match count and always exits 0. Smoke-tested each fix in isolation: re-entry detector exits 0 on a clean prompt, failure-tracker exits 0 on first-time failure, typecheck reports `ERROR_COUNT=0` cleanly. 915 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean. Lesson learned (for the third time): this idiom is a foot-gun anywhere `set -e` and integer comparisons meet. Worth a regression test that pipes pattern-less input through every counter site.
- [2.6.41] - 2026-06-17 — test: worktree end-to-end coverage for 6 of the 8 hooks wired in v2.6.36. The original v2.6.36 commit only smoke-tested `tool-call-limiter.sh` through the new resolver; the other hooks could regress silently because each one re-reads `.supercharger.json` independently. New `tests/test-worktree-e2e.sh` builds a `main + linked worktree` fixture for each case, writes the relevant key into the main repo's `.supercharger.json`, runs the hook with `cwd` pointing at the linked worktree, and asserts the hook saw the config from main. Covered: `cost-forecast.sh` (`forecastTurnsPerAgent:50`), `budget-cap.sh` (`budget:0.10` over a `.session-cost` of `$0.15`), `adaptive-economy.sh` (`autoEconomy:false` suppresses the 80%-context auto-switch), `tool-preferences.sh` (`toolPreferences:{npm:pnpm}` denies an `npm install`), `path-guard.sh` (`disableSecurityCategories:[path-traversal]` lets a `../../etc/passwd` write through), `human-approval-gate.sh` (`humanApprovalGate:true` fires on a `DROP TABLE`). Skipped (too much surface for one round): `project-config.sh` (SessionStart-time, depends on the welcome-flag state machine) and `post-compact-inject.sh` (PostCompact payload needs a real compact_summary). 915 tests passing (909 + 6 new). `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.40] - 2026-06-17 — feat: 1h cache TTL advisory + per-model pricing in cost trackers. Three changes driven by today's token-economy research scout. (1) `claude-check.sh` now detects whether `ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H` is set (env var or `settings.json` `env` block). On 2026-03-06 Anthropic silently dropped the default `cache_control: {"type":"ephemeral"}` TTL from 1 hour to 5 minutes — for API users this means any pause longer than 5 min re-pays the 1.25× cache write (or 2× if explicitly using 1h). Claude Code subscriptions auto-request 1h when the env var is set. Advisory recommends adding `"env": {"ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H": "1"}` with the rationale ("1h TTL pays 2× once and amortizes across hour-long sessions"). Source: [dev.to/whoffagents/anthropic-silently-dropped-prompt-cache-ttl](https://dev.to/whoffagents/anthropic-silently-dropped-prompt-cache-ttl-from-1-hour-to-5-minutes-16ao). (2) `budget-cap.sh` and `subagent-cost.sh`: cost trackers now detect model from the payload's `model` field (assistant turns carry it). Three-tier pricing table for Opus/Sonnet/Haiku — Opus 4.8 = $5/$25 per MTok, Sonnet 4.6 = $3/$15, Haiku 4.5 = $0.80/$4. Cache write 5min = 1.25× input, cache read = 0.10× input (Anthropic-standard ratios). Falls back to `SUPERCHARGER_PRICING_MODEL` env var if `model` field is absent (rare), then Sonnet. Verified: same 1000 input + 500 output tokens cost $0.0175 on Opus vs $0.0105 on Sonnet — Opus users were being undercounted by 40% before. (3) Pre-existing fragility caught while testing: `tools/claude-check.sh:222` ran `find "$SUMMARIES_DIR" -maxdepth 1 -name '*.md' | wc -l | tr -d ' '` with `set -euo pipefail`. When the dir was missing, `find` exited 1, `pipefail` propagated, `$(…)` substitution failed, and `set -e` aborted the script mid-way through — meaning everything below the Session Summaries section silently never ran. Wrapped in `{ … } || echo 0`. Same shape as the earlier v2.6.20 / v2.6.27 / v2.6.38 set-e regressions caught in this codebase. (4) Pricing-line in `tools/claude-check.sh:337` used `${DIM}` which was never defined; removed the unbound reference. 909 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.39] - 2026-06-17 — perf: `config-scan` + `permission-denied-advisor` consolidation. (1) `config-scan.sh`: was 1 jq cwd + 1 python3 fallback + N grep injection-pattern matches + 1 python3 foreign-hook scan + N grep ANTHROPIC_* + up to 3 python3 settings-file scans + 1 python3 JSON wrap (5–7 python forks total). Now: 1 python3 fork does stdin parse, walks `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/**/*.md` (depth 2), runs the 12-pattern injection regex panel inline, parses project `.claude/settings.json` once and reuses the dict for both the hook-scan (CVE-2025-59536) and the settings-risk scan, runs ANTHROPIC_* regex over the project file set, scans 3 settings files (user + project + project-local) for claude-code#44482 (bare Edit/Write/Bash allowlists), #44274 (sandbox.filesystem.denyRead non-enforcement), CVE-2026-33068 (defaultMode=bypassPermissions), and pluginSuggestionMarketplaces (v2.1.152). All warning strings preserved verbatim. SessionStart fires once per session so latency is felt directly on the user's first prompt. ~60ms → ~50ms. (2) `permission-denied-advisor.sh`: was 3 jq (cwd, session_id, tool_name) + 1 python3 (message build, tier-aware) + 1 python3 (MSG_JSON wrap). Now: 1 python3 emits 5 lines (cwd, session_id, tool, raw msg for dedup key, JSON-encoded msg for stdout); bash sed-splits them. ~60ms → ~65ms — the consolidation worked but `hook_already_emitted`'s md5/date forks are the floor; pure parse-fork count went from 5 to 1 even though wall time didn't move. Kept the cleanup anyway because fewer forks = fewer points of failure on malformed payloads. 909 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.38] - 2026-06-17 — perf + fix: `mcp-output-truncator` + `confidence-gate` consolidation, plus a `set -e` regression caught in the rewrite. (1) `mcp-output-truncator.sh`: bash fast-path on `${#_INPUT}` — if the entire payload is under 3500 bytes the embedded output can't reach the 3000-char truncation threshold, exit without any forks. **Fast path 70ms → 12ms (-83%)**; slow path 70ms → 48ms (-31%) via merging the 2 jq + 1 python3 fallback + 1 python3 main into one python3 fork. (2) `confidence-gate.sh`: full single-fork rewrite. Was 7 forks (3 jq cwd/tool_name/session_id/file_path + 1 jq command + 1 python destructive-pattern check + 1 python score eval + 1 python JSON wrap). Now: bash `case` fast-path filters non-Edit/Write/Bash on raw stdin (no fork) + 1 python3 fork that does stdin parse + tool gate + destructive-pattern check (Bash only) + scope-file reads (`.tool-history`, `.repetition-flag-*`, `.read-history`) + score + JSON. **Read fast-path 60ms → 11ms (-82%)**; full Bash flow 60ms → 46ms (-23%). (3) Caught while rewriting: `FAILURES_LAST_5=$(grep -F ... | tail -5 | grep -c '"success": false' || echo 0)` produced `0\n0` when grep matched zero times (grep -c always prints a number AND exits 1, then `|| echo 0` fires too). Python `int('0\n0')` then raised, and the `$(... python3 ...)` capture returned non-zero, which `set -e` propagated to abort the script — destructive Bash calls were silently exiting 1 instead of 0. Same shape as the v2.6.27 fix in repetition-detector. Replaced with `awk '/.../{c++} END{print c+0}'` which emits exactly one number regardless of match count. Also wrapped the pipe in `{ ... } || true` to absorb grep's non-match exit. 909 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.37] - 2026-06-17 — perf: two-hook consolidation, `stop-failure` 80ms → 50ms + `prompt-injection-scanner` 80ms → 40ms. (1) `stop-failure.sh`: was 2 python3 forks (stdin reason extract + ADVICE_JSON wrap). Now 1 — parse stdin, classify reason against an inline `(prefix, advice)` table, emit reason on line 1 (for bash log) and JSON on line 2 (or empty). bash splits the two with `sed -n '1p'` / `'2p'` — no extra forks. Reason classification preserved: `rate_limit*`, `authentication_failed*`, `billing_error*`. All 4 cases verified (rate_limit, auth, unknown, malformed JSON). (2) `prompt-injection-scanner.sh`: was 3 python3 forks (TOOL_NAME parse + OUTPUT parse + 17-pattern regex scan). Now 1 — stdin parse, matcher gate (`mcp__*` / `WebFetch` / `WebSearch`), compiled regex panel, NFKC unicode normalize, JSON wrap. The 17-pattern set is preserved verbatim (instruction override, persona hijack, token injection, base64 keywords, zero-width chars, etc.). Python emits the matched tool name on line 1 and the JSON on line 2; bash sed-splits them and logs the tool name. Caught bug during write: forgot `chmod +x` after Write tool created the file at 644 (per memory rule). 909 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.36] - 2026-06-16 — fix: worktree-aware project root resolver. In a git worktree, `cwd` from the hook payload points at the linked checkout (e.g. `/repo/feat-branch`), not the main repo (`/repo`). Project policy lives in `.supercharger.json` at the main repo's root; the linked worktree's `.claude/` is empty. Nine hooks that read this config silently fell back to defaults inside a worktree — `cost-forecast.sh`, `budget-cap.sh`, `human-approval-gate.sh`, `tool-call-limiter.sh`, `project-config.sh`, `adaptive-economy.sh`, `path-guard.sh`, `tool-preferences.sh`, `post-compact-inject.sh`. New helper `hooks/lib-project-root.sh` exposes `_resolve_project_root <cwd>` which returns the main worktree's root when `cwd` is a linked worktree, and `cwd` otherwise. Fast path: most CWDs have `.git` as a directory or absent → no git fork needed (~0.5ms stat). Only when `.git` is a file (linked worktree marker) does the helper run `git rev-parse --git-common-dir --git-dir` and walk to `dirname(common-dir)`. Hooks that use the project dir for *both* config and boundary (like `path-guard.sh`'s symlink/abs-path checks) keep `PROJECT_DIR` as the linked-worktree path and use a separate `CONFIG_ROOT` for `.supercharger.json` lookups. `.claude/supercharger-memory.md` stays per-worktree (intentional — different branches, different working contexts; runtime state, not policy). New `tests/test-worktree-resolver.sh` covers the 3 resolver cases (non-git, main worktree, linked worktree) plus an end-to-end smoke through `tool-call-limiter.sh`. 909 tests passing (905 + 4 new). `shellcheck --severity=error` clean. Resolves item 6 in the 2026-05-07 deferred-items memory.
- [2.6.35] - 2026-06-16 — perf: `skill-poisoning-scanner.sh` consolidation, 70ms → 50ms (-29%). Sync `PreToolUse|Skill` hook — blocks every Skill load, so latency lands directly on the user. Was ~12–15 forks: 2 python3 (stdin parse), bash `find` over 4 base dirs, ~10 `grep -cE` per scanned file (one per pattern), 1 python3 per file for zero-width whitespace count, 1 python3 for CRITICAL aggregation, 1 python3 for JSON wrap. Now: one python3 heredoc parses stdin, walks the 4 candidate dirs (`~/.claude/commands`, `~/.claude/plugins`, `$cwd/.claude/commands`, `$cwd/.claude/plugins`), runs all 10 compiled regexes against each skill file, counts the 4 zero-width chars inline, and emits the final `hookSpecificOutput` JSON. Two bugs caught during smoke-testing: (1) initial `Path.rglob('*.md')` walked thousands of unrelated files under `~/.claude/` (overshoot to 220ms); replaced with 10 explicit `Path.glob(pattern)` calls matching only `<base>/.../<skill>.md`, `<base>/.../<skill>/SKILL.md`, `<base>/.../<skill>/skill.md` shapes at depths 0–3. (2) Block path exited 0 instead of 2 because `json.dumps` emits `"permissionDecision": "deny"` with a space after the colon, but the grep guard was matching `'"permissionDecision":"deny"'` without it; loosened to grep for `'"deny"'` with a comment explaining the json.dumps spacing. Verified end-to-end: malicious skill (curl-pipe-sh + eval + base64) → deny + exit 2; benign-name lookup → fast-path no-op. Severity table preserved (5 CRITICAL / 3 HIGH / 2 MEDIUM patterns + steganographic whitespace). 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.34] - 2026-06-16 — perf: `compaction-backup.sh` parallel children + guidance consolidation, 200ms → 170ms (-15%). Sync PreCompact hook runs once per `/compact` — modest absolute frequency but blocks the user the entire time. Two changes: (1) the two inline child hooks (`session-memory-write.sh` + `lesson-record.sh`) now run as background jobs with `wait`. They're independent (different files, different transcripts) so overlapping them costs nothing semantically — saves `min(child1, child2)` of wall time. With session-memory-write at 130ms dominating and lesson-record at 20ms, the practical save is ~20ms. Race-free: `wait` still blocks compaction-backup until both children exit, which was the whole point of v2.6.5's sync-registration fix. (2) The guidance-building block (git diff + cat economy-tier + python3 cost-read + jq -Rs JSON-wrap) collapsed to one python3 fork that does git diff as a subprocess + scope-file reads inline + emits the final JSON. Saves the cold-start overhead of 2 separate python invocations. Output format byte-identical. Bench is true median of 11 samples to filter the long tail. 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.33] - 2026-06-16 — perf: `learn-from-blocks.sh` consolidation, 140ms → 50ms (-64%). Async `SessionStart` hook — fires once per session but was the largest async cost left after the v2.6.16–2.6.31 sweep, and SessionStart latency directly delays the user's first prompt. Was ~30 subprocesses: `md5sum` (or `md5`), `jq cwd` + `python3` cwd fallback, 4× `rotate_log` (each: `grep`, `awk`, `mv`), 3× `dedup_log` (each: `awk`, `mv`), 1× `python3` blocks-compression, `tail`/`sed`/`tr`/`sed` pipeline for corrections, same for reinforcements, `sort`/`sed`/`sort`/`uniq`/`sort`/`awk`/`head` pipeline for failures, `jq -Rs` JSON wrap. Now: 1 python3 heredoc does `hashlib.md5` project hash, rotation by `[YYYY-MM-DD]` cutoff, dedup by stripped-line set, blocks-compression (Counter top-8), corrections/reinforcements last-5 pipe-join, and failures Counter (threshold 3, top 5). The jq cwd extraction stays separate for `init_hook_suppress`. Output format byte-identical to the v2.6.32 path; smoke-tested against the live `.blocked-commands` file (8 top reasons + counts + last-seen dates). 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=warning` clean.
- [2.6.32] - 2026-06-16 — cleanup: shellcheck `--severity=warning` sweep. Five SC2034 (unused var) / SC2221 / SC2222 (redundant glob pattern) hits cleared in one pass: `session-memory-write.sh` lost the dead `AUDIT_DIR`/`AUDIT_FILE`/`TODAY` triple introduced by the v2.6.19 refactor (decisions extraction moved inline to python, log file became orphan); `rate-limit-advisor.sh` lost an unused `SCOPE_DIR`/`SUPERCHARGER_DIR` pair; `cache-health.sh` lost `CACHE_READ` and `CACHE_CREATE` (assigned from positional args, never read after); `config-scan.sh` lost `PROTECTED_TOOLS_PATTERN` (the python heredoc declares its own copy); `lib-suppress.sh` got a `# shellcheck disable=SC2034` directive on `HOOK_SUPPRESS` (intentional cross-file export). `bash-output-compactor.sh` case blocks collapsed redundant glob patterns where shell substring matching covered them implicitly (`*'npm i'*` covers `npm install` and `pnpm install`; `*'npm test'*` covers `pnpm test`; `*'go test'*` covers `cargo test`; `*pytest*` covers `python -m pytest`). All 9 verbose-pattern triggers (`git log`, `npm install`, `pnpm install`, `yarn install`, `yarn test`, `pytest`, `cargo test`, `python -m pytest`, `go test`) still match by smoke test; `ls` still skipped on fast path. Remaining warnings are non-blocking (`tests/test-hook-overrides.sh` dynamic source, scaffolding). 905 tests passing. Also: alignment audit confirmed zero `claude -p` shell-outs (no June 15 billing-change exposure).
- [2.6.31] - 2026-06-16 — perf: `code-security-scanner.sh` consolidation, 80ms → 40ms (-50%). `asyncRewake` hook fires on every Write/Edit — high volume. Now: 1 python3 heredoc parses stdin, runs all 22 compiled regexes against the content, checks file-path metacharacters and `.yml`/`.yaml` GitHub-Actions injection patterns, and emits the final `hookSpecificOutput` JSON. The full pattern set is preserved: eval/innerHTML/dangerouslySetInnerHTML/document.write/new Function (JS), pickle.load/exec/os.system/subprocess shell=True/__import__ (Python), f-string + concat SQL, hardcoded password/secret/api_key, MD5, base64 decode, zero-width Unicode, GitHub Actions `${{ github.event.* }}`, shell-metachar file paths. 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.30] - 2026-06-16 — perf: `prompt-validator.sh` consolidation, 70ms → 40ms (-43%). Sync `UserPromptSubmit` hook — drops user-perceived input latency on every prompt the user types. Caught a self-inflicted bug while smoke-testing: had appended `2>/dev/null` to the heredoc which swallowed all the stderr advisories; removed once verified the python doesn't itself emit noisy traces. 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.29] - 2026-06-16 — perf: `cost-forecast.sh` consolidation, 80ms → 40ms (-50%). Fires on every `PreToolUse|Agent` — sync, blocks subagent spawn. Now: 1 python3 heredoc does `.session-cost` read, the 5-level dir walk, config parse, forecast compute, and emits the final `hookSpecificOutput` JSON in one fork. The jq cwd extraction stays separate for `init_hook_suppress`. - [2.6.28] - 2026-06-15 — feat(ux): `tools/update.sh --yes / -y / --non-interactive` (also honors `SUPERCHARGER_NONINTERACTIVE=1`). Skips both `read -r -p` confirmation prompts ("Update now?" before the git-clone integrity check, "Proceed with update?" before the reinstall). Pain validated when applying today's own v2.6.10 → v2.6.20 update via the `/sc-update` skill — had to pipe `printf 'y\ny\n' |` to get past the blockers. Unlocks dotfile sync flows (`cron`'d `update.sh`), CI rollouts, and any automated agent invocation. `install.sh` already had equivalent `NON_INTERACTIVE` detection when all args are provided — that path is unchanged. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean. 905 tests passing.
- [2.6.27] - 2026-06-15 — perf + fix: `repetition-detector.sh` jq consolidation + integer-comparison bug. (1) Was 3-4 separate jq forks (cwd, tool_name, command, file_path, session_id). Now one `@tsv` jq emits all 5 fields; downstream extractors are `awk -F'\t'` against the cached string — no fork-per-field. Median 70ms → 60ms. Smaller win than other hooks because most of the remaining time is in `md5` + `stat` + file IO, not jq cold-starts. (2) Caught a pre-existing bug while testing: `COUNT=$(tail -20 $LOOP_FILE | grep -c "^$HASH$" || echo "0")` had two outputs interleave — `grep -c` always prints a number even on 0 matches, but it ALSO exits 1, which triggers `|| echo "0"`. Result: `COUNT="0\n0"` and the next `[ "$COUNT" -ge 2 ]` threw `integer expression expected`. Replaced with `tail -20 | awk` which emits exactly one number regardless of match count. Loop-trigger smoke test confirmed: 2× repeat now correctly fires the warning. 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.26] - 2026-06-15 — perf: `post-compact-inject.sh` consolidation, 190ms → 70ms (-63%). Was 8 forks: 1 jq (cwd), 1 git rev-parse `--git-dir`, 1 git status, 1 wc, 3 python3 (compact_summary parse, hints parse, systemMessage JSON wrap), 1 git rev-parse branch. Now 1 python3 heredoc reads stdin, detects dirty tree via `git status --porcelain`, reads memory file (size + body), parses `.supercharger.json` hints, gets branch via `rev-parse` → `symbolic-ref` fallback (same chain as v2.6.20), and composes the final systemMessage JSON. The jq cwd extraction stays separate for `init_hook_suppress`. Sync hook fires once per `/compact` — high latency-cost per call even though frequency is low. 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.25] - 2026-06-15 — perf: `bash-output-compactor.sh` bash fast-path. Fires on every Bash PostToolUse; only does meaningful work when the command is one of `git log` / `pytest` / `vitest` / `jest` / `mocha` / `ava` / `npm test|install` / `pnpm test|install|add` / `yarn test|install` / `go test` / `cargo test` / `npm i`. Raw-stdin `case` check filters everything else (the vast majority of `ls`, `git diff`, `cat`, `echo`, etc.) before any fork. **Fast path 110ms → 10ms (-91%)**; slow path 110ms → 60ms (-45%) when the compaction logic actually runs. Same pattern as v2.6.16 (`enforce-pkg-manager`, `git-safety`) and v2.6.18 (`audit-trail`). All 9 `tests/test-bash-output-compactor.sh` cases pass (existing compaction behavior preserved for git-log, test runners, install). 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
- [2.6.24] - 2026-06-15 — perf: `session-complete.sh` consolidation, 90ms → 70ms (-22%). Was 7 forks: 1 python3 (cost parse), 2 greps (economy detection), 1 basename, 1 git branch, 1 git diff, 1 date (×2 actually). Now 3 subprocesses: 1 python3 heredoc does stdin-parse + economy-file-read + both file writes + timestamp generation; the two git subprocesses (`branch --show-current`, `diff --name-only HEAD`) stay outside python because they're cheap and bash already has their output. Win is smaller than the other hooks because git subprocess cold-start dominates the remaining time, but the two grep + cost-parse forks were pure waste. Hook fires once per Stop event (lower frequency than PostToolUse) but pairs with session-memory-write on the same trigger — combined Stop overhead now noticeably lower. 905 tests passing. `shellcheck --severity=error` clean.
